Outside it: §6.3 `parseDeviceHR` reads the **last** column of a real PSL `_HR.txt`, which is **HRV in ms**, so 52 % of a surfaced validation card's "bpm" values are milliseconds (70 % of rows dropped); §4.2 MotionDex measures a confident breathing rate **across epochs where the strap was not recording** (linear interpolation + power normalisation discards the only cue that says "no signal"); §4.1 `sampleHz` = count ÷ span reads **18.7 Hz on a 52 Hz** real file; §4.3 no plausibility bound admits **1.6e38 mg** samples while "Immobile time" reads 20 % on a 96 %-immobile night; §6.1 ECGDex counts an **abstaining** ACC vote as agreement; §5.1 PulseDex's *surfaced* Total Power still breaks the Task-Force identity (the 07-14 fix landed in the DSP only, never the app that renders it); §1.1 `resolveDMY` computes `contradictory` and **all six callers discard it** (one anomalous row moves a night six months); §1.4 the Clock-Contract lint prints "clean across 70 files" while **44 shipped files are outside its scope** — a gate failing OPEN by omission. **§7 records the 2 refuted claims and the real adjacent defect each was hiding** (`_o2DateAnchorMs` fabricates a date from an out-of-range 14-digit filename: month 13/day 32 → a night dated 2027-02-01); **§8 names every uncovered area** (the browser lane entirely, the Integrator's TCH/noisy-OR/Poisson machinery, the DFA/SampEn/PRSA differential). Spine edits re-bundle all 8 apps + all 8 provenance fragments (serialize); **every item moves `computeHash`, so corpus re-verification is owed before release**. **CLOSED 2026-07-29:** the four unclosed punch-list items (§3.6, §4.1, §4.2, §4.3) turned out to have been **fixed on 2026-07-27 and never re-stamped** — but two were **ungated**: §3.6 had no gate at all, and §4.1's was pointed one function away from the defect (it drives `respiratoryRate`, which never calls `sampleHz`). Reverting either reddened **zero** assertions. Both now gated and verified RED; residue in `DEEP-AUDIT-III-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-29)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md) | **The coverage contract exists. Nothing emits it — and the capture nodes are the sparse ones.** Drafted arguing the Integrator needed a coverage model; `986d17e` (DEEP-AUDIT-III §6.2) **shipped one** mid-draft, so this brief no longer proposes it and explicitly builds on it. What survives is sharper. **§2** `integrator-dsp.js` records the assumption *"null for every node that records continuously, which is all of them today"* — **false for the three capture nodes**: a BLE drop opens a NEW segment, and one real night spans **24–47 segments per stream** (2026-07-26: 24 Verity PPG, 7 O2Ring, 3 H10), while `ecgdex-dsp.js`/`ppgdex-dsp.js` contain **zero** occurrences of `coverage:`. The block built for HRVDex's 29-day spot measurements is not emitted by the nodes whose recordings actually have holes. **§3** `segmentsOverlap` is consulted in exactly ONE place — `_mayOverlap`, a *boolean* — while every published **quantity** still comes from the envelope: `recWindow` returns a single `[t0Ms,endMs]`, `totHrs` sums merged envelope intervals → `apnea.overlapHours` → **`confirmedAHI = nConf / totHrs`** and the Poisson chance expectation. So even a node that DID declare sparse coverage would still be divided by envelope hours. **§4 measured:** on **2026-07-23** `trio-batch` computes the gap-aware three-way concurrency as **2.1 h** against the Integrator's `overlapHours` **6.86 h** — **3.3×** — and 07-23 is the **only** night of eleven marked `reportable` (`confirmedAHI 0.29`; on 2.1 h the same 2 events are 0.95/h). Not conservative either way: an inflated denominator understates the index while the same coverage raises the chance expectation, so the two do not cancel and the net sign is night-dependent. **§5 why no gate saw it:** GATE B is static (a wrong denominator reproduces perfectly), the equiv fixtures are single-recording and gapless, the synthetic fusion tests *encode* the assumption, and §6.2's own tests exercise the SPARSE-spot shape, not the FRAGMENTED-continuous one. **EXECUTED 2026-07-28 (part 2 — the emitters).** ECGDex/PpgDex/OxyDex now declare `recording.coverage`, each derived from evidence it was already computing and discarding (ECGDex's dropouts, PpgDex's `relSec` jumps, OxyDex's row stamps), assembled through one shared `DexExport.coverageFromSegments`. The block is **ABSENT, not null**, on a contiguous recording — no hole, no claim — so the fleet re-bundle moved **zero** export bytes. The shape that actually costs a number turned out to be the **MERGED** one: every capture file is internally clean (`gaps = 0` on all 6 ECG + 107 PPG files of 07-23) and the fragmentation lives BETWEEN files, so `tools/trio-batch.mjs`'s session merge is the path that matters — measured there, **6.71 h recorded inside a 23.02 h envelope**. `apnea.overlapCoverage` publishes `{basis, recordedHours, envelopeHours, recordedFrac, segments, declaredBy}` so the denominator is auditable and attributable. Adversarial committed twin `synthetic_ecgdex_h10_gapped.txt` (3 segments in a 60 s envelope) runs as a full equiv/GATE-C leg in CI with no corpus; suppressing the emitter reds 10 assertions including that leg. §7 states what is NOT claimed: the 07-23 finding is not false — on the honest denominator the index is *higher*. Residue → `INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md`. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-28)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md) | **What emitting `recording.coverage` turned up.** §1.1 the holes were never INSIDE a file — every capture-host session parses with `gaps = 0`; a night is fragmented because it has 6 ECG and 107 PPG *files*, so the per-file emitter alone would have been **completely inert on the corpus it was written for** and only `trio-batch`'s session merge makes it bite (a fixture that cannot express the failure proves nothing — and neither can a code path production never takes). §1.2 a sample INDEX is not a clock position: after one dropout `idx/fs` is wrong by the accumulated silence, quietly — right count, right total, drifted POSITIONS, which is what an intersection is made of. Open: §2.1 `mergeEcg` and `parseECGText` disagree by one sample about what `gaps[].idx` means; §2.2 only ECGDex has a fragmented committed twin (PpgDex/OxyDex are gated by hand-built test inputs); §2.3 `apnea.overlapCoverage` is published but rendered nowhere; §2.4 HRVDex still hand-rolls its coverage literal beside the shared helper. §3 declines a committed three-node fusion golden (it would pin the Integrator's entire output tree for a question about the denominator) and back-filling the `historical:true` fusions. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md`](briefs/NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md) | **`recording` publishes BOTH a data duration and a clock end — one scalar cannot answer two questions** (owner-ratified 2026-07-27, option (c)). The three trio nodes disagreed with nothing saying which was right: ECGDex's `durSec` is **data seconds** (`n/fs` → `nnRes.activeSec`), PpgDex's is `(n−1)/fs` over a **gap-filled grid** so it is effectively **wall span**, and OxyDex's `durationMin` is explicitly **span** — on 2026-07-17 that is **8.92 h vs 14.54 h vs 14.62 h for one night**. Both readings are defensible; having both under one name is not. **Measured cost** (2026-07-16…26 corpus): because ECGDex declared data seconds and nothing else, `t0 + durSec` lands short of the true end by exactly the dropout time, so **ECGDex's own events fell outside its own declared window on 11 of 11 nights, by +8 min to +326 min**. **The ruling:** `durSec` (how much signal) *and* `endEpochMs` (where it ends on the clock), neither replacing the other. Two non-negotiables — `endEpochMs` is **READ, never reconstructed** from `durSec`+gaps (that is a guess; Clock Contract §2.6 says null, never fabricated) — and **absent ⇒ today's behaviour**, since `integrator-dsp normalizeFile` already prefers `endEpochMs` over every duration key, so no derived fallback was added for the gapless synthetic path where `t0+durSec` is already exact. **ECGDex SHIPPED**: the parser reads the last row's Phone timestamp (round-trips `2026-06-17T01:12:19.371` → `1781658739371` exactly), `analyze` carries it as a RECORDING property like `offsetMin`, both goldens regenerated, gated by a dropout stub where data and wall span provably disagree (3.08 s vs 5.07 s). **The remaining 7 nodes now defer to `986d17e`'s `recording.coverage`** (DEEP-AUDIT-III §6.2) rather than a second convention: `spanSec`/`recordedSec` is this ruling in a richer shape, `endEpochMs` answers the envelope for a continuously-recording node and `coverage.segments` for one that is not — and `INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP` §2 shows the three capture nodes are in the second category and emit no coverage today. §5 prices the remainder honestly: ~15 emitters, every fixture's `outputHash` moves, all 8 regen tools, 11 bundles and a re-stamp of every `provenance/<App>.json` at once — a coordinated work-unit to schedule, not a drive-by. **PpgDex `endEpochMs` LANDED 2026-08-03 (§6) — and this brief's own §1 table was optimistic about PpgDex.** It lists PpgDex `durSec` as *"effectively wall span"* (gap-filled grid ⇒ `t0 + durSec` should hit the clock end, making the field redundant), which is why PpgDex was grouped with OxyDex as needing the coverage model rather than treated as additive. **Measured, the gap-fill does NOT recover all lost time:** `t0 + durSec` lands **6.6 min short** of the last stamp on the gappiest O2Ring night (`nGapSpanIntervals` 6317), **1.0 min** on the next, ~0 on contiguous nights — the shortfall scales with gap burden. Far smaller than ECGDex's +8…+326 min, same defect class, and exactly why §3 says the field is READ not computed. Gated by gapped/gapless synthetic twins differing only by a 20 s dropout — the stamp is hit to the millisecond on both, `t0 + durSec` lands ~20 s short on the gapped one, and the gapless twin has no shortfall (so the GAP separates them, not the fixture shape). Mutation-verified four ways; the one that matters is **"simplify `endEpochMs` to `t0 + durSec`"**, the refactor the gate exists to refuse. **One assertion is deliberately ABSENT:** §2.6's honest-null is UNREACHABLE through `parsePPG` (a PPG export whose stamps do not parse is rejected wholesale — the layout detector needs the stamp column), so asserting it would assert a branch no input can reach; the `?? null` guard stays, ECGDex's sibling gate does pin the null because its parser accepts stamp-less rows. §P1's perf win preserved — the backward scan is memoised, ≤40 rows, only where read. `computeHash 7b7a072ac320 → e38a8746cead`; all four goldens REGENERATED (one field each), real corpus fixture re-stamped after a green run. `bump: minor`. **6 nodes remain:** OxyDex · MotionDex · PulseDex · HRVDex · GlucoDex · CPAPDex. **§7 AUDITED 2026-08-03 — the remaining six do NOT have this defect, and the §5 item over-scoped the work.** It reads "endEpochMs on the remaining 7 nodes (additive)", treating them as uniform. Reading each derivation and measuring it: **only the two nodes that declare DATA seconds had the defect, and both are now fixed** (ECGDex `n/fs`, short by every dropout; PpgDex gap-filled grid, short up to 6.6 min). The other six already compute a SPAN — OxyDex `rows[n−1].tMs − rows[0].tMs`, MotionDex last `relSec`, PulseDex `times[N−1]`, GlucoDex `spanMs`, CPAPDex `max(session.t0Ms + durMin)`, HRVDex per-row — so `t0 + dur` lands on the clock end BY CONSTRUCTION, to within the field's own rounding. **Measured:** OxyDex's residual against the true last stamp is **max 3.0 s / median 2.0 s** over 37 nights (pure `.toFixed(1)` rounding), and the `_durBad` guard that would null `durationMin` while the last stamp is still known — the one case carrying real information — fires on **0 of 37**. **The cost is not free:** adding the field moves the export, reds the equiv gate, and forces regenerating every golden + re-bundling + three build systems + a `verifiedUnder` re-stamp. Right for ECGDex's +326 min and PpgDex's 6.6 min; a poor trade for 2–3 seconds — the same economics `CLAUDE.md` already applies to the inert `BADGE_CSS` export and the deferred version stamp. **§7.3 recommends** it ride each node's next BEHAVIOURAL re-bundle; the Integrator needs no change either way (`normalizeFile` already prefers `endEpochMs` and falls through). **Prioritise MotionDex only** if its `rows.length / 26` fallback is shown to fire on real captures — that is the sole remaining path publishing a FABRICATED duration rather than a measured one. **§8 CLOSED 2026-08-06 — both remaining items were PREMISES, and both were wrong, in opposite directions.** **PpgDex:** the brief asked for a rename because its `durSec` was "a gap-filled-grid span". Measured over **322 real PPG files** (90 with a coverage block), `durSec` tracks `coverage.recordedSec` — the **DATA** — to **0.052 %** of span at the median (p95 0.227 %) against **0.425 %** (p95 2.496 %) for `spanSec`, and is nearest the data on **75 of 90**. §6.1 had already measured the evidence (`t0 + durSec` short by 6.6 min on the gappiest night) and read it as incomplete gap-fill — but **a duration short by exactly the dropout IS data-seconds**, ECGDex's signature, not OxyDex's. So PpgDex **already satisfies option (c)** in the ruling's own vocabulary and **the proposed rename would have given the one node with both fields right two envelopes and no data.** **MotionDex:** §7.3's condition is **FALSE** — over **616 real ACC files / 121,429,712 rows / 690 h** (both corpus trees) the `rows.length / 26` fallback fires **0 times**; no parsed row anywhere lacks both a Phone timestamp and a device counter, so the field is not owed. **But the same run shows 26 is the wrong constant:** delivered ACC rate spans **20.9–202.7 Hz** (H10 median 50.7, Verity 51.7), so if ever reached it misstates duration by **0.8×–7.8×** — **462 s for a 60 s record**, beside a `startEpochMs` of **null** (Clock Contract §2.6 one layer up). **Routed, not patched:** it is a compute-path change on an unreachable branch whose `durSec` feeds three windowing denominators (`bodyPosition`/`actigraphy`/`respiratoryEffort`). **Both gated, both verified RED:** `ppgdex-dsp · export · duration-semantics` (7 assertions; the mutation IS the literal rename the item proposed — reds 2 by value while the contiguous CONTROL stays green) and `motiondex-dsp · export · duration-semantics` (6 assertions on a 200 Hz fixture where measured and fabricated are 7.7× apart — forcing the fallback reds 3 by value). Test-only; no export moved, no bundle re-stamped. **§8.3 states what is NOT claimed** (the six nodes still lack `endEpochMs` on purpose; `durSec` is not *exactly* `recordedSec`; the fallback is unreachable **and** wrong). Spawns `NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-06-BRIEF.md`. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-06)* |
| [`NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-06-BRIEF.md`](briefs/NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-06-BRIEF.md) | **One live fabrication, one residual, and a habit — the residue of closing the duration ruling.** **§1** `motiondex-dsp.js durationOf` divides by an assumed **26 Hz** when a stream resolves no time. Measured: the branch fires **0× in 616 real ACC files**, *and* 26 is wrong by **0.8×–7.8×** for that same corpus (rate spans 20.9–202.7 Hz) — 462 s published for a 60 s record beside a null `startEpochMs`. Not fixed in the parent deliberately: it moves `computeHash` for a branch no input reaches, and `durSec` feeds three windowing denominators, so `0`/`null` is not a one-liner. Sketches the likely shape (scan backward for the last resolvable row first; only then refuse) with the guardrail **do not swap 26 for a better constant** — any assumed rate is wrong for some file. **§2** 43 of 322 PpgDex files where `t0 + durSec` **overruns** the last stamp by up to 24.9 s (**0.26 %**) — all contiguous Verity records, so an `fs`-estimate residual, not gap accounting; `endEpochMs` is unaffected by construction. Park unless the estimator is revisited. **§3** The six nodes still not publishing `endEpochMs` is a standing decision (parent §7.3), and the risk is **drift, not absence**: six nodes carrying a convention that lives only in a closed brief is how the original defect arose. Four of the six have no envelope-tracking assertion; the OxyDex/PpgDex gates are the template. **§4** The method note — the parent was wrong about its own nodes **four times, always the same way**: §6.1, §7, the OxyDex `[~]`, and §8's two premises were **all** corrected by driving the shipped code over real files and **none** by re-reading the source, which supported the wrong reading every time. The tell is a table row describing behaviour with no measurement beside it. | **§3 EXECUTED 2026-08-09 — the assertion it asked for FALSIFIED the parent's own table for two of four nodes.** The four ungated nodes gain a 17-assertion envelope-tracking gate on a synthetic gapped twin, mutation-verified in BOTH directions. CPAPDex (`durSec` 8400 s against 1200 s of data) and HRVDex (`recordedSec` **null**, never 0) pass and are now ratcheted. **PulseDex's untimed branch publishes DATA seconds as `durMin` (1800 s against a 2699 s span) and asserts `coverage: 100` on a stream it cannot place in time** — the ECGDex defect one branch away, plus a completeness claim made from absence of evidence. **GlucoDex's `recordedSec` is the SAME EXPRESSION as `spanSec`**, so a 6 h CGM dropout reports full coverage — under a comment claiming they "agree BY MEASUREMENT" and citing HRVDex, the node that refuses exactly that fabrication. Both pinned as characterization, both routed to FOLLOWUPS-II (compute-path edits owing a re-bundle). §2 parked on its own default; §4's method note earns its keep for the fifth and sixth time. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-09)* |
| [`NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`](briefs/NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md) | **Two nodes fabricate coverage, and both are already pinned in the gate that found them.** Owns the fixes FOLLOWUPS §3-RESULT measured but deliberately did not land in a tests-only PR. **§1 PulseDex** — `beatTimes` returns a wall span with timestamps and a cumulative RR sum without, so `durMin` means ENVELOPE on one branch and DATA on the other; and `coverage` initialises to **100**, overwritten only on the timestamped path, so an untimed stream claims completeness it cannot know. **§2 GlucoDex** — `recordedSec` and `spanSec` are assigned the same expression, so they cannot disagree and a 6 h dropout leaves no trace (one segment, `nWithDuration: 1`); the fix reuses the `gapMin`/`activeMin` GlucoDex already computes one function away, and must resolve the `kind: 'continuous'` claim too. **§3** — each is compute-path, so each owes its own re-bundle + golden regeneration + `verify-fixtures` re-stamp, one PR per node. Both pins are mutation-verified **against their own fix**, so the gate names the assertions to update rather than silently accepting a repair. | Brief *(**DONE — 2026-08-15** — **both halves EXECUTED.** §1 PulseDex published `coverage: 100` on a stream it cannot place in time and `durMin` silently changed meaning between the two `beatTimes` branches; fixed on HRVDex's sparse-block template — envelope and data get DIFFERENT NAMES (`spanMin` / `recordedMin`), the unknowable one is `null`, and `durationBasis` says which of the two `durationMin` equals. `durationMin` deliberately KEEPS its value: checking the consumers the brief named turned up one it did not, `adaptEnvelopeNode`, which builds the Integrator fusion window from it — nulling it collapses that window to a POINT, the DEEP-AUDIT-III §6.2 regression HRVDex already paid for. §2 GlucoDex's `recordedSec` and `spanSec` were the SAME EXPRESSION, so they agreed by CONSTRUCTION and a 6 h dropout reported 100 % coverage; now the sum of measured segments cut on the cleaner's OWN gap flags + cadence (no second gap definition). **On the real 28-day Lingo corpus that is 4 segments where it claimed 1, and 5.7 h of dropout that had been counted as recorded.** ⚠️ `synthetic_glucodex_gap_golden` — a committed adversarial twin built specifically to contain a hole, re-run by CI on every push — had been reproducing the wrong answer faithfully, because nothing ever compared its coverage against its own gap: **a fixture only tests what an assertion asks of it.** `kind` stays `'continuous'` deliberately (it names the MODALITY, not completeness; nothing switches on it — checked). The segments travel to `adaptEnvelopeNode`, so a CGM dropout stops counting as overlap in fusion. **All four `KNOWN DEFECT` pins converted to contract, each in the PR that fixed its node, and each fix reddened the group first — which is what the pins were for; none remain.** **Compute-path, computed not claimed:** `computeHash e8f4070fe122 → 67624a697ce1` (PulseDex) and `2ab4fab25b6e → 5ad4d400eb4a` (GlucoDex), 3 fixtures regenerated each, `verifiedUnder` re-stamped after green corpus runs, orchestrators + docs + analysis tools rebuilt on both.)* |
| [`MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **Four defects a 37-night fold found that no single night could** — three of them SILENT (no throw, no red, no `lowConfidence`, a plausible number in the export) and the fourth a string identical on every night ever folded. Source: the 2026-07-28 re-fold of **37 trio nights** (2026-06-10 → 07-27; `2026-07-13` and all of `07-16..27` had never been folded) + **197 CPAP nights** — ~3 minutes of wall clock at 8 jobs, a cost that had been assumed prohibitive. **§0 the result that gives the rest its authority:** CPAPDex reproduces the device's OWN scoring in `STR.edf` — `residualAHI` bias **+0.055 /h**, median |Δ| 0.06, max 0.19; `centralIndex` +0.053 / 0.05 / 0.19 — so the EDF parse and event indexing are validated, and STR.edf is usable as an oracle. **§1 (P0) CPAPDex has never once measured periodic breathing** (`cpapdex-dsp.js:834`): it matches `'Cheyne-Stokes'`/`'PeriodicBreathing'` and sums `durSec`, but the AirSense 11 writes PB as a **paired `CSR Start`…`CSR End` marker with `durSec` 0** — so both the label test and the accumulation model are wrong and `periodicBreathingPct` has exported a measured-looking `0.00` on all 197 nights, on a subject whose residual disease is central-predominant. Pairing the markers reproduces the device exactly (06-11 1296 s / 4.9 % · 06-25 1869 s / 7.5 % · 06-27 924 s / 3.8 % · 07-08 598 s / 2.8 %, against STR's 21/31/15/9 — **STR's `CSR` channel is in MINUTES, not percent**, which is why the two sources look 4× apart if misread). **§1.2 is the part worth keeping: the committed synthetic twin was authored against the PARSER, not the device** — `uploads/20260613_231433_CSL.edf` encodes one TAL `+3000 [dur 180] "Cheyne-Stokes"`, exactly the shape the code expects, so the golden faithfully asserts `periodicBreathingPct: 20` while proving only that the code implements its own assumption; the two committed REAL nights pin `0`, correctly, because neither has a CSR span. Amends CLAUDE.md's adversarial-twin rule: **the twin must be adversarial against the device's real encoding, not a restatement of the parser's.** **§2 (P1) PpgDex publishes impossible HRV and flags it confident** (`ppgdex-dsp.js:2518`): 6 of 37 nights carry whole-record rMSSD **91–188 ms** against a chest ECG reading 26–42 ms on the same night, pnn50 42–58 % vs 4–18 %, all six `lowConfidence: false`. Not beat doubling (mean HR agrees ≤ 0.5 bpm), not coverage (analyzable 96–100 %, correction 2.5–13 %), not session concatenation (every 5-min epoch is elevated; 06-29 median epoch rMSSD 193) — epoch `lfhf` collapses to 0.1–0.6, the signature of an **alternating short/long interval sequence**. The gate is a pure COVERAGE gate and no threshold on it can reach them; the free SHAPE detector `rmssd > sdnnRobust` (impossible over a night, both values already computed at `:2601-2602`) holds on all six and none of the other 31, with 07-01/07-02 sitting just under the line. Matters because PpgDex feeds the Integrator consensus and is the TCH-named culprit on 22 of 37 nights (median σ ECG 0.91 / Oxy 1.09 / **PPG 2.71 bpm**). **§3 (P2) OxyDex integrates a stuck motion column** — on 07-16/17 the O2Ring `Motion` field is never 0 (0.0 % zero-samples vs ≥ 98 % on every other night; 07-18 partial at 18.7 %), so it exports `motionPct 100`, `sleepEff 0`, `arousalIndex 100` and computes every motion-GATED metric (HRV, SampEn, desat baseline) over an empty set, unflagged; 100 % motion is a sensor fault, and the fix shape is the `_durBad`/`durationInflated` precedent in the same file. **§4 (P3) the severity opener has two unreachable branches** (`oxydex-dsp.js:2144-2147`) — 37 of 37 nights read `Moderate burden` while `avgScore` spans 1.19 → 5.21, because `worstScore` is 8–10 on every night with 28 ranked metrics; a decision, not a patch, with a DISTRIBUTIONAL acceptance test. **§6 fences off what is NOT a defect** (`durationMin: null` = the gap guard working · `mode: null` = correct abstention · 7 TCH exclusions · the sub-1 h nights) and **§6.1 the two open questions that are not fixes**: OxyDex's PB detector and the device's CSR disagree (r = −0.21; mean 5.0 PB episodes on CSR nights vs 12.7 on the rest) — §1 is the precondition for examining it; and device AHI does not predict oximetric burden on this corpus (r(AHI, ODI3) = 0.06, r(AHI, hypoxic burden) = −0.05), one subject, a fusion question, not a validated finding. **§9:** a silent defect needs a second opinion rather than a better gate · a constant is a finding (`0.00` × 197, one label × 37 — nobody noticed until the column was tabulated) | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-29 — all four sections merged: §1 PR #527, §2 #528, §3 #529, §4 #530; §3's own prescription was WRONG and is corrected in place. Followed by `MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS`)* |
| [`MUTATION-EQUIVALENCE-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-EQUIVALENCE-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **(DONE 2026-08-09 — folded into `MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`; the denominator question is ANSWERED and owner-ratified, and §6's last box was closed by `CLOCK-PARSE-EQUIVALENCE`.)** 80 % of `clock.js`'s surviving mutants cannot be killed — so the 90 % target is wrong, not the suite.** Two full 127-mutant sweeps: scoped 73 % / 80 min vs **unfiltered 74 % / 108 min**, i.e. the group filter costs **one mutant out of 127** (measured, previously assumed). Survivors group into six functions, two holding half. Probing all 15 in `hostAxis`/`correctionAt` for a **distinguishing input** — n=2..60 geometries × window 0/1/2/3/99 × 11 query offsets — found **only 3 killable**; the other 12 are ties and clamps where the mutated operator cannot change the result. Reachable ceiling ≈ **76 %**, not 90 %. Proposes reporting `killed / distinguishable` with every equivalent one classified. The 3 are now pinned and mutation-verified.  ⚠️ The mechanism §8 built was **fed once**: `tools/mutate-equivalence.json` still carries only this brief's 3 entries while ~83 measured classifications sit in prose — successor §2. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-09)* |
| [`MUTATION-FLEET-EXPANSION-2026-08-25-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-FLEET-EXPANSION-2026-08-25-BRIEF.md) | **(DONE 2026-08-31 — Phases 1 and 2 only; Phase 3 stays DEFERRED and §4 stays PROPOSED.)** Widen `DEFAULT_FLEET` beyond the nine `*-dsp.js`. **§0 is a design invariant, ratified: the model widens what is SEARCHED, never what DECIDES** — probe/draft only, drafts stay human-read proposals, gates stay the arbiter, and the local inference server is out of every verification path. **Phase 1 (tonight):** `clock.js` + `manifest-gate.js`, both **verified to generate mutants rather than assumed to** — 190 and 69 respectively; the deliberate NUL that makes `manifest-gate.js` read as binary to `file`/`grep` does **not** impede the generator. **Phase 2:** the **20** cross/registry/fusion/edf/coimport files (measured, not the ~19 estimated), `cpapdex-edf.js` and the `*-cross.js` stats first; the "per-file co-load recipes" framing proved stale — there is ONE shared `SPINE` list and §2a measured six files as loading on it alone. **Landed: `DEFAULT_FLEET` is 30 files = 9 DSPs + Phase 1's 2 + Phase 2's 19** (20 minus `oxydex-fusion.js`, reclassified to Phase 3), and the crawl→probe→draft cycle is verified per stage on both Phase 1 files with non-zero artifacts. **§2a-bis corrects §2a's guard row:** FOUR files carry `typeof` guards, not one — but the count was never the discriminator, since three guard *environment globals* (UMD boilerplate, cannot false-kill) and only `cpapdex-fusion.js` guards a *module dependency*, whose realm `SPINE` already completes. **Phase 3 DEFERRED** (render/app need a DOM-shim harness; browser rigs cover execution). **§5's two method traps:** a count that equals its own cap is the cap — both Phase 1 files first reported `60`, which is `--limit`'s default; and canary staleness arrives in **correlated batches**, since #1643 staled `oxydex` and `integrator` together, while `ppgdex` had **0** commits since its crawl and so is stale-`after` vintage rather than drift. | Brief *(DONE)* |
| [`CLOCK-PARSE-EQUIVALENCE-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CLOCK-PARSE-EQUIVALENCE-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md) | **(DONE 2026-08-09)** Closes the box `MUTATION-EQUIVALENCE` §6 left open and nobody owned — `clock.js`'s parse-family survivors (`parseTimestamp`/`_ckMk`/`resolveDMY`/`_ckP2`), which fell between that brief (scoped to the 15 in `hostAxis`) and `JS-DSP-MUTATION-FLEET` (the DSP fleet; `clock.js` is the spine). **8 survivors, not the 14 the parent claims** — three waves plus wave 9 landed and nobody re-counted — and **0 killable**: radix-0 `parseInt` under a digits-only regex, an extra loop iteration that reads `undefined` and `continue`s, and over-determined `_ckMk` validation where disabling one redundant `!==` leaves the others. The verdicts match the earlier pass; what changed is that they are now **backed** — that pass ran a battery whose only positive control sat in `hostAxis`, proving reach there and nothing about these functions. Re-probed with in-family controls it came back **3-of-14 BLIND**: `_ckDMY(a,b,preferDMY,locked)` was called with one argument so the locked branch never ran, and `L94`'s `b > 12` needs `b` **exactly 12**. Fixed to 14/14 before any verdict was read. Earns the rule that **a positive control must live in the same function as the mutant it clears** — a control elsewhere proves the realm loaded, and an unreached mutant is indistinguishable from an unkillable one. Instrument: `tools/probe-clock-equivalence.mjs`, which prints the control tally first and marks a run BLIND if any control is equivalent. |
| [`JS-SEALED-ASSERTION-DEAD-END-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md`](briefs/JS-SEALED-ASSERTION-DEAD-END-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md) | **Lexical name resolution is not analysis — the JS "sealed assertion" hunter was abandoned, and the zero it reported was the reason.** The Python sibling (`capture-host/blind_spots.py`, #965) finds 690 discarded-argument blind spots in 0.39 s and is mutation-validated; the JS transfer is not. Scoping said no first and was right: the double family barely exists there (**22** stub/fake/spy mentions in **32,895** lines of `dex-tests.js`, **zero** in the runner — groups drive REAL modules through `env`), and JS mutation is already parallel and tag-scoped. Narrowed to the family this lane DOES have (an assertion that computes its own answer, precedent removed from #945), a lexical hunter reported **132 → 96 → 72 → 5 → 0** hits as five false-positive classes were fixed by hand (reassignment · accumulator flags · in-place mutation · nested-bracket index-assign · out-parameters), ending at zero with a self-test 6/6 and a reported reach of **4,901 assertions, 99 % resolved**. **A planted tautology of exactly the hunted shape was NOT flagged**, and was counted among the 99 % — because name resolution is file-wide with no scope, and `s` merges **261** distinct assignments (`a` 223, `m` 143, of 2,950 names). The 99 % measured COLLISIONS. §2.8 of `papers/dead-ends.html` in another domain, now recorded there as **§2.9**. Tool deleted rather than shipped: a zero meaning *could not resolve* reads as *clean*, the exact failure it existed to expose. Records what a working version needs (AST + scope resolution, control dependence, out-parameters). **Salvaged:** `dex-tests.js:14548` asserted `1.5 - CLEAN_HI > 0.4` — arithmetic over two test-local constants — while its comment claimed it would red if the threshold widened; it now probes `cls()` for the module's own boundary. | Brief *(**DONE** 2026-08-05)* |
| [`MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **The residue of executing MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS** — the questions its fixes UNBLOCKED rather than answered, plus the capture-side causes nobody has chased. **§1.1 (measurable only because §1 landed):** OxyDex's periodic-breathing detector and the device's CSR **disagree, with the wrong sign** — mean OxyDex PB episodes is **5.0** on the four nights the machine scored CSR>0 and **12.7** on the 33 it scored CSR=0 (`r = −0.21`), while OxyDex's impression says "CS pattern likely — review CPAP pressure" on 28 of 37 nights against the device's 4. Possibly two different constructs each behaving correctly; currently unexamined, and the advice rests on it. **§1.2:** device AHI does not predict oximetric burden here (`r(AHI,ODI3)=0.06`, `r(AHI,hypoxic burden)=−0.05`; 06-14 AHI 1.11 with burden 16.8 vs 07-23 AHI 8.00 with burden 0.9) — a fusion question for `PAPERS-ROADMAP`, one subject, not to be surfaced in a node. **§2 capture-side:** what broke the O2Ring motion byte on 07-16..18 and then **healed it by itself** is unexplained — the device's own `.dat` was 94–98 % zero on the very same nights, so one decode was right and one wrong on identical data; an intermittent corruption that self-heals is the worst kind to leave, because the next occurrence is silently guarded rather than noticed. Plus the reconnect storm (**42/73/99** fragments on three nights) handed to `VIGIL-RECONNECT-BACKOFF-AND-LINK-COUNT`. **§3 unclaimed data already on the card:** CPAPDex parses past `Arousal` and drops it; `reraIndex` reports a measured-looking `0` where the device emits no RERA label at all (should be `null`); `SA2.edf`'s wired `SpO2.1s` is the drop-free source the fragmented nights argue for. **§4 process:** a brief's fix sketch is a lead not an instruction (**third time** — §3's prescription shipped green and missed one of the two nights it existed for, because the fault was per-SOURCE and a merged night looks healthy by fraction); **`--ours` on `provenance/*.json` during a rebase is a GATE-B trap** (right for bytes, wrong for the ledger — caught as `1513466c… ≠ recorded a290a0…`); **`npm run check` includes `typecheck` and the suite does not** (4231 assertions + both build gates + provenance green locally, then red on `tsc` in CI); same-app units serialize even in separate worktrees; and a mutation check on a new invariant group is worth five minutes — the one here would have passed against the old code | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **`fitClockOffset` estimates each channel separately and then VOTES; pooling instead is measurably better.** Every failure of the current estimator — the arbitrary tie (#589), the corroborated-but-wrong night, the nights that yield no number — is one architectural choice: it discards the joint evidence, then rebuilds a decision by voting on distinct-node counts, throwing away the `peakOverFloor` and CI it computed. Measured on **31 nights re-folded under current code**: cluster vote **22/25** in the 36–42 min band, strongest channel 24/25, strongest non-stage 25/25, **POOLED joint fit 29/29** — and pooling additionally resolves **4 nights no per-channel fit could touch** (06-14, 06-19, 07-05, 07-25), because eight weak channels together carry what none carries alone. The statistic slides ONE candidate offset across the night and z-scores every channel against **its own** chance floor (scale-free, so a dense channel cannot dominate), needing **no allow-list** — a noise channel contributes noise at every lag and cannot move the peak. **Null-calibrated (93 random anchor sets):** per-night Z is *not* a separator (12/31 real nights sit below the null's max 9.6) — the evidence is **concentration**: 29 nights inside a 3.58 min band (2.0% of the search range) the null hits 6.5% of the time, ~10⁻³⁴. Answers the parent's open §2b prediction, which the per-channel fit could not: post-correction **−22.33 / −21.17 min on two independent nights, agreeing within 1.16 min**. Also **retracts** parent §2d's *"consuming only corroborated fits is 7/7 correct"* — 2026-06-15 is corroborated by 2 nodes and reports 1.53 min. **EXECUTED 2026-07-31** — `fitClockOffsetPooled` shipped (27 gated assertions), `runFusion`/`integrator-app`/`trio-batch` cut over, `fitClockOffset` deprecated-not-deleted. Corpus reproduced the headline exactly (**29/29 vs 22/25**, whole corpus 2.3 s) and added the property that matters more: **every night it calls confident is in band (21/21), and every night it does NOT is also in band (8/8)** — the in-run null is conservative, never wrong-directioned. Confidence is the **gap-shuffle permutation p-value**, chosen because it degrades honestly on periodic anchors (which pin the offset only modulo their period). Execution added two things the brief did not ask for: the **planted-offset control** (which caught a 37 s argmax bias inside the ±45 s plateau — the point estimate is now the support centroid) and **`underpowered`/`pFloor`**, after a `nullIters: 10` run returned a clean zero-significant negative that was purely an artifact of 1/(N+1) ≥ 0.09. ⛔ **§8.2 CORRECTED the same day** — the *"21/21 confident nights in band, never wrong-directioned"* claim was an **n=31 artifact**, the same shape as the `7/7` it retracts: on the **maximum 45-night corpus** (§8.4 — +9 exports-only nights, +5 admitted by `--min-overlap 0`, since three-way overlap is a *fusion* precondition the clock fit does not need) it is **26/27**, because **2026-07-23 is confident at 35.18 min**. What holds on 43 pre-correction nights: pooled returns a number on **43/43** (vote 36), **41/43 in band** (vote 30/36), and 15 of the 16 non-confident nights are in band anyway. §8.5 diagnoses the error — not stale exports (the other 8 legacy nights are normal), not a device clock (two channels of the SAME H10 file disagree by 3.58 min), but **half-period aliasing** (half the night's mean apnea interval is 3.47 min) — and deliberately ships **no guard**, because inventing one from a single night is fitting the estimator to its own corpus. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **Once the clock is pinned, every channel is on one timeline — and the pairs do not say what the ladder says.** Executing the pooled fit produced a *physiology* instrument as a by-product: with each night's CPAP↔wearable offset measured, any channel pair can be swept at 1 s resolution against its own shuffled null. 44 pairs / 30 nights ⇒ **9 significant on ≥5 nights**, with the same-device and IMU↔IMU controls landing at 1 s as they must. The finding: `autonomic_surge` ↔ `movement_onset` is significant on **29/30 nights** (Z 11.3) but its latency is **bimodal — +12 s on 22 nights, −22 s on 7 — with a depletion at simultaneity (10 of 992 deltas within ±5 s)**. The per-night MAD of 3 s hides this; only the pooled delta histogram shows it. **Three explanations tested and rejected**: epoch quantisation (0 % of stamps on any grid), CVHR cycle aliasing (median `periodSec` 20 s predicts modes where the histogram has a hole), detector mutual exclusion (no motion gate in the emitter). Also records that this **contradicts `CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW` §2d's latency ladder** (which orders movement 30 s *ahead* of the surge) — the ladder being the weaker evidence, inferred through the now-deprecated estimator — and that the CPAP rows are **circular** for latency because they were aligned by a fit those same channels dominate. Explicitly refuses to build a metric or a paper on it until the modes are conditioned on arousal intensity and both fiducials document which instant they stamp. Also carries the parent's one confident error (**2026-07-23**, §3) with a candidate guard — reuse the deprecated estimator's clustering as a **disagreement detector** rather than a selector — held back pending validation against all 45 nights; and records that `trio-batch`'s hard-coded `have.length < 3` trio gate (§4) puts **2026-06-06/07/09/13** permanently out of corpus for want of a third device, though a 2-device night is fittable in principle. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01 — §1 + §3 + §4 + §5-window + §6.2 resolved: the bimodal latency was the FIDUCIAL. `autonomic_surge` stamps the bradycardia TROUGH, not the rebound it is named for, which sits `periodSec` later (median 20 s). Re-measuring the same 30 nights against trough+periodSec turns a bimodal +10/−20 distribution with a hole at simultaneity — **10 of 992** deltas within ±5 s — into **one mode with 330 of 915, 36.1 %**. `tMs` unchanged (published contract, and the trough IS the right CVHR fiducial); `meta.peakTMs` now publishes the rebound. `CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW` §2d amended, ladder deliberately still not rewritten. **§3 REJECTED with the measurement that rejects it:** the proposed channel-disagreement guard flags **22 of 22 correct confident nights**, because agreeing-channel argmax range (70–9425 s) dwarfs the peak support (0–65 s) — individual argmaxes are noise, which is why pooling replaced the vote. No guard is calibratable either: 2026-07-23’s raw data is gone and the 36 reproducible nights contain **zero** confident-but-wrong. The mechanism is already caught at source — planted periodic anchors → `ambiguous`, confidence withheld, and a planted half-period alias still returns the TRUE offset — now gated. This corpus was never at risk: apnea-train interval CV **1.04–2.18**, and an aperiodic train cannot form a comb. **§4 DONE:** `trio-batch --allow-partial` (default OFF) — the hard-coded 3-device gate is a FUSION precondition the clock fit does not need, and it was dropping **42** nights, not the 4 estimated, every one with CPAP data. 41 fold, 41 fit, **16** clear their own null. Three coupled changes were needed; without the third the flag admitted nights and `printClockFit` still never ran on them. **CPAP clock drift MEASURED** (the §7 open question): all 31 confident offsets against date decline monotonically ~41→38 min across May→July, and a negative slope is present **independently in both subsets** — but **no ppm figure is quotable**: trio-only says −9.0 ppm, partial-only −29.1 ppm over a different span, and the trio slope is barely 2× its own scatter. Nothing computes with a fixed offset, so nothing shipped is affected. 2026-05-31 fits confidently at 25.08 min, 13 min below its neighbours — unexplained, recorded rather than silently dropped.)* |
| [`WEARABLE-SYNC-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/WEARABLE-SYNC-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **Nothing ever compared the wearables to each other, and they do not agree.** Every clock estimator in the suite measures a wearable against the CPAP; none compared the wearables with EACH OTHER, and all of them assumed a shared timeline. Measured from the raw accelerometers: H10 vs Verity sits **1.8–4.9 s apart (median 3.3 s) on 24 of 24 phone-captured nights — not one inside 1 s** — and **0.10–0.39 s on all 6 box-captured nights**. A systematic bias on one capture path, eliminated by the vigil box, invisible for months because no code made the comparison. It was invisible for a second reason too: the only existing wearable-to-wearable contrast was the ~30-event `movement_onset` channel, which resolved just 8 of 31 nights and INFLATED two of them to +13/+15 s — figures this brief supersedes with ~7 h of continuous 50 Hz data. Ships `IntegratorDSP.activityEnvelope` + `alignEnvelopes` (windowed NCC of accelerometer norms, lag regressed against time so offset AND **drift in ppm** come from one fit — Straczkiewicz 2021 doi:10.3390/s21144777, BMAR arXiv:2501.16015, Bent 2022 doi:10.3390/jimaging8050120, Knapp–Carter 1976, Theil–Sen, Louis 2010) plus `tools/wearable-sync.mjs` and a 13-assertion gate incl. **planted 400 ppm drift recovered to 417**. Confidence is a **concentration test** with an exact binomial tail, after two recorded failures: a window COUNT called two unrelated nights confident (multiple comparisons), and a usable-FRACTION rejected all six box nights that agreed to 0.2 s (most of a sleeping night has no movement to correlate). Also fixes an NCC that could return **r > 1**. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **The offsets are applied. One finding survives, one does not, one cannot be checked.** Discharges `WEARABLE-SYNC` §6: every cross-device figure in the repo assumed the wearables shared a timeline, and 27 of 33 measured nights are >1 s apart. Verity mapped onto the H10 timeline (offset SUBTRACTED, applied to event times *before* the fit); unmeasured nights **excluded, not defaulted to zero**; drift deliberately NOT applied (median 0.0 ppm, but junk on low-window nights — 1666 ppm on 4 windows — so a per-night slope through four points would add noise). **SURVIVES:** the `autonomic_surge ↔ movement_onset` bimodality — 29 nights, **zero sign flips**, both modes shift by the offset so the separation is invariant (39 → 38 s); previously checked on 7 nights. **WITHDRAWN:** the ordered latency ladder — only 3 channels reach 5 confident nights, medians span 16 s against IQRs of ±20 s that overlap completely, and `desat_event` (the rung the story rested on) never reaches 5. Built from point estimates with no uncertainty, it does not survive its own error bars — the same failure as the retracted `7/7` and `21/21`. **UNCHECKABLE:** no impulse bridges the O2Ring to a Polar, so its offset is structurally unmeasurable and every OxyDex timing figure carries that unknown. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`O2RING-SYNTHESISED-AXIS-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-SYNTHESISED-AXIS-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md) | **The O2Ring has no clock in its files — its timestamps are drawn, and the ppm you measure is the drawing error.** `WEARABLE-DRIFT-DIRECT` found the ring swinging −2282…+141 ppm while both Polars held a few ppm, called it "not a clock" and left the mechanism open; erratic-but-sometimes-perfect is not what a bad crystal looks like. **From the raw bytes:** both Polars start their `sensor timestamp` at a device epoch ≈ 8.385×10¹⁷ ns; **the ring starts at 0**, because the axis is built at capture time — `O2PPG_NS_STEP = int(1e9/O2PPG_FS_DEFAULT)` with `O2PPG_FS_DEFAULT = 125.738`. Across 60 k samples it uses only **46 distinct increments**, all exact reciprocals of a chosen rate (125.738 · 127.513 · 127.768 · 128.024 Hz) — a crystal does not emit four discrete periods. So its "ppm" is **the error in the assumed rate**: one 379-min fragment at 128.024 Hz reads **+91.8 ppm**, another at the hard-coded 125.738 reads **+783.4** — implying a true delivery rate ≈ **125.836 Hz**, which `CAPTURE-HOST-DEEP-AUDIT` §145 reached from the other side. That explains erratic, sometimes-perfect, night-dependent, and unfixable-by-span at once. **RETRACTS every closure/TCH result involving the ring leg** (two of three pairs) — the −2.2 ppm closure is now best read as coincidence and the TCH degeneracy needs no correlated physiology; Polar↔Polar is untouched. **And it settles why `hostAxis` must not copy the span gate:** the gate is about **leverage** (host timestamps are non-monotonic — 2,948 backward steps, max 287 ms measured — so endpoint jitter over 11 min is 712 ppm and over 373 min is 21), while the ring's problem is **provenance**, where no span helps. Four reasons given, plus a cheap exact test: **first `sensor timestamp` == 0 ⇒ drawn axis**. Guardrail: do not re-calibrate the constant — it improves the number and destroys the evidence that it is drawn. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-03 2026-08-02)* |
| [`TCH-PAT-DRAWN-AXIS-GUARD-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TCH-PAT-DRAWN-AXIS-GUARD-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md) | **The shipped three-cornered hat spent a drawn-axis leg as a clock corner — and the guard was dead.** ppgdex-dsp.js:442 warns closure/TCH/PAT must read `timingSource` first; proven at runtime that two things were still broken in the app. (1) `fitClockClosure` has the §F3 guard but is TOOL-ONLY (never called in-app); the shipped path `fuseHRVConsensus → _tchHat` had no filter. (2) `timingSource` was never plumbed onto the fusion rec — ppgdex exports it top-level but `adaptEnvelopeNode` read it nowhere, so even §F3 read `undefined` and kept every leg. A `timingSource:'none'` PpgDex was spent as a full TCH corner. **Fix:** plumb `timingSource` onto the rec + exclude `'none'` legs in `_tchHat` (mirrors §F3, surfaces `excluded`); null/omitted stays usable so no fixture output moved (GATE B: integrator_tch_golden still reproducible). Test bites against the unfixed code. **PAT follow-up:** `pat-feasibility-worker.js` is vulnerable (reads no timingSource; sharedClock can't catch a drawn axis) but its functions are in NO test lane, so a guard there would be untested — deferred to the extraction point rather than shipping untested. Re-bundled Integrator+OverDex; 6021 assertions green. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-08)* |
| [`CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-AND-CLOSURE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-AND-CLOSURE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Two devices are never on one clock, and the suite has been assuming they are.** Body-worn devices drift relative to each other by tens to hundreds of ppm — enough to walk past a whole heartbeat inside one night — so every cross-node measurement fitting a SINGLE offset per night reports that movement as noise, poor coupling, or a physiological limit. Reached three times independently, twice by sessions that retracted themselves getting there. **Beat correspondence 5–26 % → 43–98.8 %** (100 % in the best blocks of four nights) once the offset is refit per 5 min; chance control at identical DOF is 22–27 %. **The per-block offset is a PHASE and must be UNWRAPPED** — the search spans several comb teeth, so the argmax falls back one RR whenever the offset drifts past a boundary (15 such jumps on 07-27), and a slope through that measures the sawtooth. **Three-corner closure** (`d(A↔B) ≡ d(A↔C) − d(B↔C)` — free and falsifiable) is what caught the missing unwrap; it holds to **≤7 ppm** where correspondence ≥91 % and blocks ≥86, and fails where the phase is undersampled (754 ppm is a broken unwrap, not a crystal). All intervals are **waveform-derived** (Pan-Tompkins RR · 3-LED optical spine PPI), never device files — required, since the Verity’s `_PPI.txt` is header-only on **107 of 107** files and its `_HR.txt` all-zero. **Control:** through the built-in `ECGDSP.validateRR`, the waveform RR reproduces the H10’s own `_RR.txt` to **0.23 % mean / 1.2 % RMSSD** on the night closure holds best — so the drift is in the CLOCKS, not the beat detection. The PPI side has no hardware control available at all and is currently unvalidatable against a device. **Project-wide:** `fitClockOffsetPooled` fits one offset per night — safe for CPAP (0.24–0.78 s ≪ its 15 s support), **not** for wearable↔wearable at 100+ ppm, and nothing marks the difference; **PAT becomes reachable** (per-block IQR 43–112 ms vs the flat, chance-level 264–330 ms under one offset — `pat-gate` wants ≤60 ms); the **capture host** is the right place to measure offset continuously; and `integrator-tch.js`’s reference-free decomposition, used today for amplitude σ, applies to TIMING once closure holds — per-device jitter rather than pairwise differences. **Method lesson:** three honest chance controls all PASSED while the model was too simple — a control tells you whether you beat chance, never whether you asked the right question. | Brief *(**PROPOSED (deferred 2026-08-27)** — every executable item closed; the last box is ⛔ BLOCKED on corpus: the closure's passing set anti-selects for measurement quality, so it needs nights with ≥2 fragments per device AND consistent legs)* |
| [`CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-17-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-17-BRIEF.md) | **What executing the per-device σ item surfaced.** `CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-AND-CLOSURE` §5 asked for "a third **mechanical** channel — or measure against the capture host"; the host route is done (344 streams, 16 box nights, `tools/device-stability.mjs`, gated `device-stability · per-device-sigma`), and this carries the residue. **(1) `hostAxis.stability`’s `atShortestMs`/`atLongestMs` are ms/s, not ms** — which is why the same object multiplies by 1000 to get `ppmUncertainty`; the names understate their values 1000×, a unit mislabel in shipped spine code (additive `…Ppm` fix, but `clock.js` is a shared-spine change, so land it with other spine work). **(2) `tools/dual-clock-rate.mjs` carries the defect this work found and fixed** — its `MAX_CRYSTAL_SPREAD_PPM` compares fragment rates by raw max−min, treating −21.0 ± 2.4 ppm and −119.5 ± 309 ppm as a 98 ppm disagreement when they are the same measurement; it is protected today only by its >3 MB length filter, which excludes the imprecise fragments rather than reasoning about them. **(3) Two stale headers** — `HOSTAXIS-STABILITY` (every §6 item shipped in #1227) and `WEARABLE-DRIFT-DIRECT` (§6 all `[x]`, §7.5 closure closes 4/4) both still read PROPOSED; found by checking code against Done-when, not by reading headers. **(4) The real precondition for inverse-variance weighting** is a per-CHANNEL σ of the offset estimate, not the per-device clock σ — genuinely open and small. **(5) The §5 PAT item is left deliberately untouched**: it reads open while the same brief’s §3.6 refutes both routes to it, but `PAT-NO-VALID-ANCHOR` is IN-PROGRESS, so that thread is owned elsewhere and closing it from outside is the §📌 collision that produces no merge conflict. **(6) `tools/doc-search.mjs` is not on `main`** — it lives only on `origin/claude/doc-search`, so invoking it reads as a broken tool rather than an unlanded one. | Brief *(**DONE — 2026-08-27** — every box closed; the single `[~]` needs σ_y in `dual-clock-rate`, a separate work-unit, recorded as a deferred sub-item)* |
| ⛔ [`IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **⛔ RETRACTED 2026-08-01 — the title claim is WRONG; RR↔PPI CAN align these devices.** Every measurement here fitted a **single constant offset to a pair drifting up to 123 ppm** — more than one RR interval per night — so a scan correct at bedtime compares the wrong beat by morning. Refitting per 5-min block takes correspondence from the **5–26 %** reported to **43–98.8 %** (best blocks 100 % on four nights), against a same-DOF +1 h control of **22–27 %**. **Beat times identify a clock offset only modulo one heartbeat under a CONSTANT offset** — that comb is real mathematics and still forbids searching harder; what resolves it is a drift term. Started as a correction of a claim made hours earlier (that IBI beats ACC↔ACC as an alignment signal: r = 0.532 vs a 0.032 null, a 16× margin, against ACC 0.29/0.065) — the correlation was real, the conclusion was not, and the 1.3 s lag disagreement between the two methods was the falsification that got reported as an open question. **Then the control used to make that point turned out to have the same defect one level down.** The wrist↔finger table was built from nearest-beat matching, which is bounded by ±RR/2 and therefore CIRCULAR — a linear median of it is undefined near the wrap. Re-measured 2026-08-01 with a lag sweep (`tools/beat-comb-analysis.mjs`, `--selftest` planted-truth + `--dir` corpus), the coincidence curve is a **comb whose period is the mean RR, with teeth of equal height** (94–100 % of peak) — so the offset is ambiguous mod one beat by construction, for any statistic. Consequences: the old "5–26 % of beats correspond" column was just **the comb sampled at zero lag** (reproduced within 2 points on all 6 nights); **2026-07-27 was never defective** — its 326 ms "outlier" was the +420 ms tooth, and it has the HIGHEST correspondence of the six (2.54× chance); and near-zero medians do **not** prove the timebase sound — those are simply the nights too flat to form teeth. **Why ACC↔ACC works and this cannot:** an activity envelope is APERIODIC, so its cross-correlation has one peak; a beat train is periodic, so its coincidence curve cannot. **Align on aperiodic features.** **Postscript 2026-08-01 — the two-stage escape route is CLOSED too:** intervals could in principle refine *within* a beat once a coarse method picks which beat, but that needs the coarse stage inside **±0.6 s** and the pooled clock fit resolves to **15 s** — a **25× gap**. Both ways to sharpen it were tested on the same 36 nights and **neither helps**: `autonomic_surge` at its rebound (22→21 confident, support unchanged) and `desat_event` at `onsetTMs` (support 15→**20 s, worse** — a conceptually sharper instant that is empirically noisier). Pooling absorbs a single channel’s offset, which is what pooling is for. Survives: the comb itself, the `autonomic_surge` fiducial finding (event channels over ±60 s — drift of 2–3 s cannot make a 34 s bimodality), and the CPAP work (different pairing, intra-night drift ≪ its 15 s support). **The lesson that cost most:** a chance control tells you whether you beat chance, not whether your model is too simple — the original circular-shift null was honest and passed while one number was being fitted to a system needing two. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01, §Retraction)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Every gate is green and five of eight nodes cannot hand the Integrator its currency.** The suite was driven end-to-end in real Chrome the way a user drives it — files through the actual `<input type=file>`, exports by clicking the actual buttons — on the most complete night in the corpus (2026-07-26, 208 MB H10 ECG + `_RR` + `_ACC`, O2Ring `.dat` + BLE CSV + 46 MB finger PPG, ten ResMed EDFs). **Every node computed correctly** (ECGDex: 208 MB in 10.6 s at 433.4/433.7 min beat coverage). **Five then could not give the user the file:** GlucoDex `⬇ JSON` threw `GLUDSP.glucoCells is not a function` — the function was single-sourced into the DSP by `71b89df` to stop the app’s copy drifting and never PUBLISHED on `global.GLUDSP`, so the app threw while `glucoBuildNodeExport` (lexical scope) kept the gates green for five days; MotionDex enabled `#mxExport` inside a `display:none` bar it never `.show`s (rect 0×0, click rejected "Element is not visible") — the one node whose bar is static markup, so it never inherited the `.show` every other node writes with its buttons; HRVDex made `.mobile-nav` permanent at all widths (z 500) but only ever lifted `#exportBar` (z 400) inside `@media (max-width:1080px)`, so **every export button is pointer-intercepted at ≥1200 px**; and **PulseDex + HRVDex both define `exportGanglior()` wired to no control at all** — so the Integrator’s own remediation text ("re-export via the node’s *Ganglior* button") named a button that did not exist. **OverDex additionally loses OxyDex silently:** `classify()` asked the adapter registry before the node-export sniff, and `adapters/oxydex-spo2.js` claims `/oxydex/i` on the FILENAME at 0.95 — so `OxyDex_*.json` was fed to the CSV row parser, died as "no usable SpO₂ rows parsed", and was never retried (`adapters/libre-cgm.js` carries the same `/glucodex/i` rule). Same six files: Integrator fused 6 nodes / 1 finding, OverDex 5 / 0. **The shape of the bug is the finding** — every gate verifies `compute()`; **none presses a button**, so the app-layer export path rotted in five places independently. Also records, deliberately **unfixed**, a **~42 min CPAP clock offset** (OxyDex desats vs CPAPDex apneas peak at −2510 s: **23/33 paired vs 3/33 at zero**, chance ≈8; the O2Ring’s BLE and `.dat` sources agree with each other to −15 s, 13/13) that the Integrator **found and vetoed** — `peakOverFloor 3.89` against a threshold of **4**, and an agreement test that demands corroboration from ECGDex, which emits sleep stages and autonomic surges and cannot witness a respiratory event.  **EXECUTED** — all five fixed and re-verified in Chrome on the same night: the Integrator now fuses **8 nodes / 449 min** (was 6, with PulseDex at 0 events and HRVDex unstamped), OverDex **8 recordings / 1 finding** (was 5 / 0). Ships the gate that was missing: an `exportBarProbe` in render-coverage that asks whether `#exportBar` is displayed and whether `document.elementFromPoint` at each button’s centre lands on that button — a hit-test, because a z-index regression is invisible to `offsetParent`/`getBoundingClientRect`, which is exactly how §3 walked past every structural check. **The gate found three more defects within minutes:** MotionDex had **no render-coverage leg at all** (its bundle was never booted — how a `display:none` bar survived from birth); its **▶ Demo button had never worked** (`MOTIONDSP.genSynthetic is not a function` — `window.MotionDex` published that name, `MOTIONDSP` did not: §1 again in another node); and **GlucoDex’s render-coverage leg was HOLLOW** — on a bare page with no data it scored 16 numeric tokens against `minNums:15`, matched all four of CV/GMI/range/glucose **in the empty state’s own help text**, and cleared `minChars:1500` at 2832 chars, so every assertion in it passed on an empty app. Legs now settle on `#exportBar.show`, the one condition an empty app cannot fake. **Negative-controlled:** removing the §3 CSS fix reds exactly one leg (HRVDex 27/28) — a gate never seen to fail is not evidence. Gates: **4723/4723 zero skips** with `DEX_UPLOADS` on the real corpus (the GATE-C legs actually ran; no export bytes moved), 3 fixtures re-verified via `verify-fixtures.mjs`, browser suite **4719 / 327 groups** (+42 assertions, +1 leg). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-POOLED-CLOCK-APPLY-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-POOLED-CLOCK-APPLY-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **The Integrator already contained the better clock instrument, wired downstream of a veto.** `trio-batch` corrects the CPAP clock on every night it can measure; the Integrator, on the same evidence, corrected **1 night in 24** — because `fitClockOffsetPooled` only ran for a node the COARSE detector had already accepted, and then only to REPORT, while the applied shift came off the coarse 30 s grid. Measured on the 24 trio nights carrying CPAP EDFs: coarse finding **1/24**, pooled confident **19/24**, pooled offset in the documented 30–50 min band **24/24** (37.6–41.7 min). **The veto is not the threshold** — `minPeakOverFloor` is 4 and these nights score **5–12**; it is the ALL-PARTNERS-MUST-AGREE clause, which lets **ECGDex** (sleep stages + autonomic surges, blind to a respiratory event) discard the night. *(This corrects the parent `EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE` §7, which blamed a 0.11 shortfall seen on one night.)* The fit now runs for every dated node and supplies the applied, **sub-second** offset (#624). **After: 19/24 corrected, all CPAPDex, all in band, zero mis-attributions**, the 5 abstentions being nights the fit is not confident. **Attribution honesty:** ranking by |offset| blamed OxyDex/ECGDex for the CPAP’s 39 min (right magnitude, wrong side), and ranking by corroboration breadth failed too because `nNodes` is **3 for every node on almost every night** — the fits are symmetric, so **the statistic cannot decide the side**. It is decided instead by the physical asymmetry the repo already documents (wearables share one disciplined host clock at 0.10–0.39 s; the ResMed has none), so only a node named **un-disciplined** is corrected. **States what it does NOT fix:** on 2026-07-26 desat pairing goes **3/33 → 12/33** but the **199 s residual** exceeds the −15…+60 s coupling window, so DESAT MATCH RATE stays 0 % — the pooled fit averages channels that carry real physiological latency, and the next step is a NARROWER fit on the channel pair a coupling actually scores. **PAT answered with a number:** plateau **median 20 s / max 85 s** against `pat-gate.js`’s **60 ms** bar — ~330× too coarse, so event-coincidence alignment can never feed PAT; that needs `pat-align.js` ACC anchors, and even those sit at 0.10–0.39 s. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`CPAP-CLOCK-LONGITUDINAL-SEGMENT-2026-08-21-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CPAP-CLOCK-LONGITUDINAL-SEGMENT-2026-08-21-BRIEF.md) | **Step-aware longitudinal CPAP offset — fill CPAP-only nights, refuse across steps.** The per-fusion `fitClockOffsetPooled` already measures each night's CPAP offset when a co-recorded reference exists, but a CPAP-only night gets nothing and a single pooled offset was `#1606`-known-inadequate (*"the CPAP clock STEPS by an hour mid-corpus"* — travel). Adds `fitClockOffsetSegments`: detects steps between adjacent anchors (`|Δ| ≥ 10 min` OR `|ppm| > 200` — travel/tz jumps vs crystal drift), fits linear drift per segment with plausibility checks (residual RMS ≤ 90 s, `|ppm| ≤ 200`), interpolates unanchored nights *inside* a passed segment, and **refuses** across step boundaries, on extrapolation, and in single-anchor/failed segments — each with a named reason. Truth wins: an anchored night's measured offset is never overridden by the fit. Pure, deterministic (Clock Contract). Setting the CPAP clock is impossible (no BLE setter, cloud-only) and moot: this corrects the data timebase. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-21)* |
| [`PAT-GEOMETRY-PROBE-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PAT-GEOMETRY-PROBE-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md) | **DONE — every timeline defect here was a SHAPE, not a wrong number, and each was spotted by eye only after a wrong conclusion built on it had shipped. Detectors for the five shapes, plus a scanner that walks the alignment chain and attributes a shape to a STAGE.** `saturation` (driftRange pinned at the 450 ms window — nine nights at 420–442) · `sawtooth` (offset wrapping mod one RR) · `censoring` (PHYS discarding up to **97.4 %**) · `drawn` (a synthesized `index × rate` ladder, which carries no timing information yet is SMOOTHER than a real clock, so it wins any comparison rewarding smoothness — 34 ms of fake tightness against the host axis’s honest 61) · `step` (the O2Ring’s 3796–22416 ms axis jumps against the H10’s 16–40). **Design borrowed from mutation testing — the SIGNATURE IS THE MUTANT** — and the gated property is SPECIFICITY, not sensitivity: each planted shape must fire its own probe and only its own, with a clean signal, a smooth trend and a random walk firing nothing. **Two fixture defects had to be fixed first, both the repo’s recurring one:** the first plants sat outside `[200,650]` so the bounded probes fired on them (a fixture tripping two rules tests neither), and the `ramp` control used hash jitter, which really does contain discontinuities. ⚠️ **The scanner had the same class of bug:** it decimated the axis before probing, and decimation DESTROYS a constant-delta signature — so it reported `ok` on the O2Ring’s known-synthesized axis; an inert detector reads exactly like a clean result. **Attribution measured:** 08-04 axes clean at stages 2/4 with the defect entering at PAIRING (59.8 % discarded, 6 wraps, step-ratio 22.4) — the attribution `PAT-WANDER-ELIMINATION` spent nineteen tests failing to make; 08-10 shows `drawn` gone from both axes after the capture-side timebase fix (0.993 → 0.626). ⚠️ `drawn` at stages 1/3 is a LEAD not a verdict — a host-disciplined axis interpolating between anchors can be locally constant; confirm against `hostAxis.applied` or the `# timebase=` header. Detectors only; nothing in the runtime gate reads them yet. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-11)* |
| [`PAT-PACKET-ARRIVAL-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PAT-PACKET-ARRIVAL-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md) | **DONE — the PAT blocker is a per-connection BLE buffering offset spanning 2.2 SECONDS between nights (Verity−H10: −867…+1321 ms) against a ~10 ms requirement. Four mitigations tested and eliminated; the one that works ships here.** Each PPG file spans exactly ONE `link_epoch`, so the offset is constant per connection with no second connection to difference against — which is why **7 of 10 nights are anatomically impossible** (the ankle, the LONGER path, arriving before the finger) and the usable corpus is **2 site-nights of 10**. **Eliminated by measurement:** a better BT adapter (changes magnitude, not uncertainty) · a PPS/stratum-1 clock (**the host clock CANCELS** in `t_foot − t_R`; measured root dispersion 2.3–2.6 ms is already 100× better than the term that matters) · **respiration** (right idea — a ~4–5 s period exceeds the whole ±1.3 s range where beats mod-one-RR cannot — but the **O2Ring is AC-coupled**, DC swing **6** vs pulse 39, so RIIV cannot exist in it, and the Verity baseline swings **47×** its pulse and buries it) · **heartbeat on the ankle ACC** (chest 5.6–23.1× a phase-scrambled control, **ankle 1.37–1.45×** with no consistent arrival — the ballistocardiogram is a torso phenomenon). ⚠️ **A tap on one site is NOT a shared event**: chest–ankle transient coincidence is **1.1–1.3× chance** and ankle–finger is BELOW chance, independently reproducing `ENVELOPE-ANCHOR-EXPORT`’s 3-of-75; the workable form is bundling all three sensors and tapping them together before donning. **Why the min-filter fails and the fix:** buffering is one-sided so `min(host − device)` should be the offset (NTP’s estimator) — but `StreamWriter` BACK-TIMES every per-sample `phone` stamp across the packet from one arrival, so the lower edge is a smear the width of the packet: measured, the minimum sits **27–115 ms below the 1st percentile**, an outlier not a floor. `on_pmd` already had the true `arrival` and threw it away; `PmdArrivalLogWriter` now records it beside the packet’s first/last device stamp. Sidecar never a column (the vendor layouts are a POSITIONAL contract that silently corrupted consumers once already, 2026-07-18); written BEFORE the writer gate since the offset is a property of the LINK. ⚠️ **Does NOT recover existing nights** — it makes future ones measurable, and does not itself correct anything. Mutants killed: blank→0, arrival rounded to the second, ns as float. capture-host **3257 passed**. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-11)* |
| [`ALLAN-DEVIATION-2026-08-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/ALLAN-DEVIATION-2026-08-12-BRIEF.md) | **IN-PROGRESS — three clock analyses in one session reached wrong or unsafe conclusions with ad-hoc statistics; the field's answer is Allan deviation and this suite had been circling it for months.** Allan built it because **standard deviation DIVERGES for these noise types as the sample count grows** — so "does it average down?", asked with SD of block means, was ill-posed before the data were considered. Clock Contract §7's "never quote a ppm without the span" is a hand-derived special case, and §7's own "−20.3 ppm over 373 min vs −65.8 over 10.9" IS a σ_y(τ) curve reported as two anecdotes. `capture-host/allan.py` — OVERLAPPING ADEV from a phase series (`arrival − device` is one already; nothing new to capture) + a slope classifier, since the slope names a MECHANISM a ppm cannot: τ^−1 phase jitter · τ^−1/2 white FM · **τ^0 a FLOOR where averaging buys nothing** · τ^+1/2 random walk · τ^+1 drift. **MEASURED on the real sidecars: all four Polar streams are white/flicker PHASE (slope −0.99 to −1.00) averaging to 0.023–0.094 ms — the clock sits ~100× INSIDE PAT's 10 ms budget and is NOT the bottleneck**, so the "14 ms within-connection wander" was phase noise the fitted line already removes. Ring: white FREQUENCY at **615 ms**, four orders worse — a mechanism, not an inference from its scattered ppm. Known-answer tested against synthesised white-PM/white-FM/RW-FM/drift (−1.000/−0.545/+0.462/+1.000 vs theory) with a canary that all four map to DIFFERENT names. Reported in `nightqc` as `stability`, **gated by NOTHING** — the last two arrival diagnostics that shipped with thresholds both fired on every stream of the first real night. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-12)* |
| [`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-2026-08-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-2026-08-12-BRIEF.md) | **IN-PROGRESS — most of what this suite keeps re-deriving is solved in another field; the sweep, with citations, so the next session does not derive it a third time.** ADOPTED: Allan deviation (§1, own brief). **BUILT AND REJECTED ON ITS OWN EVIDENCE:** Fourier-domain template TOA (Taylor 1992 FFTFIT, `tools/pulse-template-toa.mjs`) — the estimator is provably correct (planted sub-sample shifts recovered to **0.0016 samples**) but on the external inter-LED test it gives −9.1 % at 55 Hz and **LOSES to intersecting tangents at 176 Hz** (1.70 vs 1.86 ms best case). ⛔ The compounding prediction — matched filter × higher rate — **was wrong**: at 176 Hz the tangent foot has enough samples and whole-pulse integration stops paying. ⚠️ The far larger effect in that table is 18.78 vs 1.70 ms between two NIGHTS, which is confounded and must not be read as the rate's effect. ADOPTED AS DISCIPLINE: **Kriegeskorte et al. 2009** names "double dipping" — the rule `pat-gate.js:201`, `pat-host-offset` §3c.4 and `PAT-COMPENDIUM` §6.1 each state without a citation, and which was violated again on 2026-08-12 by calling an 89 %-censored window "the best". REJECTED WITH THE MEASUREMENT: geometric hashing (the beat-coincidence COMB is already proven in `IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT`; the resolution is a drift term, not a finer search) · Tobit/censored regression (its bias deteriorates ~60 % censoring and our nights run **46–68 %**) · 3-LED fusion (**measured 2.3 %**, the error is common-mode). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-12)* |
| [`SEARCHBACK-AWARE-INJECTION-2026-08-15-BRIEF.md`](briefs/SEARCHBACK-AWARE-INJECTION-2026-08-15-BRIEF.md) | **Executes CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS §7.3's named increment and reverses its sign: gap-planted injection UNDERSTATES the miss rate ~3x (1.56 % vs 0.53 %), because in-rhythm beats face an adaptive threshold held high by full-amplitude neighbours — attenuate those too and recovery goes 1.2 % → 84 %. ⚠️ Three review rounds each corrected a different claim here, and the survivor is a defect in the MEASURING INSTRUMENT: template subtraction leaves a beat-shaped residual, so α=0 is not silence and the nominal SNR axis understated the effect threefold. (DONE 2026-08-15)** |
| [`BEAT-CAPTURE-RECAPTURE-2026-08-15-BRIEF.md`](briefs/BEAT-CAPTURE-RECAPTURE-2026-08-15-BRIEF.md) | **Builds on #1292: the sparse-cell adequacy rule is necessary and NOT sufficient. A full night clears it in every cell and still puts 48.5 % of beats in the unobserved class, because the log-linear multiplies the SINGLE-SOURCE cells and those mix real missed beats with detector false positives. Adds the modified Chao (blind to singleton contamination by construction) as a diagnostic + floor — with its own ~70 % downward bias under dependence measured, so the floor cannot be read as an estimate. (DONE 2026-08-15)** |
| [`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md) | **PROPOSED — the second sweep (satellite remote sensing · econometrics · inertial navigation · software-engineering reliability · experimental physics), opening with a PROOF that one of our questions has no answer.** 🔴 §1: the TCH correlation ρ is **NOT identifiable from three sources** — 3 pairwise variances, 4 unknowns — and `tch-per-epoch-rho.mjs`'s "direct" measurement is the **polarization identity**, `corr(a−c,b−c) ≡ ½(V_AC+V_BC−V_AB)/√(V_AC·V_BC)`, verified to **5e-15**, i.e. the same three numbers rearranged. It is also biased by the reference's own variance: **reports ρ=0.5 when the truth is 0** at equal sigmas (200k-draw Monte Carlo vs algebra, 4 rows). So the earlier "measure ρ directly" recommendation is **WITHDRAWN**, and the 0.173 motion-proxy figure proves nothing — it compared a weak proxy against a non-measurement. ❌ §4 PROPOSED, BUILT AND REJECTED IN ONE PASS: `hostAxis` smooths **one-sided** BLE delay with a **symmetric** width-21 median, and `capture-host/clock_offset.py` already uses **Paxson's per-subset MINIMUM** while its docstring **cites `hostAxis`** — so the transplant looked free. Measured, it inverts on a clock STEP: on a smooth plant `min-21` beats `median-21` 8.0 vs 19.7 ms, but add a 250 ms step and the shipped median-21 is **best of all 16 variants** (44.6 ms) while every one-sided statistic collapses to ~240 ms — a windowed minimum lags a FULL window at a discontinuity where a median lags half, which §7 disqualifies by contract since `maxStepMs` exists to surface steps. 🔴 Separate defect found on the way: **the width-21 experiment is NOT in the repository** — §7's `9→77, 21→57, 41→168, 81→245` is prose with no harness, so the fleet's clock-smoothing width was unreproducible; now `tools/hostaxis-estimator-bakeoff.mjs` (seeded, real 3001-anchor geometry, scores the real `DexClock.hostAxis`). It still does **not** reproduce §7's ordering, so numbers here are against *a* plant, not *the* plant. Also: **Extended Quadruple Collocation** (Pierdicca 2017) *automatically identifies which pair* is cross-correlated and is runnable on the existing 4-stream CPAP nights with no new sensor · **Newey–West HAC** is the closed form for the closure tolerance's measured 10× OLS underestimate (r=−0.238 vs leg magnitude; bimodal, 12 nights ≤17.8 ppm then 34.8/46.3) · **GMWM** replaces reading a noise type off an Allan slope, and MotionDex is sitting on the IMU literature unused · **Knight & Leveson 1986** names the #1200 common-mode class (replicated 2026 with coding agents) with **blind analysis** as the countermeasure. ⚠️ Do NOT delete the negative-variance path to adopt a non-negative estimator — it is the independence alarm; and Schatzman 2021 measured extended TCH **better than GCOV** at large τ. ⚠️ §3.1 MEASURED, and it corrects §3's own claim: E-QC resolves the correlated pair only **up to its COMPLEMENT** — exact-pair accuracy plateaus at a coin flip (51.7 % at N=5000) while pair-or-complement reaches 98.3 %, because in K₄ every consistency identity containing edge (a,b) also contains its disjoint edge (c,d). No sample size fixes it; it narrows 6 candidates to 2 and the tie is domain knowledge, i.e. an assumption. 🔴 A first scoping said the corpus could not run it ("only 2 nights") — **that was wrong and is corrected in place**: it measured `uploads/` (20 EDFs, 3 dates) instead of the real corpus `Ecg nightly/CPAP/` (**1194 EDFs, 183 dates**). Actual overlap is **28 nights ≈ 2436 epochs**. 🔴 But that was wrong too: **the fourth stream is EMPTY** — `SA2.edf` declares `Pulse.1s`, decodes cleanly, and is the vendor's **−1 no-data fill in all 189 files** across the whole corpus (best night 0.0 % valid), because the ResMed oximeter module was never attached.
