Real night via `trio-batch`: **80 ppm (2.13 s over 444 min), offset −0.12 s, corr 89 % vs chance 21 %, IQR 52 ms** — where a constant-offset fit reports 16 %. **Gated by PLANTED recovery** (−500 ms/0 ppm and +300 ms/−60 ppm both recovered **exactly**; a planted 250 ppm flattens to 33 and is **declared** out of range via the published `maxDriftPpm`). **The controls found two real defects, fixed not shipped:** argmax on a flat plateau (a **~330 ms** offset bias — replaced by a support centroid, the same fix `POOLED-CLOCK-FIT` made after its own 37 s argmax bias), and a block offset regressed against the block START rather than its midpoint, tilting drift by half a block. Ships `chanceCorrespondence` beside every result because the fit maximises the statistic it reports — `confident` demands ≥2× over its own control. `medianIqrMs` 52 ms sits inside `pat-gate.js`’s ≤60 ms bar (**not** a PAT pass — coupling ≥55 % and a real pulse-arrival median remain separate). Called from `trio-batch`, deliberately **ungated from `--cpap`** since it needs no CPAP anchors; **not** wired into `runFusion`, whose recs deliberately drop `timeseries` to avoid multi-MB retention — that trade-off gets its own work-unit. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`ENVELOPE-ANCHOR-EXPORT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/ENVELOPE-ANCHOR-EXPORT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Anchors are too sparse a carrier: 75 movements a night, 3 of them shared.** Builds the pipe `INTEGRATOR-POOLED-CLOCK-APPLY` §6 identified — the Integrator ships `alignEnvelopes`/`PATAlign` (good for **0.10–0.39 s**) and can never use them, because **no node-export carries anything mechanical**. Built end-to-end: `DexExport.motionAnchors` reusing `PATAlign` (no second copy), emitted by ECGDex (H10 chest ACC) + PpgDex (Verity limb ACC) from inside `analyze` where the raw rows are in scope, `pat-align.js` co-loaded and classified, goldens regenerated, suite green at **4847**. **Then measured, and backed out.** On 2026-07-26: ECGDex **75** anchors, PpgDex **67**; anchor-list match gives 13/75 at ±500 ms (chance ≈0.2 — real) but only **3** windows clear `minCorr`, median −1512 ms, **MAD 270 ms**. The estimator is sound — a planted 100 ms shift is recovered as **99.94 ms** — the SAMPLING is not: a chest strap and an arm band share few discrete movements. `WEARABLE-SYNC` reached 0.10–0.39 s with **windowed NCC over the whole 50 Hz envelope**; anchors keep ~10² bins of ~10⁵, so the compaction that made the field affordable is what made it uninformative. **Backed out rather than shipped** because `ganglior.node-export` is a published contract that should change once, and a field with no working consumer is the defect #636 had just fixed. Leaves the real design question with sizes: 50 ms bins = 576,000 values (impossible), 4 Hz = ~700 KB (too big), 1 Hz = ~170 KB (affordable, not sub-second), anchors = ~30 KB (too few pairs) — and argues the **capture host** should measure the offset directly, since it holds both raw streams and already disciplines both Polar clocks.  **§3.5 CORRECTS its own opening:** the claim *"no node-export carries anything mechanical"* is wrong in a way that matters — the exports already carry **explicit per-beat times** (`timeseries.rr.tSec` / `timeseries.ppi.tSec`; 22,460 and 22,145 beats on 2026-07-26), a sub-second shared channel already on the bus needing **no contract change**. Measured: interval-sequence correlation **r = 0.411**, but the implied time offset has **MAD 22 s / IQR 47 s** because dropped beats accumulate an index error, and only **15.9 %** of beats have a counterpart within 100 ms — an independent reproduction of `IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT`’s **5–26 %** on a night it did not use. PAT-window deltas median **336 ms** (physiologically right, clears `pat-gate.js`’s `physical` check) with **IQR 265 ms** — failing the ≤60 ms bar by **4.4×**, exactly as 16 % correspondence predicts. **The limit is physiological, not plumbing**, so one direction closes: do not build an RR↔PPI aligner.  **§3.6 tests a TWO-PASS scheme** (coarse ACC lock dissolves the mod-RR ambiguity, beat trains then refine) — right shape, still fails, and **the control is the finding**: best IQR over a ±3 s sweep is **264 ms at −0.90 s**, while the same statistic **shifted a full hour away** gives **330 ms** at the same ~46 % coverage. Being an hour wrong looks like being right; the IQR is flat everywhere. At ~16 % beat correspondence a 640 ms window with ~1 s beats fills by chance at every offset, so the measurement is the WINDOW’S width, not a pulse transit. Without the control the sweep reads as an alignment. What would move pass 2 is **better beat correspondence, not a better search** — restrict to high-SQI PPG epochs; `PAT-FEASIBILITY`’s `coupling >= 55 %` already encodes that, and this night is nowhere near it.  ⛔ **§3.7 RETRACTS §3.5 AND §3.6 — the 16 % was DRIFT, and RR↔PPI works.** Both measured a **drifting pair with a single constant offset**. Refitting locally in 5-min blocks on the beats each node says it OBSERVED (99.7 % of RR, 97.5 % of PPI): **median local correspondence 90.6 %** (best 99.6 %) against a **+1 h chance control at 21.3 %** carrying the same search degrees of freedom. Beat correspondence is **~90 %, not 16 %**. The pair drifts at **5.2 ms/min = 87 ppm → 2,264 ms over 435 min** — two seconds, larger than an RR interval, so a constant-offset match walks off the correct beat partway through the night, which is exactly the signature §3.5 saw (strong interval-sequence r, hopeless implied offset at MAD 22 s) and §3.6’s flat sweep. **Per-block IQR 43–112 ms, median ≈50 ms — inside `pat-gate.js`’s ≤60 ms bar**, so the precision PAT needs is REACHABLE on this pair (not a PAT pass: coupling ≥55 % and a real pulse-arrival median remain separate). The correct instrument already exists — `WEARABLE-SYNC`’s `alignEnvelopes` regresses lag against time so **offset and drift come from one fit**. **Method note:** both wrong conclusions came from one omission — no drift term. The chance control caught §3.6’s non-result but cannot catch a MODEL that is too simple; a control tells you whether you beat chance, not whether you asked the right question. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-03 — a completed negative; §3.5/§3.6 retracted, carrier choice routed to §4)* |
| [`POOLED-FIT-CHANNEL-DISPERSION-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POOLED-FIT-CHANNEL-DISPERSION-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Three channels agree on the CPAP offset to under a minute, and the pool is not weighting them.** Measures WHY the applied pooled correction still sat **199 s** from what desat↔apnea needs. Per-channel `ownOffsetSec` vs the CPAP across 24 nights: `motion_artifact_segment` **37.67 min (MAD 0.67)**, `autonomic_surge` **38.08 (MAD 0.25)**, `desat_event` **39.17 (MAD 0.50)** — versus `periodic_breathing` 36.08 (MAD 3.75), `stage_light` 24.92 (**MAD 35.75**), `hrv_drop` 27.67 (**MAD 62.08**). **The top three are a physiological ladder, not scatter** — movement → surge → desaturation, spanning **90 s** with MADs of 15–40 s, exactly the order circulation time predicts. So the 90 s spread is SIGNAL: the clock offset is common and each channel adds its own latency, which means a single pooled median hands a desat-scored coupling an offset biased ~1–1.5 min early — most of the residual. Also finds that `fitClockOffsetPooled` has **NO per-channel stability gate at all** (its deprecated sibling gates on bootstrap-CI width, with a note that stage impulses "clear a peak-over-floor test at essentially arbitrary lags"), so `stage_light` enters untested and joins the winning cluster **9 of 19** nights. **Deliberately changes nothing:** the obvious cut is refuted by its own measurement — a uniformly-scattering channel would land in the 180 s agreement window ~**7 %** of the time and `stage_light` lands there **47 %**, so it carries real signal on most nights and pooling exists because channels are "individually weak and jointly decisive". Lists three directions (per-coupling offset — **with an explicit circularity warning**, latency-aware pooling, importing the CI gate), each needing a null calibration. Explicitly does **not** revive `WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED` §2’s withdrawn wearable↔wearable ladder — different anchor, different pairing, better resourced.  ⛔ **§1 RETRACTED the same day.** The ladder is **not new** — it is `CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW` §2d re-derived (37.5/38.0/39.5 there vs 37.67/38.08/39.17 here, **same CPAP anchor, same channels**), and `WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED` §2 had already **withdrawn** it for insufficient power. §1’s defence — *"a different comparison … against the CPAP anchor"* — was **false**, since §2d is CPAP-anchored too; it was constructed rather than checked. The withdrawal’s reasoning also applies unchanged: it retracted on *"IQRs of ±20 s that overlap completely"*, and §1’s own MADs are **15–40 s** — the same dispersion, so a 90 s span across three rungs establishes no ordering, and calling it *"signal, not error"* repeats the exact point-estimate-without-error-bar failure both prior briefs retracted. The measurement **also duplicated existing tooling** (`trio-batch.mjs printClockFit` already prints per-channel z per night). **What survives:** §2 — `fitClockOffsetPooled` admits channels on event count **and nothing else**, while its deprecated sibling gates on bootstrap-CI width, so `stage_light` enters untested with ±35.75 min scatter and joins the winning cluster 9/19 — and §3’s measured refusal to cut it (**47 % vs a ~7 % chance rate**). The 199 s attribution drops from measured cause to untested hypothesis. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-03 — measured and declined; §1 retracted, §3's directions are an owner's call)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **The hollow leg was not one leg — two of six were vacuous and a third missed by a single token.** Answers the parent’s open question by measurement: each render-coverage leg’s OWN predicate replayed against its bare bundle with no data injected. `renderCoverageApp` settles on `_numTokens >= minNums && labels.some(…)`, and on the EMPTY app **GlucoDex scores 16/15 with all four of CV/GMI/range/glucose matched in its own "Load a CGM export…" help text**, **PpgDex 17/12** — both fully vacuous — while **HRVDex misses by ONE token (11/12)**, OxyDex by two, PulseDex by three. `minChars` separates nothing: five of six empty apps already exceed 1500 chars. The root cause is the metric, not the numbers — a label regex over `innerText` cannot tell a rendered result from an instruction telling you how to produce one. The parent’s `#exportBar.show` settle is the right condition and not by luck (**false on all six bare bundles**), so all six are guarded today; the hole left was the NEXT leg, so an unguarded full leg now **reds** instead of passing quietly. **The three bespoke legs were audited too and need no change** — ECGDex settles on ≥6 populated VALUE CELLS + `bpm`, CPAPDex on a revealed `#resultsView` + ≥2 painted canvases, IntegratorPB on a named finding. An earlier pass of this audit **wrongly flagged ECGDex as hollow** by applying the generic body-token proxy instead of its real predicate; the correction is the keeper — **counting value cells is materially stronger than counting numbers in `innerText`**, and is the principled end state for the generic legs if the export-bar guard ever proves insufficient.  **Also WITHDRAWS a parent finding.** Parent §11 logged "`OxyDex .dat` emits no `recording.coverage` while its CSV path does" as a defect; checked before acting, it is **correct behaviour** — `oxyCoverage` returns `null` on a contiguous night by design ("contiguous ⇒ no claim to make (DexExport contract)"), and the two paths differ because the RECORDINGS differ, not the code: the `_STORED.dat` is **442.9 min of uninterrupted device memory** (nothing to declare) while the BLE `_SPO2.csv` is **149.4 min with a real 2-segment dropout**. The Integrator’s "envelope basis · 100 %" is right for a contiguous night. **The generalisable error:** a defect was inferred from a difference between two OUTPUTS without checking whether the two INPUTS differed — two ingest paths disagreeing is only a bug if they were given the same recording. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **OverDex advertised an adapter it could not feed and a node it never booted.** `resmed-edf` sits in OverDex’s own nine-adapter UI list, and a dropped ResMed night routed correctly then died — *"unusable frame: EDF is binary + multi-file — pass `ctx.buffers`… the text argument is not used"*. **Two stacked gaps, neither in the adapter** (correct since it was written): (1) `ingest()` called `readText` on everything, so the documented `ctx.buffers` hatch — the same mechanism `oxydex-spo2` uses for `ctx.parseCSV` — was never populated by anyone; bytes are now read ALONGSIDE text for `.edf` only, and the set is decoded **ONCE** (one night = one EDF set of BRP/PLD/SA2 + EVE/CSL grouped by the adapter’s §F4 rule) with every other file marked **folded**, never blanked — a consumed file must say so. (2) With bytes flowing it died again on a realm **one script short**: the `__DEX_NAMESPACED__` block listed six DSPs and not cpapdex, while `signal-orchestrate.js` already had `cpapHost()` / `emitCpapNodeExport()` / `cpap` in `_HOSTS` gated on `canEmit('cpap')` — the orchestration was complete, the realm was not. Real night: **CPAPDex · 29 events, fused**; previously nothing. **Records, deliberately unresolved, that the two ingest paths disagree by one session** — same ten files, app window starts **21:08:19 / 28 events**, OverDex **21:02:25 / 29 events**, identical end: OverDex keeps a ~6-minute leading mask-on session the app drops, which moves therapy hours and the AHI denominator, not just a count. Unobservable until now (the path did not run), and picking a side from one night is the error `POOLED-CLOCK-FIT` §8.5 refused. Also records the identical missing co-load in `Data Unifier.src.html` as **owed**, since fixing one sibling orchestrator and not the other is how they drift. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-III-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-III-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **CPAPDex was silently discarding 76 EDF files across 16 of 199 nights.** `FOLLOWUPS-II` recorded, unresolved, that the two CPAP ingest routes disagreed on one night (app 28 events from 21:08:19, OverDex 29 from 21:02:25) and refused to pick a side from a single night. Resolved on the corpus — and the answer is worse than a disagreement: **there were two implementations of one rule, and the app’s loses data.** `cpapdex-app.js` clustered on a **>15 MINUTE** gap then did `c.files[e.type] = e` — *"last-wins on duplicate type"* — so when a second therapy session starts under 15 min after the first (mask off, bathroom, mask on) ResMed writes a second complete BRP/PLD/SA2/EVE/CSL set and **the app drops the first one entirely**: its scored apneas vanish with no error and a lower AHI. Replayed over the real SD card (**199 night folders, 1008 EDF files**): **76 files silently discarded across 16 nights — 8.0 %**, worst night 2026-04-23 seeing **2 sessions where the rule sees 6** (14 files dropped). The §F4 header had already warned of exactly this failure at ±60 s ("8 sessions in the ~180-night reference corpus"); the app’s window is **fifteen times wider**. **Resolved by deleting the duplicate, not by choosing** — the rule now lives once on `CpapEdf.groupSessionSets`, the shared reader both surfaces already load, with the adapter delegating and keeping its public name. On 2026-07-26 the app goes **1 session / 28 events / 7.27 h → 2 / 29 / 7.33 h**, matching OverDex exactly because they now run the same function. **Fixture immobility PROVEN, not assumed:** synthetic goldens regenerate byte-identical, and the two real-recording fixtures the regen tool had to SKIP (06-12, 06-16 — and a skip is exactly where a stale fixture hides) were grouped under both rules straight from the card: identical set count AND identical file membership. Also closes `FOLLOWUPS-II` §4 (the Data Unifier’s missing cpapdex co-load — patching one sibling orchestrator and not the other is how they drifted). Gate extended with the delegation + real-corpus shape, and **negative-controlled** (removing the `!byType` clause reds it). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-IV-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-IV-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **PpgDex exported an O2Ring finger night as a wrist-worn Polar Verity.** The last unfixed defect from the end-to-end Chrome run that opened this family: `ppgdex-app.js` **hardcoded** `device: 'Polar Sense'`, so a real export contradicted its own source field — `source: "Wellue_O2Ring-S_…_PPG.txt"`, `device: "Polar Sense"`. Every PpgDex export ever taken from the app named a Polar Verity whatever the instrument was, while the DSP had already worked out the truth (`site: 'finger'` for a single-channel pleth, because *"a 1-column pleth IS an O2Ring ring"*). **A provenance field naming the WRONG instrument is worse than an absent one** — an absent field prompts a question, a confident wrong one ends the enquiry — and it mislabels the body site, which the morphology tiers depend on (wrist literature does not license a finger reading). **Same two-builders shape as the rest of the family:** the DSP builder gained `site`/`siteSource` in #626 and the app’s never did, because **nothing executes the app’s builder** — the equiv legs run `PpgDex.compute`. The app block now matches its sibling: device follows `r.site`, plus `site` + `siteSource` (the honesty carrier — `device-default` = inferred from the optical layout, `declared` = the wearer said so), so no new inference is invented. Verified through the UI on **both** real instruments: the O2Ring file → `finger` / *O2Ring-class single-channel finger pleth*, the Verity file → `wrist` / *Polar Verity Sense (3-LED)*. **Source-scanned** because no dynamic lane can see that builder (same reasoning as `verifiedUnder`’s forbidden-writer rule), with a control that the DSP sibling still emits the pair. **Negative-controlled twice — one by accident worth keeping:** the scan first went RED against the FIXED source because the explanatory comment quoted the offending literal, proving it can see the string at all. Also records that `recording.device` has **no machine consumer** (searched integrator/overdex/unifier), so the fix moves no fusion arithmetic. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`OXYDEX-SPO2-SERIES-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYDEX-SPO2-SERIES-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **The oximeter's primary signal never left the node.** OxyDex exported SpO₂ **nowhere** — its whole `timeseries` block was 89 five-minute epochs of `{hr, motionIndex}` for a night in which the device recorded **~26,500 samples**: a ~300× reduction applied at the **export boundary, not by the sensor**, which is why the apnea→desaturation transit resolved 3 nights of 39. Ships `timeseries.spo2 = {hz:1, n, values[]}` on a uniform grid from `startEpochMs` (measured 2026-07-26: 26,546 samples, 98.7 % non-null, **298×** the epoch count). Additive — `epochs` untouched, block ABSENT rather than empty so a reader can tell "predates the field" from "no usable SpO₂"; a second the device never reported is `null`, never `0` (the most severe desaturation physically possible) and never carried forward (which reads as stable oxygen). **1 Hz kept although measurably oversampled** — 6.5 % of adjacent seconds differ, median identical-run 8 s, 2 s bins would cost ≤1 pp — because that bandwidth is one subject / one oximeter / one night, and a bin size baked into an export contract degrades any faster patient invisibly. **No committed fixture carries a timeseries block**, so nothing moved and the field would have shipped ungated on a green suite that never ran it — hence `oxyBuildSpo2Series` is exposed purely to be gated (11 assertions). | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`DESAT-ONSET-FIDUCIAL-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/DESAT-ONSET-FIDUCIAL-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **A desaturation has two instants and the export carried one.** `desat_event.tMs` is the NADIR — right for scoring, wrong for timing, because the event begins when saturation starts falling and bottoms out a desaturation-duration later, so any correlation against it measured the coupling PLUS that duration. Measured paired on the corpus: transit from the nadir is **19 s longer** than from the onset, and that 19 s is the desaturation itself. `startTMs`/`endTMs` were already computed and stamped from the parsed rows, then **discarded at the export** — same loss, same boundary as the SpO₂ series. Now on `meta` as `onsetTMs`/`endTMs`; `tMs` unchanged. Verified **496/496** desat events over 36 nights. **Fixes the fiducial, not the yield** — still 2 nights of 36, because `desat_event` is artifact-gated to the clinical ODI definition (7–15/night); density comes from `timeseries.spo2` (10 nights) instead. That is the real argument for exporting the series: **a node exporting only its own clinical events forces every consumer to inherit a definition chosen for a different purpose.** No committed fixture carries a `desat_event` at all — third change today that moves no fixture and would have shipped ungated on a green suite. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **The CPAP's clock is ~39 minutes slow, and nothing in the suite can tell.** Every CPAP-to-other-node event comparison the Integrator has made was aligned against it; the fusion did not fail loudly, it found no overlap — indistinguishable from *there was no overlap*. A device/config fault, **not** a Tepna bug; the Tepna gap is the blindness (`integrator-dsp.js:3320`, `toleranceSec` default **120 s**, against a node **2,370 s** away). **Found sideways** while asking `MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS` §1.1 whether OxyDex PB episodes land inside device CSR spans (0/20 — but chance was ~1.1, so that proved nothing); what stood out was the first PB episode landing at **exactly +24 min** after the CSR span on two separate nights. Tested against a pairing with a KNOWN lag — apnea→desaturation is 10–40 **seconds** by circulation time — cross-correlating CPAP `_EVE.edf` TAL times (no DSP in the path) against OxyDex desat events over ±60 min: **peak 6.21× over floor at +39.5 min, 240 coincidences vs 27 at lag 0, which is BELOW the random floor of 38.7** (36 nights, 807 CPAP events, 536 desats). **Not a parse bug** — every EDF header start time was checked against the ResMed filename, which encodes it independently: they agree on every file. **Not drift, not a pooling artifact** — estimated PER NIGHT, **27 of 32 nights land within ±5 min of 39.5** with their own 4.7–23.5× peak-to-floor, stable across seven weeks. **The CPAP is the outlier, not the oximeter** — repeating against a second independently host-captured node (ECGDex `autonomic_surge`) gives **4.28× at +38.0 min**, again nothing at zero; the three host-captured signals agree with each other by construction, since `trio-batch` requires a three-way overlap to accept a night and accepts 37. 39 min is no timezone and does not grow over seven weeks: a clock set approximately once. **Breaks:** every CPAP co-observation (`alsoObservedBy`, the apnea-confirmation path, the redundancy accounting `INTEGRATOR-FUSION-ISSUES` §3.1 protects) has run on an empty intersection; `tools/cpap-oxy-couple.mjs`; and §1.1 itself is **void as measured** — it was measuring the clock, not the detectors. Per-night aggregates are unaffected, so §1.2's AHI-vs-hypoxic-burden decorrelation stands. **Proposes:** estimate the lag that maximises co-occurrence and REPORT a suspected skew rather than a quiet nothing (a fusion that finds zero overlap between two busy nodes has learned something and currently discards it) — and explicitly **do NOT auto-correct**, which would make the fusion look right while resting on an estimate and mask the real fix. Same fail-closed discipline as `DEEP-AUDIT-FOLLOWUPS` §C2  **§2b ROOT CAUSE, part-identified 2026-07-29:** the AirSense exposes no time-of-day setting but does expose a TIMEZONE, and it was on **GMT−5** for a device in **Asheville**, which is GMT−4 in late July — so the machine ran exactly **60 min behind** local civil time. That does not account for the measured **39 min**: 60 − 39 leaves the machine's own clock running **~21 min FAST**, with no exposed control to fix it. **Falsifiable prediction:** correcting the timezone adds 60 min, so it should NOT zero the offset but FLIP it — nights after 2026-07-29 should read **≈ +21 min AHEAD**; a reading of ≈0 would instead refute the 21-min inference and expose a measurement bias. **Two design consequences:** the corpus now contains a **STEP CHANGE**, so a pooled per-device offset must detect a step and be versioned by date rather than averaged across the discontinuity ("alert if it drifts" was too weak — this is a reconfiguration, and it is instant); and this is a **Clock Contract interaction**, since the CPAP's EDF carries local civil time with NO zone and Tepna stores it as floating `tMs`, so a timezone change shifts every later timestamp by a whole hour with nothing in the file to say so. The offset is a property of device CONFIGURATION, not only oscillator drift — a device that travels or crosses DST steps again, which is why the anchor estimator's per-anchor offsets are the right shape: they measure *when* as well as *how much*.| Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31 — residual ≈21 min confirmation owned by `POOLED-CLOCK-FIT`)* |
| [`OXYDEX-PB-OVERCALL-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYDEX-PB-OVERCALL-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **OxyDex emits periodic breathing on 92 % of nights; the machine scores it on 13 %.** Over **39 paired nights** OxyDex emits a `periodic_breathing` ganglior event on **36**, the ResMed's device-scored PB on **5** — chance-corrected agreement **κ = −0.039**, not weak agreement but *none* (2×2: both 4 · device-only 1 · OxyDex-only 32 · neither 2; reproduce with `tools/pb-agreement.mjs`). **It is the `estimatedAHI` shape one node over** — a detector firing on nearly every night carries no information whatever its threshold was tuned to, and it reaches the user with an imperative attached (*"CS pattern likely — review CPAP pressure"* on 9 nights in 10) AND the fusion layer as a `ganglior_events` currency, where an always-on channel degrades every observer-counting rule. **Explicitly NOT established:** the device scores flow with its own thresholds and is not ground truth, so disagreement ≠ OxyDex is wrong; n=1 subject; and the threshold's derivation has not been read — it may faithfully implement a published rule whose base rate does not fit a treated-CPAP subject, in which case the honest fix is wording/tier, not a number. Prescribes: read the basis before touching a threshold, measure the operating point rather than guess it (tuning to improve κ on 39 nights of one subject would be overfitting, and "no defensible threshold" is an allowed outcome), temper the imperative regardless, and check the fusion blast radius. **§4 items 1-2 ANSWERED 2026-08-01 — the threshold has no citation, and the sweep shows it is not measuring periodicity.** The gate is `lowMotion && sustained && cross >= 6` against an **absolute** 95 % level; its three constants describe themselves as "detector tuning" and "algorithmic", with no paper and no clinical criterion — and the gate contains **no periodicity test at all** (`cycleLen` is computed into `meta` after the decision, and never gates anything), where AASM scores CSR on a 40-90 s cycle, >=3 consecutive cycles and a crescendo-decrescendo envelope measured against the patient's OWN baseline. The sweep (`tools/pb-operating-point.mjs`, driving the shipped `processNight`) over 37 nights: flagged **36/37 (97 %)**; overnight mean SpO2 **94.6-96.6 %**, so the baseline straddles the crossing level every night and the trace sits within +/-1 % of it for a median **64 %** of the night — on INTEGER-quantised 1 Hz data, where 94/95/96 dither crosses `>= 95` continually with no periodicity. Episode count tracks **% of night below 95 % at r = 0.893** and mean SpO2 at r = -0.821: it is measuring mild hypoxemia burden, not periodicity. **So the shape is wrong, not the number** — raising `OSC_FLAG_CROSSINGS` only yields a stricter hypoxemia threshold still labelled "periodic breathing". Third instance of one pattern after `estimatedAHI` and effort-based apnea typing; twice already the answer was to withdraw the claim rather than retune it. **§4 item 4 ANSWERED 2026-08-01 (§6) — the fusion blast radius is real, not inert.** `tools/pb-fusion-blast.mjs` (driving the shipped `adaptEnvelopeNode` + `fusePeriodicBreathing`, no reimplementation) over the **24 nights** where a trio OxyDex export pairs with a CPAPDex one: OxyDex fires on **23/24 (96 %)**, `fusePeriodicBreathing` corroborates on **3/24**, and **0/24** still corroborate with the OxyDex leg removed. **The measurement had to be a counterfactual** — "3 of 24" reads conservative and answers nothing; the informative quantity is how many survive deleting the always-on observer and changing nothing else, and none do. `corroborated` is `nObservers >= 2`, so a leg on 96 % of nights makes it arithmetically a **one-observer rule wearing a two-observer label**, where the deciding observer is the CPAP's own PB scoring — the rater κ = −0.039 was measured against. The KPI publishes `nObservers` as its headline value under *"signals corroborate"*, and the leg joins the noisy-OR at 0.5 × 0.6 = 0.30, lifting a device-only **0.80 → 0.86** on every block it touches. **Bounded:** `fuseApneaEvents` pools by impulse over `desat_event`/`spo2_desaturation` only, so PB reaches neither the confirmed-apnea rule nor `confirmedAHI` — §3.4's analogy to the double-counted apnea index does NOT extend. **One leg was not measured:** the ECGDex cardiac-CVHR observer is **unexercised, not inert** — it reads `apnea.cvhrIndex` and 0 of 24 committed trio ECGDex exports carry an `apnea` block, but that block landed 2026-07-23 (`11091ef`) *after* the corpus was generated (2026-07-12); corpus staleness, and `tools/trio-batch.mjs` would bring it into scope. **No behaviour changed** — every remedy (withdraw the leg · stop calling it corroboration · redesign the detector) is the same owner surface decision as §5.4, and withdrawing the leg silences the fused finding entirely on this corpus with no measured compensating gain. What landed is the measurement plus a **characterization** gate (metamorphic pair holding the OxyDex leg identical while toggling only CPAPDex, plus the 0.80 → 0.86 uplift as a number), labelled as characterization in source so it is not read as endorsement; mutation-verified both directions. Remaining item 3 (the user-facing string) stays with the owner **§4 ITEM 3 EXECUTED 2026-08-02 (§7) — the qualifier now OBSERVES instead of prescribing**, and **this brief's own number was wrong.** The string fires on **68 %** of nights (25/37), not 97 % — 97 % is the PB-FLAG rate (`oscEpisodes > 0`), a looser gate; the string additionally needs `csScore >= 2`, which 12 nights do not clear. Re-measured by driving the shipped `processNight` over all 37 nights (`csScore` distribution `{0:3, 1:9, 2:10, 3:15}`). 68 % does not change the conclusion, but quoting an unmeasured number for the thing being described is the failure this brief exists to object to. **What changed:** *"CS pattern likely — review CPAP pressure"* → *"CS pattern indicators N/3 — screening signal, no periodicity test"*, and the UARS sibling (*"consider UARS protocol"*) with it — same defect one line below. **The score, its 0-3 ladder and the `>= 2` gate are UNCHANGED**: a wording fix, not a retune, because §5.2 found no defensible threshold and retuning would be guessing. **Gated** by a group with two lifetimes — known-answers pin today's wording, a source scan pins the CONTRACT (no imperative therapy instruction in any qualifier, whatever the wording) and carries the anti-vacuity leg. The exact revert reds **7** assertions. **Compute-path, computed not claimed:** `computeHash a58e4ba0a687 → a6a4245eb9e9` (MOVED ⇒ re-verification owed); both real fixtures regenerated with **exactly one field moved each** (`0.summary.impression`), stamped `verifiedUnder` after a green corpus run; the two orchestrator bundles re-built too (they inline oxydex-dsp). **The synthetic golden did NOT move** — that night never reaches `csScore >= 2`, so the committed-input fixture CI re-runs every push is blind to this change: exactly the asymmetry `FIXTURE-VERIFICATION-GATE` was written about. **Still open:** §6.4's fusion remedy, and the sibling `csLabels` likelihood vocabulary at `:2137`/`:2242` (*"CS pattern probable (Likely)"* — same overclaim, no prescription, but it touches the findings card + reference guide + `cohesion-badges` parity, so it is its own decision). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-02)* |
| [`OXYDEX-PB-OVERCALL-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYDEX-PB-OVERCALL-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | The two owner **surface decisions** the parent measured but deliberately did not make. **§1** the fusion remedy — removing the OxyDex leg silences the fused finding entirely (**0/24 corroborate**), so the three options are withdraw-the-leg, stop-calling-it-corroboration, or rebuild the detector; a default is recommended so the decision has one. **§2** the `csLabels` ladder (`Unlikely·Possible·Probable·Likely`, `oxydex-dsp.js:1526`) rendered as *"CS pattern probable (Likely)"* at `:2242` — the same overclaim the parent tempered in the sibling string, left because it moves three surfaces at once (DSP + reference guide + findings card) and a partial edit reds `cohesion-badges` parity. Carries the parent's guardrail: do **not** tune `csScore` toward the CPAP's PB scoring (n=1, κ = −0.039). **§2 EXECUTED + §1 DECIDED 2026-08-09.** **§2:** the likelihood ladder is WITHDRAWN — `csLabels`/`uarsLabels` were `Unlikely·Possible·Probable·Likely` **indexed by a 0–3 tally**, and the lead read **"CS pattern probable (Likely)"**, a likelihood asserted twice from a score that cannot support it once (`detectOscillations` has NO periodicity test and counts crossings of an ABSOLUTE 95 % level, so on a 94.6–96.6 % corpus it tracks hypoxemia burden at r = 0.893; κ = −0.039 vs the CPAP, worse than chance). Four surfaces moved together (both ladders → `N/3 indicators`, both leads → a bare count, the findings-card displayVal, and the guide's *"Cheyne-Stokes **Probability** (0–3)"* → an indicator count that is explicitly not a probability). **The score and every gate on it are UNCHANGED** — guardrail held. Gated (`oxydex-dsp · pb-overcall · honesty`, 9 assertions, both ladders driven through the REAL criteria) and **mutation-verified: 5 red on the exact revert**. Two catches worth keeping: the real corpus showed the first wording made the impression say the **same sentence twice**, and the source scan **went blind** when reworded — caught only because anti-vacuity is an assertion, now structural. ⚠️ **§2's parity worry was unfounded and the reason matters: `csScore` is NOT in `oxydex-registry.js`, so `cohesion-badges` never mapped that guide row** — same blind spot as the stale-citation class. **§1: OWNER CHOSE OPTION 3** (fix the detector) over the brief's own option-2 recommendation, spawning `OXYDEX-PB-DETECTOR-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`; option 2 is NOT the fallback if it stalls, since §2 already landed the honest vocabulary — the evidence-based fallback is option 1. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-09)* |
| [`OXYDEX-PB-DETECTOR-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYDEX-PB-DETECTOR-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md) | **Build a periodic-breathing detector that measures periodicity** — the owner's option-3 call on `OXYDEX-PB-OVERCALL-FOLLOWUPS` §1, spawned separately because a detector is a measurement and needs its own validation. **What is wrong:** `detectOscillations` counts downward crossings of an **absolute 95 %** level and nothing else — no cycle-length criterion (`cycleLen` is computed AFTER the decision and gates nothing), no crescendo–decrescendo, no consecutive-cycle requirement — so on a 94.6–96.6 % corpus with integer 1 Hz oximetry it measures **mild hypoxemia burden** (r = **+0.893** with time below 95 %, r = **−0.821** with mean SpO₂), not the rhythm "Cheyne-Stokes" names. **Spec:** baseline-relative crossings, a cycle window that GATES the decision, ≥ 3 consecutive cycles — and §2.1 flags that the window itself is **not agreed** (option 3 says 40–90 s, `computePatternScores` says 40–130 s; settle it from the literature and move both sites). **The validation problem, and why this is hard: there is NO ground truth.** The CPAP is n = 1, a black box, and disagrees at κ = −0.039, so "agree with the CPAP" cannot be the bar without breaking the parent's guardrail. The answer is **construct validity**: an adversarial twin pair with IDENTICAL desaturation burden differing only in periodicity — which the current detector cannot separate **by construction** — plus a falsifiable corpus criterion that the r = 0.893 hypoxemia correlation must **break** (state the achieved r; still above ~0.6 means it is the same thing renamed). κ is **reported, never optimised**. **"Earns its place" is measurable:** removing the OxyDex leg must change the fused outcome on SOME nights — today 0 of 3 corroborated nights survive its removal, i.e. a witness that always agrees is not a witness; if it still changes nothing, option 1 (withdraw) becomes correct **on evidence**. Guardrails inherited: no tuning toward the CPAP, no underivable threshold, the badge stays `experimental` (passing a self-designed construct test is not external validation), and the withdrawn likelihood vocabulary does not come back. §6 prices the full §🔒 cycle including the **four** build surfaces `oxydex-dsp.js` reaches. | Brief *(DONE — 2026-08-17)* |
| [`OXYDEX-PB-DETECTOR-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-17-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYDEX-PB-DETECTOR-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-17-BRIEF.md) | **What the PB detector's execution turned up and did not close** — six items, each marked by how strongly it is established: the sliding `cycleIntervals` count (latent hazard, verified NOT a live defect), ρ = 0.98 inherited rather than corpus-measured, the ECGDex third observer never exercised, κ resting on 4 device-positive nights, the synthetic generator still on the retired 40–90 s window, and the unverified gap-spanning intervals. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS — parked 2026-09-05: §4 alone open and DATA-blocked (κ on one subject's 61 pairing nights); flips DONE on new data)* |
| [`VIGIL-HARDENING-II-2026-07-25-BRIEF.md`](briefs/VIGIL-HARDENING-II-2026-07-25-BRIEF.md) | **Hardening pass II over `capture-host/` — the DESTRUCTIVE paths** (out-of-suite; no bundle/`manifestHash`/provenance impact). Pass I took the signal path; this takes the code that can delete a night or destroy the config, with the box at 13.2 % free and retention about to engage. **§1 retention could delete the only copy** — `plan_prune` deleted by AGE alone, so with the backup volume absent the 15th night would have removed a recording whose only other copy was on a disk the box cannot see (measured: age-only would delete `2026-07-16`/`-17` within 6 nights; gated, it deletes nothing). Closes `VIGIL-OVERNIGHT-FINDINGS-2026-07-24` §P3.2. The stall is deliberate and made loud three ways (`status.retention_held`, an edge-triggered warning, and a rewritten low-disk alert — the old text read as "raise keep_nights", the one action that would not help). **§1.2** `active_nights` failed OPEN toward deletion: `except OSError: continue` made an unreadable night (EACCES/EIO/EMFILE) look settled and therefore prunable — the doubt licensed the delete; now only `FileNotFoundError` is inactive, and the test that pinned the fail-open was changed deliberately. **§1.3 the `.archived` marker is not proof the copy survives** — found while testing §1.1 and it would have defeated the gate: the marker records that a copy was MADE, and 6 of 10 nights carried one against a volume that is no longer present, so `unarchived_nights` now requires the marker AND `dest/<night>` to exist, with an absent dest protecting everything. **§2 `config.yaml` was written non-atomically** — `open(path,"w")` truncates first, so a full disk or power cut left the appliance's whole config empty, SILENTLY (it is read once at startup, so it surfaces as "recorded nothing all night" at the next restart); replaced with mkstemp + fsync + `os.replace` + directory fsync, and the read side now refuses a non-mapping config with a message that names the problem instead of an `AttributeError` several frames later. **§3 records five hypotheses that did NOT survive** — IMU units verified on real data (|acc| 998/1028 mg vs 1 g; gyro 3.8 dps at rest); the magnetometer "scale error" is a **false positive** from hard-iron offset, and a sphere fit (bias moves the centre, not the radius) gives **0.4483 G vs Asheville's ~0.48 G**, re-confirming DEEP-AUDIT-2026-07-22's refutation of the 8× claim — **use the sphere fit, not `|B|`**; the O2Ring `[7]`=PI / `[11]`=motion mapping is correct once off-finger frames are excluded (PI non-zero 100 %, motion 99.8 % zero); `archive_night` cannot mirror onto the boot disk (the caller guards it). **Gate:** capture-host pytest **971 → 984 green**, Node lane 3899 green, `build --check` clean; §2's headline test **mutation-checked** against a reverted `_save` after a first draft reached for the closure, got `None`, and proved nothing. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-25)* |
| [`VIGIL-HARDENING-III-2026-07-25-BRIEF.md`](briefs/VIGIL-HARDENING-III-2026-07-25-BRIEF.md) | **Hardening pass III — the DECODE and CONTROL surfaces** (out-of-suite; no bundle/`manifestHash`/provenance impact). Closes the four modules passes I/II named unaudited: `polar_pmd` framing internals, `bonding`, `oxyii` decode below the live-frame mapping, and the remaining `webmon` endpoints. **§1 a part-decoded PMD delta frame was stamped as the frame's TAIL** — `_decode_delta` has five guards that abandon a corrupt frame part-way (all added because real corruption was seen: 0.41 % of MAG rows, 2026-07-18), while `decode_frame` back-times from `last_ns`, the stamp of the frame's LAST sample — which a truncated frame never decoded. Reproduced on a synthetic 10-sample ACC frame truncated to 5: every survivor placed **96.2 ms late** at 52 Hz, scaling with how much was lost, and the last survivor claiming a timestamp that belongs to a sample that does not exist. Right values, fabricated times. The true sample count is unrecoverable once a block header is corrupt, so the frame is now DROPPED (`_decode_delta_ex` reports truncation; `decode_frame` raises and `on_pmd` records it in `last_error`) — an honest gap per the Clock Contract. `_decode_delta` stays as a bare-list wrapper so every known-answer test on real Verity frames is untouched; **two tests changed deliberately** — they asserted `len(s)==1` ("only the reference sample survived"), pinning the VALUE protection while blessing the TIME fabrication in its worst form (a lone reference sample is the frame's FIRST, stamped at its END = a whole frame's span of error). **§2 `_valid_mac` accepted a trailing newline** — `.match()` on `^…$`, and Python's `$` also matches BEFORE a trailing newline. Not a command injection (only a lone newline gets through, so nothing can follow it into the `bluetoothctl` script), but `/api/remember` persists the address and one carrying a newline never matches a real BLE address again — precisely the `writers.IDENTITY_FIELDS` failure: "remembered ✓", then silently never captured for the life of the box. Now `.fullmatch()`. **§3 records six surfaces verified clean** — `oxyii` reassembly already resyncs on an implausible length header and bounds its buffer, `decode()` checks magic/complement/length/CRC-8; `polar_pull`'s user-supplied `session` cannot traverse (`strip('/').replace('/','_')` leaves no separator); the device endpoints are allowlisted against `cfg["devices"]`; `bonding` uses `create_subprocess_exec` (no shell); `axis_scale` and `session_restarted` confirmed. **Gate:** capture-host pytest **984 → 992 green**, Node lane 3899 green, `build --check` clean. **§5:** three passes have now found three instances of the same class — fabricated TIME attached to real samples — each invisible because the tests asserted on values and counts, never on placement. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-25)* |
| [`STORAGE-OFFLOAD-TARGETS-2026-07-25-BRIEF.md`](briefs/STORAGE-OFFLOAD-TARGETS-2026-07-25-BRIEF.md) | **A Storage card in the Vigil sidebar — where a finished night goes, and when** (out-of-suite; no bundle/`manifestHash`/provenance impact). The box writes ~350 MB/night onto a small SSD, so nights must leave. Seven protocols are offered but **not as equals**, and the card says so: **iSCSI · NFS · NVMe-oF · SMB** are KERNEL MOUNTS needing root, and `capture.py` runs with **`CapEff: 0`** (measured — its own recovery ladder's `hciconfig reset` returned exit 1 for this reason), so for those Vigil stores the target and **generates the exact systemd `.mount` unit + root commands** to install once, then verifies it. **rsync-over-SSH** is implemented end-to-end because the daemon can actually do it; WebDAV/FTP are declared but fail LOUDLY ("not implemented — use a mount or rsync") rather than appearing to work. **The check the feature rests on: `ismount`, not `isdir`** — an unmounted mountpoint is a present, empty, WRITABLE directory on the boot disk, so `isdir` says yes and 350 MB/night lands on the wrong filesystem while the operator believes it is on the NAS; `local` is the deliberate exception. This feeds VIGIL-HARDENING-II §1's retention gate directly — an unready target means nothing is confirmed off-box, so nights are HELD rather than pruned. **No password field exists**: `validate()` rejects `password`/`secret`/`token`/… outright (config.yaml is world-readable and the monitor is LAN-reachable through Caddy), so auth is by reference — an SSH key path for rsync, a root-owned credentials file path for SMB. **These values become argv**, so a leading dash is rejected (`-e/bin/sh` in the host slot would be read by rsync as an OPTION), `--` terminates the operands, there is no shell, and mount `options` are charset-restricted because they land in a generated unit where a newline would append `[Service]` directives. **Schedule** is `after_settle` or `daily at HH:MM` in **local civil time** (Clock Contract — no timezone field on purpose) and `due()` anchors on the WINDOW OPENING, not "≥24 h since last run", which would drift later every day. **Confirmation, not optimism:** a night is marked `.archived` only when a follow-up `--dry-run` finds nothing left to transfer. **Gate:** capture-host pytest **992 → 1035 green**, Node lane 3899 green, `build --check` clean, `monitor.html` JS syntax-checked. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-25)* |
| [`VIGIL-COEXISTENCE-AND-RANGE-2026-07-26-BRIEF.md`](briefs/VIGIL-COEXISTENCE-AND-RANGE-2026-07-26-BRIEF.md) | **Radio coexistence, range recovery, and a journal that hides its own warnings** (out-of-suite; `capture-host/` only, no bundle/`manifestHash`/provenance impact). All three items were MEASURED on the box on 2026-07-26 — a CPAP bulk transfer and a deliberate walk-out-of-range overlapped an evening of continuous monitoring. **§1 `capture.py`'s WARNINGs are filed in the journal at PRIORITY=6 (INFO)** — `logging.basicConfig` puts severity in the `%(levelname)s` TEXT only, so `journalctl -p warning` returned nothing across a window holding **33 application warnings** (11 O2Ring + 11 Verity + 10 H10 link errors + 2 STARTUP). Found by making that exact mistake: the overnight watch logged "zero warnings" five times while they sat in the journal. `alerts.py` reads `/api/state`, not the journal, so the product's alerting is UNAFFECTED and this must not be written up as "alerts are broken" — what breaks is every standard operator tool (`-p warning`, journald alert rules, log-shipping severity filters), each returning clean. Fix is a `<N>` syslog prefix (systemd's `SyslogLevelPrefix=yes` is the default, so no unit change and no new SOUP). **§2 WiFi bulk transfer vs BLE, quantified** — 0 reconnects in the 18 min before a CPAP harvest, **16 in the 15 min during it** (Verity 9, H10 7, O2Ring 0); load average 0.34, so NOT starvation. Carries a **methodological correction**: a parallel session measured the same interaction by RSSI over 3 min and inverted the attribution — it reported the Verity unaffected when the Verity is the worst churner (9 reconnects, flat RSSI) and the O2Ring worst when it is the only untouched device (0 reconnects, RSSI *improved* to −57). **RSSI cannot see a link that dropped**; use `link_epoch` deltas over the whole transfer. Extrapolates to ~70 reconnects over a full 65–70 min pull, which is the empirical case for the idle-window rule. **§3 out-of-range recovery PASSES, re-acquisition does not** — all three sensors dropped within ONE 5 s sample and all three self-recovered with no intervention (down 74–90 % of a 16 min window, deepest −100 dBm); the 12.7 min hole was recorded as a NEW SESSION FILE with nothing interpolated, which is exactly what `trio-batch`'s session merge stitches. The weak part is AFTER return to range: the O2Ring re-dropped 4× in 3.5 min, giving the reconnect-backoff work its first measured target (≤1 re-drop per device in the 5 min after recovery). **§4** the CAP_NET_ADMIN ladder is disarmed by design (P1.2) and the one warning saying so is itself unfilterable per §1. **§5** `/etc/udev/rules.d/99-tepna-btdongle.rules` on the box is two fixes behind and needs a password sudo. **§6 records three hypotheses that did NOT survive**, including the CPU-starvation theory that load 0.34 refutes — believing it would have produced a scheduling fix for a radio problem. | Brief *(DONE — **§1 EXECUTED 2026-08-04**: the `<N>` syslog prefix ships (`_PriorityFormatter`/`_install_logging` in `capture.py`), so journald can finally filter on severity and the brief's own sequencing blocker is cleared — §2/§3 can now be re-measured through an instrument that sees warnings. **NO CODE WORK REMAINS as of 2026-08-04**: §2's guards both ship and gate the real call site (`blocking_devices` refuses a harvest while a sensor is actually STREAMING; `due_now` confines the run to a 2 h afternoon window, excluding the nocturnal band by construction), and §5 is RESOLVED on the box — the udev rule is byte-identical to the repo with all five vendor ids, and autosuspend is off on both real adapters (the `auto` entries a naive sweep finds are USB root hubs, not the dongle). Left: two FIELD re-measurements (§2, §3) and one owner DECISION (§4) — owner/hardware-gated, not actionable. 5 mutants killed incl. one bug this work introduced (`force=True` ripped out pytest's caplog handler). Field confirmation on the box still owed. §2·§3·§4·§5 remain)* |
| [`VIGIL-COEXISTENCE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-16-BRIEF.md`](briefs/VIGIL-COEXISTENCE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-16-BRIEF.md) | **What outlives the parent once it closed — two items, NEITHER of them code.** The parent had carried *NO CODE WORK REMAINS* in its own banner since 2026-08-04 and stayed open anyway, because §3 needs a controlled physical walk-away (and must avoid the parent's confound: the original observation coincided with a WiFi transfer window, which §2 established cannot share with BLE capture) and §4 is a privilege decision. **§4 is the SAME question as `VIGIL-AUTO-UPDATE-FOLLOWUPS` §2** — the adapter-recovery ladder wanting `CAP_NET_ADMIN` and the restart path wanting `NOPASSWD` hit one wall from two sides (a capture user who can write `/opt/tepna` plus one granted privileged command is root in two steps), so it routes into the box privilege-model design the owner directed rather than being decided twice. 🔴 **§2 RETRACTED the same day it shipped.** It asserted the recovery ladder is disarmed; checked on the box hours later, `AmbientCapabilities=CAP_NET_ADMIN` is on `tepna-capture.service`, the live process carries it (`CapEff` bit 12), the *has no CAP_NET_ADMIN* warning has not fired in 3 days, and the watchdog is actively managing wedges. The decision had already been made and shipped. Its supporting "measured cost" argument was wrong twice over: today's Verity fragmentation happens **with** recovery armed, so it is evidence about the adapter, not for a grant that already exists — a real measurement attached to a stale premise, which reads as corroboration. Only §1's field re-measurement is genuinely open; the separate **update/restart** `NOPASSWD` question stands in `VIGIL-AUTO-UPDATE-FOLLOWUPS` §2, and the brief now names the two in-repo precedents for it (`link_rssi.py`'s ambient-capability helper, `webmon.py`'s *an argument the caller chooses is still an argument the caller chooses*). | Brief *(PROPOSED — parked 2026-09-02: §1 is a physical walk-away test with the owner present; §2 retracted; no code)* |
| [`POSTURE-SIGN-AND-NADIR-LABELS-2026-07-20-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POSTURE-SIGN-AND-NADIR-LABELS-2026-07-20-BRIEF.md) | **Two labels that lied — both found by reading a real vigil night's export against the raw signal, both with GREEN gates throughout** (executes E1+E2 of `VIGIL-OBSERVED-ERRORS`). **(1) MotionDex posture sign INVERTED.** `classifyGravity` mapped `gz < 0 → supine`, but an accelerometer at rest reads **+1 g on the UP axis**, so a chest sensor reads `gz > 0` when supine. The 2026-07-19 night exported `dwellFrac.prone = 1.0` over 3.8 h with zero position changes, while **ECGDex read the identical H10 ACC as `supine`**; measured chest Z was **+973 mg**; owner confirmed supine. Not a judgement call — the fleet already gate-locked the opposite: `ecgdex-dsp.js` uses `uz > 0 ? 'Supine' : 'Prone'` with a test asserting *"+z gravity → supine"*, and PPGDex's posture test feeds `z=+990` as supine. **MotionDex was the sole outlier of three nodes** on a value documented as the *canonical cross-node position vocabulary*. Every gate stayed green because `genSyntheticACC` emitted gravity on **−Z** under the comment "supine torso" — the twin was built to the same inverted belief as the classifier. Fixed classifier + generator + the device-frame comment; the golden **did not move** (both flips cancel — the fixture always *meant* supine and now *is* supine), and the committed twin was regenerated via `genSyntheticACC({seed: 7})` (seed recovered by matching the existing X/Y noise) so **only the Z column changed**, an exact mirror about ±1000 mg. **(2) OxyDex `nadirBins` binned DEPTH under absolute-level key names.** Keys say `above91/b90_91/b88_89/b85_87/below85` but it branched on `e.depth` (drop in points) against 4/6/9/12 — a proxy that only matches the labels when baseline ≈ 95 %; the true `nadir` was discarded one line earlier in a `.map()`. It reported `b88_89: 4` on a night whose **lowest SpO₂ was 91 %**. Both corpus fixtures show the same systematic overstatement — `OxyDex_2026-06-13_1056` `above91: 0 → 9` and `below85: 1 → 0`; `OxyDex_2026-06-25_0439` `above91: 0 → 7` — i.e. it **invents hypoxemia**, always in the same direction. Now carries `nadir` and bins on it, tiling so each event lands in exactly one bin; an event with no finite floor is never binned. **Gates:** suite **3450 assertions / 220 groups green with ZERO skips** under `DEX_UPLOADS=<corpus>`; the equivalence gate **caught both defects before any fixture was touched**; `build.mjs --check` clean across 11 bundles (MotionDex `→dc240e5619dc`, OxyDex `→60a50d07b575`, both orchestrators rebuilt); fixtures regenerated by tool then `verify-fixtures.mjs` stamped 13 `verifiedUnder` off a green corpus run. **(3) The regen loop could not record an INPUT-ONLY change** (found while landing 1): `runRegen()` skipped `rerecord()` whenever the output was byte-identical, but the ledger triple includes `inputHashes` — so E1 (whose two flips cancel, holding the golden while its input moved) stranded GATE B on `input-drift` with no tool able to fix it and hand-editing forbidden. `rerecord()` now no-ops when the triple is already true and is called unconditionally; verified idempotent. **Lesson:** a twin generated by the code under test is a *consistency* check, not a *correctness* check — both bugs needed a real signal read against physics. Follow-ups: a cross-node posture-convention gate · ECGDex still stamps no `motionIndex` · a physical-plausibility check on 100 %-one-posture nights | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-20)* |
| [`HRVDEX-ALL-NIGHT-SCOPE-2026-07-20-BRIEF.md`](briefs/HRVDEX-ALL-NIGHT-SCOPE-2026-07-20-BRIEF.md) | **HRVDex was built for ~5-min Welltory SPOT readings; feeding it ECGDex overnight exports puts a different MEASUREMENT UNIT through machinery that assumes the first** — surfaced by running the real corpus end-to-end (50 raw H10 `_ECG.txt` → ECGDex → **28 nightly rows** → HRVDex, 2026-06-06 → 07-13). **Why:** a spot reading is a POINT SAMPLE of the circadian HRV curve (hence `d_rmssd_circ`'s ±8 %/−5 % factors); an overnight recording is an INTEGRAL across ~8 h of that same curve, so the confound is already averaged out and applying the point-sample factor **introduces** a bias rather than removing one — selected by `getUTCHours()`, which for a long recording reflects when recording BEGAN, not physiology. **Measured:** 27 of 28 nights started 21:00–23:00 and **one at 01:06**, so that night was graded `÷1.08` against its neighbours' `÷0.95` — a **12.7 % split inside one homogeneous set**; and **"Morning only" kept 1 of 28**, silently emptying the screen for exactly the data the bridge imports. **Landed:** (1) **`_spanMin`** seeded from the strongest available evidence (`recording.durationMin` → 5-min `timeseries.epochs` grid → summed `sleep.stageMinutes`) and NEVER guessed — **unknown span ⇒ NOT all-night**, so every pre-existing Welltory row is untouched. (2) **`_hrvIsAllNight`** (`ALL_NIGHT_MIN_MIN = 180`, matching `trio-batch`'s `--min-hours`) + **circadian bypass** (`circAdj = 1.0`), publishing `d_all_night` so consumers stop re-deriving from the hour. (3) **`morningOnly` checkbox → three-way `measScope` select** (All · Morning spot · All-night ≥3 h) because these are different units, not two filters over one pool; legacy checkbox still honoured. (4) **The morning band no longer judges an all-night row** — LF/HF's `0.4–2.5 good` is a MORNING-REST reference and the corpus sits at median ≈2.0 with nights at 2.4–3.0, so it would have flagged nearly every night; an all-night row now shows the value with **no verdict** (`neutral`, "morning band n/a"). No overnight band was invented — there is no citable one and §📚 forbids fabricated authority; **an honest '—' beats a number graded on the wrong scale**. Fixing this exposed a real renderer bug: the KPI colour ternary fell through to `--red`, so an ungraded value would have painted as the WORST reading. **Verification:** suite **3462 assertions / 221 groups green with ZERO skips** under `DEX_UPLOADS`; **12 new assertions** lock the regression (identical physiology at 22:00 vs 01:00 starts must agree · a 5-min spot still gets ÷0.95 · unknown span never upgrades · `stageMinutes` fallback); **backward compatibility PROVEN not asserted** — `verify-fixtures.mjs` re-ran the real Welltory fixtures and their **outputs did not move**, only `verifiedUnder` re-stamped; on the real 28 nights rows adjusted went **28/28 → 0/28**. Follow-ups: a properly-cited all-night band · duration-normalised SDNN · high overnight SDNN can be sleep-disordered breathing, not fitness | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-20)* |
| [`PMD-DECODE-SCALE-AND-RATE-2026-07-19-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PMD-DECODE-SCALE-AND-RATE-2026-07-19-BRIEF.md) | **Two `polar_pmd.py` defects, found by validating the GYRO/MAG decoders `CAPTURE-HOST-FOLLOWUPS-II` left open** — both produce plausible numbers, no exception, no gate red. (A) **GYRO/MAG are written as raw int16 LSB but labelled `[dps]`/`[G]`** — `decode_frame` (`:271–275`) applies no scale though the device reports `range=[2000]`/`[50]` and `parse_settings_response` already parses it; proof is that written gyro values (max 6000) **exceed the device's own ±2000 configuration**. Fix = `range/2^15`, giving a textbook ±3 dps rest bias. ⚠️ The MAG "field is 2× too strong" reading is a **trap** — a sphere fit (n=81 693) shows it is **hard-iron offset** (centre −494/−188/+1129 LSB, true radius 270.3 LSB → **0.41 G**, correct); MAG takes the same one-line fix. **ACC is native mg and must NOT be scaled** (gravity check: H10 median **1000.9 mg**). (B) **Back-timing assumes the nominal rate** (`:251` `step_ns = int(1e9/fs)`) but the Verity's dies free-run: **MAG 20.516 Hz vs 20** (+2.58%), GYRO 51.684, ACC 51.672, PPG 55.132; H10 ECG is exactly 130.0000. Sign decides the symptom — MAG over-reaches → **678 backwards timestamps** (up to −112.6 ms); GYRO/ACC under-reach → **silent gaps** (22 ms), which is why they looked clean. Fix = derive the step from consecutive `last_ns` + first-frame/dropped-frame guards. **§3.1 kills two hypotheses** (padding over-read — excess is *proportional* to frame size, not constant, so sample counts are correct; `int()` truncation — 6 orders too small). Structural ⇒ affects **every Verity capture including the tri-device σ nights**, but correctable in place (raw LSB is right, `last_ns` is preserved) — no re-recording. §6 records the adjacent O2Ring 359× reconnect churn (link margin, −83 dBm, not code). **§2+§3 LANDED 2026-07-19** (`axis_scale` + device-clock back-timing; 136 pytest pass, each new assertion verified against the pre-fix decoder; real-frame replay: MAG backwards stamps **1042→1**, within-frame spacing now matches end-to-end rate). Execution surfaced two unplanned defects, both gated: **float64 cannot hold a Polar ns timestamp** (~8.4e17 > 2^53 — the offset must be subtracted as an integer) and a frame arriving **closer than nominal** needing a non-overlap clamp. Fresh-capture acceptance **PASSED on the restarted box** (MAG backwards stamps **0**, gyro bias −2.7/+2.3/−1.2 dps, ACC still native mg). **§7 adds the MAG hard-iron calibration baseline:** the scale is confirmed FOUR ways (incl. a magnet-saturation clamp at **exactly 50.0000 G**), and a two-room tumble proves the offset is **intrinsic to the band** (centre moved 0.073 G between rooms) while the **bedroom contributed ~2 G ≈ 4× Earth's field** — so a one-off calibration constant cannot work and any MAG consumer needs **per-session** hard-iron estimation. **§4 acceptance RE-CONFIRMED 2026-07-22** on a larger post-fix segment (`20260720210553`, 554 k MAG samples): **0** non-monotonic MAG stamps, phone-fs≈sensor-fs to **0.00 %** on all four streams, gyro bias −2.85/+2.13/−1.23 dps, MAG sphere radius **0.446 G** — brief flipped **DONE**. §5 corpus rescale has **no committed/shipped impact** (no Verity GYRO/MAG is committed; MotionDex only counts + normalizes to µT at parse, correct for post-fix files); the local pre-fix rescale is a pure-multiply, in-place, deferred until a physical-units consumer ships. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-22)* |
| [`O2RING-PROTOCOL-2026-07-17-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-PROTOCOL-2026-07-17-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — Wellue O2Ring-S (T8520 "OxyII") BLE protocol + capabilities**, reverse-engineered + hardware-validated. It is **NOT legacy Viatom** (OxyII service `e8fb…`, advertises `S8-AW`, Random-Static MAC → resolve by name). `0xA5` frame envelope + **CRC-8 poly 0x07, no AES** (XOR auth w/ MD5 "lepucloud" salt). **Live** (auth 0xFF→setup 0x10→poll 0x04, 24-B header: SpO₂/HR/motion/battery/contact). **Stored-session download** — the `.dat` the phone app syncs: opcodes **0xF1 list · 0xF2 open+size · 0xF3 read-chunk · 0xF4 close**, needs **MTU≥517**; Format A = `[10B hdr][3B SpO₂,HR,status ×N @1Hz][48B trailer, O₂-score×10 @42]`. **Quirks:** advertises ONLY when worn, phone app grabs the single link (close it), short advertising burst (early-exit scan). **Validated:** pulled a 10 h night (SpO₂ 96.3%/HR 50), stored-vs-live SpO₂ MAE **0.76%** / HR **2.42 bpm**; **ring clock ~+151 s off NTP** → stored-`.dat` time needs correction. Code: `oxyii.py`, `pull_session.py` | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-07-17)* |
| [`OXYII-PROTOCOL-HARVEST-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYII-PROTOCOL-HARVEST-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md) | **Harvested `nglessner/o2ring-s-protocol` into our OxyII implementation.** Anchored by a byte-level proof their protocol IS ours — their documented GET_INFO frame `A5 E1 1E 00 02 00 00`+CRC `BF` equals our `encode(0xE1,seq=2)` (committed known-answer test). **New read-only capability:** GET_INFO (firmware/serial), GET_CONFIG (20-field settings struct), GET_BATTERY — all pure parsers. **Highest-value:** the Format-A 48-byte session-stats trailer (`parse_oxy_trailer` + `oxy_is_finalized`), wired into `pull_session` `.meta.json` as `device_summary`. It gives the ring’s OWN avg/min SpO₂ + desat counts (an independent cross-check on OxyDex — **validated byte-exact against a real 95 KB .dat**: total-seconds 31701 and min-SpO₂ 81 match the body) and the `48 12 5a da` sub-magic as a reliable finalisation predicate (size-equality is not — the ring can report full size before the trailer flushes). **MTU:** we do NOT force 517; bleak-on-BlueZ auto-negotiates 247 (upstream confirms sufficient; the 517 in their Bumble examples is a Bumble artifact) — added a WARN when the acquired MTU is <200 so upstream’s silent-drop failure fails loud. **Deliberately NOT harvested:** SET_CONFIG / FACTORY_RESET / FACTORY_RESET_ALL (persistent-state writes) and the unused AES path. **Reverse contribution owed:** we know `0x05` is a 2-channel signed optical stream (upstream: "purpose unknown"). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-08)* |
| [`OXYII-ACQUISITION-CHARTER-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYII-ACQUISITION-CHARTER-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md) | **The lead's charter for the OxyII acquisition-hardening runner** — PHASE-0 audit of the owner's 50-section spec against the tree (~60% already built and field-hardened: reconnection, bounded reassembly, idempotent autopull, RTC discipline, raw PPG — all KEEP-fenced) plus **binding rulings** where the charter overrides the spec: clean-room attestation SCOPED (the protocol core has owner-ratified harvest lineage, `OXYII-PROTOCOL-HARVEST`), hardware design-inputs pulled to the FRONT (single-link handoff arbitration, adapter deafness), **no SQLite** (append-only JSONL idiom), no scheduler/framework, lifecycle derived from `run_oxyii`'s real states. The build = G1 transactional `.part`->validate->atomic-commit stored sync + G2 inventory ledger + G3 restart reconciliation + G4 transition journal, each with planted controls. §6 records the shared-seam boundary with the CPAP arm (PR #1674). | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-23)* |
| [`OXYII-PRESENCE-MODEL-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYII-PRESENCE-MODEL-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md) | **DONE — 2026-09-05 (Heron): presence-aware scheduling MEASURED against the 2026-08-23 poller baseline over 10 worn nights — median close→commit 1.6 min vs ~30 min expected, 4.5 pulls/day vs ~24; it loses on same-night earlier fragments (the `latest` doff scope, 4 of 22 sessions 6.5–10.8 h late → residue `2026-09-05-doff-pull-latest-strands-fragments`).** Originally: the ring's presence is measurable, not inferable from advertising (charter G6, spec §2–§6). ⚠️ Verifying the evidence base changed the design: §3's proposed post-recording probe is largely unnecessary because the discriminator is already ONE BYTE in the live 0x04 frame — `contact == 0x03` is file-open, i.e. RECORDING (`oxyii.py:229`, `O2RING-PROTOCOL` §62–63). The probe becomes the escalation, not the first move. 🔴 But contact answers what the SENSOR sees, not whether the DEVICE is present: with the ring off, contact reads 'no finger' while BLE stays up — so a model conflating them reports a healthy ring as absent every time it is taken off. Absence of advertising is likewise not absence of device (docked rings stop advertising, the UB500 goes deaf, USB is a dead end), so UNKNOWN must be reachable at runtime. Serviced-presence episodes are evidence-based — a new session stamp, a 0x03→0x01 file close, an RTC discontinuity — never a bare timeout, because an hour of silence from a drawer and from a dead adapter are the same observation. Four open questions left unanswered rather than guessed. | Brief *(DONE — G6 measured)* |
| [`O2RING-PRESENCE-TRIGGER-IMPL-2026-08-26-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-PRESENCE-TRIGGER-IMPL-2026-08-26-BRIEF.md) | **IN-PROGRESS — §36's implementation map for the autonomous-harvest charter, built as a DELTA against `OXYII-DAT-AUTO-HARVEST-REFINEMENT` rather than a fresh inventory.** `doc-search` surfaced that 1437-line IN-PROGRESS owner-issued spec on the first query; a from-scratch map would have re-derived it and risked contradicting live rulings (the `3 → 1` end wait, `which=latest` close scope), which is the exact failure the charter's own §1 forbids. Relationship ruled **EXTENDS**. Of the charter's three §3 concepts, **two already existed and are lead-ratified** — the LINK and RECORDING axes; the new one is **PRESENCE** (advertisement observed without a connection). ⚠️ `OxyState.NOT_SEEN` reads like absence and is a LINK state — deriving one axis from the other is the conflation §3 forbids. Presence enters as a THIRD `by_*` term on the existing dispatch (§14: a new trigger, never a second downloader), takes `which=latest` because it races the same closing window a doff does, and binds the existing `pull_deadline`/`flush_gate` **by injection** so a second deadline cannot grow. 🔴 Ships COLD: `scan_coexistence_verified` is a separate key because §2's passive-scan matrix has not run — enabling a feature must not assert a measurement nobody took. Standing ruling elevated: **BLE identity is ADDRESS-ONLY, never local-name** (names are attacker-controlled; a match lets any device in range summon a GATT connection on the shared radio). | Brief *(PROPOSED — presence trigger)* |
| [`O2RING-POWER-AWARE-BLE-LIFECYCLE-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-POWER-AWARE-BLE-LIFECYCLE-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md) | **PROPOSED (core BUILT — a fourth O2Ring axis, POWER, beside LINK · RECORDING · PRESENCE; `capture-host/oxy_power.py`).** The owner task's §1 audit against `476dfdf0` found the protocol path already in SAFE→CONNECT→IDENTIFY→INVENTORY→DOWNLOAD→VERIFY→COMMIT→DISCONNECT order and every protection (F1-wedge recovery, restart-storm hold, address-only identity, offline lock) in place — and found **no model of the radio at all**: the presence observer scanned ACTIVE at a 50 % duty cycle all night with the ring absent, `autopull_poller` reconnected immediately after a failed connect `retries` times per cycle, a charger/doff/presence pull could interrupt a live raw-PPG stream, and five radio-ownership mechanisms had no RESOURCE_WAIT. Built: 12 explicit power states with 45 legal edges (the waiting states structurally cannot reach CONNECTING — that is how "no connect-fail loops" is enforced), a named radio owner, three scan policies (8 / 17 / 50 % duty chosen from the presence TRANSITION), seven declared timeouts, failure-typed backoff ×2 per strike, three strikes → 30 min COOLDOWN, battery bands from adverts that never trigger a connect, synced-idle re-armed only via WORN→RECORDING→REMOVED, counters on `webmon /state["power"]`. Wired as a VETO before any `_*_PULLED` latch is spent; manual pull ungated. 55 engine assertions (30 adversarial) + 18 wiring cases on the real pollers. 🔴 Unproven until the ring is on the bench: passive-scan acceptance by vigil's BlueZ and the night-long power budget (§22/§24, owner-scheduled). | Brief *(PROPOSED — core built; §22/§24 hardware pending)* |
| [`DOCS-LEDGER-HEADER-REFS-2026-08-27-BRIEF.md`](briefs/DOCS-LEDGER-HEADER-REFS-2026-08-27-BRIEF.md) | **PROPOSED — a brief name in BACKTICKS on a header line is resolved by nothing.** `docs-ledger` already refuses a dead relative link in `DOCS-INDEX.md`, but a brief's own header cites its neighbours as `**Executes:** \`SOME-BRIEF.md\`` — prose to every checker in the repo. Surfaced 2026-08-27 when an owner-issued charter's PR went DIRTY and was closed, leaving a window where a brief's `Executes:` named a document absent from `main`; that PR would have gone green citing it. ⚠️ The near-miss was caught by reading the state of a PR nobody was driving — not a repeatable control, which is the argument for this one. **Measured at `c0505b99`: 267 refs on the `**Status:**` line, 1 dangling (in flight).** The SCOPE is the load-bearing choice: widening to the first 8 lines finds 289 refs and **1 false positive** — a template name in quoted prose (`ESM-MIGRATION-YYYY-MM-DD`) whose real dated brief exists. 🔴 States plainly that it finds ZERO real defects on current main: the value is prospective, making the failure impossible to ship rather than caught by somebody happening to look. | Brief *(DONE — gate hole)* |
| [`OXYII-DAT-AUTO-HARVEST-REFINEMENT-2026-08-24-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYII-DAT-AUTO-HARVEST-REFINEMENT-2026-08-24-BRIEF.md) | **IN-PROGRESS — owner-issued spec (§1–27 verbatim in the appendix) asked for event-driven harvest on recording-end; the §1 architecture map found that decision already SHIPPED and DEPLOYED — and never once executed.** 🔴 `auto-pull: armed` appears **0** times in the box's whole journal against **312** poller lines, and zero trigger firings ever: the loop returns on `if not pcfg.get("auto") or not pcfg.get("on_charger", True)` and the box config has `on_charger: False`. ⚠️ That flag's name describes a THIRD of what it does — it also silently disables the NOT-WORN trigger, which the code's own comment calls "the only reachable trigger for a coin-cell device such as the H10", so the H10's sole retrieval path has been dead for as long as the flag has been set (the fabricated-absence class). The symptom is an ABSENT LOG LINE — nothing failed, nothing errored, and no gate can observe a line never printed; it was found by COUNTING arming lines, not by reading code. Dissolves two live arguments (settle-vs-sleep, and this morning's slow night) as debates about a path that never ran — the poller being healthy AND the system being slow were both true. Unit 1 splits the flag and makes arming print in BOTH directions, changing no deployed behaviour; the awake-tail measurement, the duration_s state machine, T0–T7 latency and §22's restart matrix are sequenced after. | Brief *(PROPOSED — auto-harvest)* |
| [`OXYII-G1-TRANSACTIONAL-SYNC-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYII-G1-TRANSACTIONAL-SYNC-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md) | **PROPOSED — the download becomes a transaction (charter G1, spec §8–§19), designed against the measured envelope.** The organising fact: cost is link ACQUISITION, not bytes — a median 78 KB payload moved inside a p90 69.2 s window, so connection count is everything and the retry policy IS the performance policy (a bound stated as a bare count is meaningless; it must be link-seconds against the p90). Five separate functions because every boundary between them is a crash point with a different correct recovery; `select()` is PURE, so the NEW/PARTIAL/VERIFIED/FAILED policy is a unit test rather than a field observation. The ten named crash points are the planted-control list. 🔴 Validation layer 3 (record-boundary) is an honest GAP — it may need a subset port from the JS parser, so a VERIFIED row must state WHICH layer verified it: 'size+finalised' and 'parses' are different claims, and conflating them is the false completion §31 forbids. SHA proves identity, never validity. The re-serve/resume choice sits behind ONE function pending the drop test; re-serve-from-start is the safe default, because a wrong resume offset yields a right-sized, silently corrupt file. | Brief *(DONE — G1 design)* |
| [`OXYII-G1-TRANSACTIONAL-SYNC-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYII-G1-TRANSACTIONAL-SYNC-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md) | **PROPOSED — what G1 left open, and what the gates taught while it shipped (#1702).** G1's own open items: layer-3 semantic validation (needs a JS record-parser subset port; until then `VALIDATION_DEPTH = "size+finalised"` is written into every row so a VERIFIED never over-claims), the physical drop test that sets `resume_strategy`'s one flag, `pull_session` wiring, and fsync durability routed to the chaos lane as a NAMED item rather than filed as untestable — removing either fsync leaves all 51 tests green, measured. 🔴 Two tooling defects found while gating it are worth more than the feature: (1) `mutate_diff` returns a verdict NOT REPRODUCIBLE FROM ITS INPUTS — the first run after a test is ADDED does not credit it, then refreshes the cache so the next run is correct (run 6 survived / run 7 killed, identical tree). The evidence self-destructs, which is why it survived; 'clearing the cache fixes it' was a CONFOUND and was the defect's founding evidence for two weeks. (2) An entire module can drop out of the gate while LISTED as covered — `run_one` sets `error` for exactly one failure mode, so a CRASHED mutmut counts as a successful run and nothing in the file distinguishes crashed from clean. Both fixes are ratified and HELD to their controls. §5 carries the method findings — decision-asserted-payload-not (26 mutants survived a 100%-coverage suite), the boundary test sitting off the boundary, symmetric arms with one covered, and the absent-side-effect alarm that caught a false green. | Brief *(PROPOSED — G1 follow-ups)* |
| [`O2RING-OPCODE-SURFACE-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-OPCODE-SURFACE-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — all 256 OxyII opcodes swept**, complete coverage on device `S8AW2100`. **25 undocumented responders** beyond the 8 known, three clusters all adjacent to documented commands (hence nearest-known-first planning). **`0x83` = VIBRATE, confirmed** 5/5 with contact held — a silent alert channel to the finger the vendor app does not expose. **`0x03` = drain the PPG buffer** (`[4B][count u16][samples]`, ~125 Hz) which **caps at 250 samples ≈2 s and silently discards the overflow** — poll ≤2 s, treat `count==250` as saturation; does not disturb measurement (A/B 0/12 invalid both arms). Reply byte 3 takes three consistent ACK/NACK-shaped values, **not decoded**. **Five findings retracted in one day** — *the null must be a documented harmless command, never silence*, and actuators need a human observer because the data frame cannot see them. Code: `probe_oxyii_opcodes.py` | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-03)* |
| [`O2RING-BUZZ-FIDUCIAL-2026-08-19-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-BUZZ-FIDUCIAL-2026-08-19-BRIEF.md) | **The commanded 0x83 buzz as a self-written timing fiducial** — step 1 DONE on hardware (~1.1 s motion artifact, motion channel is the detector); open: the latency distribution, then the ring↔H10↔Verity cross-device marker. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-19)* |
