| [`SIGNAL-PATH-AUDIT-2026-08-20-BRIEF.md`](briefs/SIGNAL-PATH-AUDIT-2026-08-20-BRIEF.md) | **Whole-signal-path audit** (owner-requested, 4 parallel auditors, every finding re-executed): three date/clock divergences sharing one root (per-field date checks vs the canonical round-trip) — F1 GlucoDex silent roll, F2 oxyii Feb-31 telemetry kill, F3 Integrator t-only −24 h band — plus the verified negatives so they are not re-hunted. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-22)* |
| [`EXTERNAL-LANDSCAPE-2026-08-20-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXTERNAL-LANDSCAPE-2026-08-20-BRIEF.md) | **The external landscape** — sibling projects (independent SET_CONFIG witness, .vld decoders, Polar's own ~21 ppm drift numbers, PulseDB), the ankle-PAT / nocturnal-BP / oximeter-validation literature, the measured novelty check, and the publishable pleth-timing gap. | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-20)* |
| [`STRATEGIC-PRIORITIES-2026-08-26-BRIEF.md`](briefs/STRATEGIC-PRIORITIES-2026-08-26-BRIEF.md) | **The competitive agenda, owner-dispositioned 2026-08-26** — what makes Tepna first in its class against OSCAR/NeuroKit2/Kubios/LSL, in the OWNER'S execution order: **P1 interop IMPORT first** (WFDB/XDF/EDF/OSCAR adapters as the acquisition funnel — import converts communities; device breadth converts one device at a time) · P2 device breadth through the existing Unifier/adapter spine (Garmin/Fitbit/Apple Health/Oura file exports → Philips CPAP → Libre/Dexcom; vendor scores import at `heuristic`) · P3 the "challenge this number" provenance-bundle export (the measurement roadmap's user face) · P4 longitudinal change detection with honest uncertainty (OverDex + the ICC trait/state priors) · **P5 public benchmarks GATED on ~2 error-free weeks / PAT-pipeline integrity (owner's gate)** · P6 SDK as documentation only (EEGDex stays parked) · P7 sleep staging blocked on NSRR data. Anti-moat list: no cloud, no ML black boxes, no coaching, no device claims. Dashboard file; work ships as dated briefs per item. | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-26)* |
| [`O2RING-RAW-STREAMS-ABSENT-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-RAW-STREAMS-ABSENT-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **⚠️ PARTLY SUPERSEDED (2026-08-05) — the raw red/IR half is REFUTED: the ring DOES export both channels on `cmd 0x05` with the SDK argument `{0x07, 0x01}`, which no probe here had sent (see `O2RING-RAW-DUAL-WAVELENGTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md`). The raw 3-axis ACC finding stands.** As written: **the ring does NOT export raw red/IR or raw 3-axis ACC**, hardware-validated negative. It must compute the red/IR ratio internally (SpO₂ *is* that ratio) and integrate 3-axis ACC (`motion` is a derived scalar) — but the firmware puts neither on the wire. All 256 opcodes swept empty, 16 responders re-tested with args `none/00/01/02` (**byte-identical replies**), `0x03` with 2-byte args, and a **positive control**: `0x03` drains and scores 0.126 (one channel) while `0x05`'s fixed 922 B scores 1.02–1.45 against a 1.15 noise floor. Records the false lead — worn, `0x03`'s per-payload length differences are **buffer accumulation, not channel selection**. ViHealth corroborates: it shows only the 1 Hz SpO₂/PR/motion OxyDex already has. Confirms **`0xE1` = device serial** (`2592302100`). ⇒ **PAT needs no red/IR** (`0x03` gives 125 Hz pleth); **re-deriving SpO₂ is impossible on this hardware**. | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-04)* |
| [`O2RING-RAW-DUAL-WAVELENGTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-RAW-DUAL-WAVELENGTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md) | **The raw two-wavelength PPG we concluded did not exist — `cmd 0x05`, argument `{0x07, 0x01}`.** The sweep behind `O2RING-RAW-STREAMS-ABSENT` probed all 256 opcodes with `none/00/01/02` and scored `0x05`'s fixed 922-byte reply as noise: a correct measurement of the wrong request. **An opcode sweep tests opcodes, not the argument space behind each one** — its negative result means "no response to the arguments tried", never "no such capability". Sent the argument the vendor SDK (`lepu-blepro`, `oxyIIGetRtPpg`) specifies, both channels are ordered waveforms: successive-difference ratio **0.0148 / 0.0168** against **0.3395** for the same bytes shuffled (23× rougher). Ships an opt-in `ppg2w` capture stream — `parse_rt_ppg` (count from the device's own field, slice bounded by the buffer, so the observed 2-byte trailer is ignored rather than absorbed into a record), `write_ppg2w`, and a `BUS.register` so the monitor surfaces it like any other stream. **Two things deliberately NOT claimed:** which u32 is which wavelength (a vendor-header claim, and load-bearing — SpO₂ is a ratio-of-ratios, so a swapped pair yields a confident *wrong* saturation, not an error; columns recorded in device order as `channel 0;channel 1`, with the decisive R-vs-reported-SpO₂ test written down and blocked only on ring reachability), and the **rate** (every reply carried exactly 102 records regardless of poll spacing = a buffer-cap signature, not a rate — so `fs=0` and a zero `sensor timestamp [ns]` rather than a borrowed 125 Hz grid). Parser tests verified against **four hand-applied mutants**; the header-parity gate's exhaustiveness tripwire caught the new stream and was extended, not widened. Out-of-suite Python only — no bundle, no `manifestHash`, no fixture re-recorded. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-05 — triaged 2026-09-01: protocol DECODED across two hardware runs (`0x05` is not a plethysmogram, `0x03` is); the remainder is HARDWARE-shaped, and §5's three failed optical experiments are recorded so they are not repeated)* |

| [`O2RING-RAW-DUAL-WAVELENGTH-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-RAW-DUAL-WAVELENGTH-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md) | **The `cmd 0x05` channels are SIGNED and we shipped an unsigned read — plus `rows − markers = 124.91 Hz`, independently reproducing the 125.000 Hz ADC.** Over 61066 real samples an unsigned read maxes at 4294966954 (within ~3000 of 2³² — a small negative wrapping); read signed the range is −285410..3478709 and **no sample exceeds the 24-bit signed max**, so the format is 24-bit two’s complement sign-extended — the TI AFE44xx output format, inferred from data rather than from a datasheet. Silent and destructive: one wrapped 4.29e9 ruins any mean, and it ruined ours — the parent brief’s "AC/DC is 10× too large" was computed over wrapped values and is **withdrawn** (signed: 0.83%, an ordinary perfusion index). The wavelength withdrawal **stands**, on its positive control. **Confirms `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH` §2 by arithmetic on a recorded night** (126.06 row Hz − 1.156 markers/s = 124.91 Hz), and finds the `156` marker on a **second opcode** (`0x03`, 100% isolated single samples). **`0x03` is the real waveform** (112.9 Hz lossless; its beat count reproduces the ring’s own PR to 0.1 bpm) and is a *different* stream from `0x05` (≥153.3 Hz). Records **three failed optical experiments** and the catch-22 behind them (exposed sensor ⇒ front end off; worn ⇒ no light path), plus the owner’s sunlight observation pointing at the one stimulus strong enough to beat it. Open: what `0x05` is, the marker-rate-vs-HR mismatch (1.22× / 1.96×), and `0x03`’s 112.9 vs 125.000. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-05 — triaged 2026-09-01: §2 confirmed 124.91 Hz, §2.1a REFUTED the 100 Hz reading (the delivered rate is the cap, not the device), §3 withdrawn, §4 identifies the real waveform stream. Sole open item is §2.1 — the marker rate is not the heart rate — and it needs device time)* |
| [`O2RING-FRAME-SAMPLE-LOCK-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-FRAME-SAMPLE-LOCK-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — the ring emits EXACTLY 126 PPG samples per status frame**, measured over 90 captured sessions (median 125.997, 0.003 off the integer) and confirmed independently by the host column's re-anchor cadence landing every 126 samples = 1.00 s. **Therefore the two streams are ONE clock** — their ratio is a hardware constant, so no ppm, absolute rate or independent timebase can be recovered by comparing them. **Sample loss becomes countable** (`126 × frames − samples`) rather than inferred from arrival timing (`O2PPG_GAP_MIN_S`). Corrects a crude reading that called the host column *drawn*: the 94% `+8 ms` / 5% `+7 ms` split is **millisecond rounding** of the 7.953 ms step, and the real re-anchors (median **+11 ms**) are already implemented in `capture.py`. ⚠️ The delivered-rate figure from this measurement is **dropout-contaminated — do not re-calibrate `O2PPG_FS_DEFAULT` from it**. **§7 EXECUTED 2026-08-03 — §4(a) built, and it corrected this brief's own §1 twice.** `oxyii.ppg_sample_count` + `capture.O2PpgFrameLedger` (beside `O2PpgGrid`, never replacing it) + three columns appended to the OXYFRAME sidecar. **(a) The 126 lock is an AVERAGE, not a per-frame constant** — declared `N` is **124-128** in steady state (poll interval jitters 0.989-1.007 s; the connect frame carries **250**), so `126 × frames − samples` carries ±2/frame of device-side jitter and returns **−5 120** (a *surplus*) on the largest clean night. It ships **signed** as `counted_loss`; what §4(a) actually detects is intra-frame loss, i.e. `declared − delivered`, shipped as the exact constant-free **`truncated`**. **(b) `Δduration` is a ±1 s QUANTIZED counter — a step of 2 is NOT a lost frame.** Recovering frame boundaries (each frame's last sample is stamped at its host arrival, 2 ms median residual) gives host interval **+0 → 1.000 s · +1 → 1.005 s · +2 → 1.005 s** and next-frame samples **125 · 126 · 127**; a missing frame would show ~2.0 s, a recovered backlog ~252. The ring's second is 1.00346 host-s against a 1.0028 s poll, so the counter occasionally ticks twice (159x) or not at all (180x) and the two nearly cancel. Read as loss those 159 steps would claim **~20 000 dropped samples** where `O2PpgGrid` found **397** — 50x, and authoritative-looking because arithmetic rather than a threshold. **Third misreading of that field** (`frame_gap()` first; §7's own first draft third — §6's method note applies to it exactly). **`O2PpgGrid` is vindicated and nothing is retired:** weighted regression over 60 clean sessions / 60.9 h reads `126.04 − 6.9×steps_ahead_frac − 128.9×inferred_gap_frac`, where a signal costing its samples must read −126.04 — the inferred gaps are real loss at **102 %**, the duration steps are **nothing**. `O2PPG_FS_DEFAULT` untouched, gate-asserted. capture-host **2557 passed at 100.00 %**; JS-lane guard that `oxydex-dsp.parseCSV` ingests the 13-column OXYFRAME byte-identically. Residue → `O2RING-FRAME-SAMPLE-LOCK-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`. | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-03)* |
| [`O2RING-FRAME-SAMPLE-LOCK-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-FRAME-SAMPLE-LOCK-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **What executing the 126:1 lock left open.** Six items, each a question the execution raised and could not close. **§1** confirm the per-frame counts forward from the new `ppg_n` column rather than by reconstruction — §7.2's frame boundaries were recovered indirectly (sound at 2 ms median residual, but still an inference about this device, which is how the previous three readings went wrong); one night reads it off the file. **§2** model the step quantization so it PREDICTS the 159/180 ratio instead of merely explaining it — testable against sessions at different poll cadences. **§3** retire `counted_loss` or give it a measured nominal (it carries a constant, rides `device_seconds`, and reports a surplus on clean nights). **§4** the status frame's PHASE within its 126 samples — carried forward unchanged from the parent §4, **PAT needs it**. **§5** quantify the BLE-latency residual on a `Σ N`-anchored `hostAxis` leg before treating the ring as a timing source. **§6** a second ring — every number (the 126:1 lock, 126.037/device-second, −3446 ppm, the quantization, the +11 ms residual) is one unit `S8AW2100`. **§5 SCOPED 2026-08-04 — is O2Ring finger PAT reachable? The ring is not the blocker, and neither is timing.** **Corpus:** 16 pairs / **38.1 h** of simultaneous BOX-captured O2Ring finger PPG + H10 ECG (largest 9.3 h); box only, since phone nights put two wearables ~3.3 s apart vs ~0.2 s. **Timing:** per-frame re-anchor corrections median **+3.1 ms, IQR 8.0 ms**, only **0.0086 %** of samples corrected beyond 60 ms — **7.5× inside `pat-gate.js`'s `residIQR ≤ 60 ms`**, and anchor spacing median **126 samples**, a third independent confirmation of the 126:1 lock. **Route:** the ring emits **no ACC at all**, so `pat-matchrate-strict.mjs`'s alignment can never run — but those anchors correct *drift* between two independent device clocks, and a box pair shares one NTP daemon while the ring has no clock at all, leaving a **constant δ** that leave-one-block-out absorbs by construction (coupling answerable, absolute PAT not; δ-drift untested). **Result: NEGATIVE** — 29 681 beats, legacy 31 %/19 % (ratio 1.62, p 0.022) but **strict 7 %/7 % (ratio 0.96, p 0.87)**, indistinguishable from chance under the statistic that can fail. **The control exonerates the ring:** `PAT-UNDER-PERBLOCK-ALIGNMENT` §3a's six Verity nights, run WITH the real ACC alignment, score strict 5–9 % with four of six below chance — statistically identical. **So the blocker is §3a's own open item** — legacy `matchRate` 24–42 % there vs 90–96 % in §2, a ~3× harness disagreement its author flags as blocking the coupling verdict; this pass **reproduced it rather than resolving it**, so no PAT result currently carries information, including this one. Recommendation: reconcile the harnesses first. **§4 resolves as a duplicate of §5.3** — the status frame's phase and the constant δ are one unknown; irrelevant to coupling, and for absolute PAT blocked on **hardware** (no motion channel) rather than analysis. | Brief *(PROPOSED)* |
| [`O2RING-LIVE-PPG-WAVEFORM-2026-07-17-BRIEF.md`](briefs/O2RING-LIVE-PPG-WAVEFORM-2026-07-17-BRIEF.md) | **Capture the O2Ring's live ~100 Hz PPG waveform** — the finger-site pleth we currently discard. Each live `cmd=0x04` reply is a 24-B status header (the 1 Hz SpO₂/HR we save) **plus a ~125 Hz raw PPG body** (confirmed by the OxyII reference *and* our own reassembler comment); the onboard `.dat` is **1 Hz only** (decoded: 36 000×3 B = 10 h), so the waveform lives **only** in live BLE traffic and is thrown away every second. **Phase 0** measured the frame on-device (`24+2+count` layout, gap-free); **Phase 1** decoded the body (`oxyii.parse_ppg`, single-channel u8, validated vs the paired ECG @49 bpm); **Phase 2** captures it to the existing PSL ppg layout (no new parser branch) with host-arrival back-timing. **Phase 3** monitor scope card SHIPPED; the **PPGDex round-trip acceptance** and the honest **single-channel** path it depends on are the two remaining Done-when items, both owned by `PPGDEX-O2RING-FINGER-SITE`. Rate **calibrated to 125.738 Hz** (not the ~100 Hz assumed at scoping); layout now recorded in `O2RING-PROTOCOL` §3b. ⚠️ Until the single-channel path lands, capture writes the one value **replicated across ppg0/1/2**, so PpgDex reports a fabricated `ledAgreementPct: 100` at `measured` tier (`ENGINE-VERIFICATION-FINDINGS` §1.3). A second finger-site optical waveform beside the wrist Verity, one NTP clock. Out-of-suite (`capture-host/`); extends `O2RING-PROTOCOL`. **DONE 2026-07-20** — both remaining Done-when items met: the PPGDex round-trip PASSED on a real capture (`docs/O2RING-FINGER-ROUNDTRIP-2026-07-20.md`) and the honest single-channel path + `ppg1` one-column capture landed (PR #275/#276); the replication warning is closed. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-20)* |
| [`POLAR-OFFLINE-DOWNLOAD-2026-07-17-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POLAR-OFFLINE-DOWNLOAD-2026-07-17-BRIEF.md) | **Polar onboard offline-recording download (PS-FTP), wired into the Vigil monitor** — the Polar sibling of the O2Ring `.dat` puller; executes `POLAR-SDK-CAPTURE` Track A. Pulls the recording a Polar device stores in its own flash, over BLE, from the monitor's Devices view (**📥 Recordings** → list → Download). **USB is HID-only** (`0da4:0008` "Polar INW4J", `/dev/hidrawN`) — no mass storage, BLE is the only path. Protocol verbatim from the Polar BLE SDK: service `FEEE`, MTU char `FB005C51`, **RFC60** length prefix + **RFC76** air-packets (`bit0 next · bits1-2 MORE 0x06/LAST 0x02 · bits4-7 seq`), `PbPFtpOperation{GET,path}` → `PbPFtpDirectory`/raw file; hand-rolled protobuf, **GET-only**. Code: `polar_psftp.py` + `webmon.py` (`/api/polar/recordings`,`/api/polar/pull` — renamed on the vigil-merge; O2Ring kept `/api/pull`) + `monitor.html`. **Bonding is mandatory** (un-bonded read → `UNLIKELY_ERROR 14`). **Hardware finding:** the Verity stores only an **exercise `.BPB` session (HR series, ~55-62 bpm)**, **NO raw-PPG `.REC`** — raw PPG is live-PMD-only; pulled + byte-verified a 7-file session. **Caveat:** trusted-device BlueZ auto-reconnect races the web pull (standalone CLI reliable after `bluetoothctl disconnect`); pause capture + idle device before pulling. Follow-ups: `SAMPLES.BPB`→CSV decoder (**the one carrying the value**), web-pull robustness; **automate-the-button is RETIRED for PPG** (reconciled 2026-08-26 — the raw-PPG `.REC` needs ~300x the ~2 MB flash, and offline PPG is exclusive with live PPG) | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS — parked 2026-09-02: pull core shipped; `.BPB` decoder + web-pull demo both wait on a real H10 pull in the owner-scheduled probe window, Heron)* |
| [`POLAR-ONBOARD-BACKUP-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POLAR-ONBOARD-BACKUP-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Record on the Polar devices themselves, so a dropped BLE link stops being unrecoverable** — the Polar equivalent of the O2Ring `.dat`. Verity records **raw PPG offline** (firmware >= 2.1.0; this unit is 3.0.16) while **HR + GYRO stay live**, which the SDK's same-data-type exclusion forces and which is also what yields a continuous device-to-host clock map (HR carries no sensor timestamp; gyro does). H10's ceiling is **RR — never ECG** — and possibly only HR. **Corrects** `POLAR-OFFLINE-DOWNLOAD`'s "no raw-PPG offline recordings exist": that tested *exercise mode*, a different feature. The start op must ride `capture.py`'s existing BLE link or it drops the stream it protects. Phase 0 probe: `capture-host/probe_polar_onboard.py` (read-only — the start wire format is unestablished). Push alerting is a **precondition**: every failure mode here looks like a green box with a short file | Brief *(PROPOSED — parked 2026-09-02: gated entirely on §6 Q1, the idle-H10 recording-start probe (#2042 endpoint, Heron runs it); NO closes it, YES makes it one unit)* |
| [`POLAR-ONBOARD-BACKUP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POLAR-ONBOARD-BACKUP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md) | **What the 2026-08-10/11 hardware session surfaced — four fixes shipped, three items left, and one of them holds a safety setting DISABLED on the live box.** `power.drop_not_worn_sec = 0` on vigil until §2 lands: the optical worn detector's threshold (5,000) and worn cluster (129–207) were derived at **55 Hz** PPG, and at **176 Hz** the ambient channel reads ~650,800 with a 208-count spread — a pegged value, not a light level — so it reported `worn: False` for an armband showing a textbook pulse at 57 bpm. Two changes the same day, neither checked against the other: PPG default → 176 Hz in the morning, worn detection calibrated at 55 Hz in the evening; the afternoon measurement that appeared to validate it was taken on a genuinely unworn device and so agreed for the wrong reason. Fix order: **refuse rather than guess** (publish `worn = None` when the rate is not the calibrated one), then re-derive per rate, then prefer the PPI contact bit, then restore the drop. §3 — **SDK mode and PPI/HR are mutually exclusive** on a Verity (Polar's own product doc) and the monitor offers both as independent switches, so the config can express a state the hardware cannot hold; the UI must say so before the click. §4 — **no pull path reaches an H10 at all**: the charger trigger cannot fire (CR2025 coin cell, `charging` permanently false) and `autopull_poller` is hard-scoped to Wellue/Viatom, so recording into its single slot fills it once and then silently records nothing — retrieval before recording. §5, needing no code: **a fake written by reading the implementation encodes its bugs** — fixing `parse_status_response` exposed three fakes emitting a 3- or 4-byte control-point envelope where the wire uses 5, plus a fourth that IndexErrored on every parameterless op and had silently made SDK-mode status untestable; and **a gate you did not run reports nothing, which reads as success** — `pytest` without `--cov` emits no coverage table, so 3,264 passing tests looked green while the 100 % floor was never evaluated. Shipped alongside: #1155 (a control-point ERROR reply parsed as measurement state), #1161 (`ppg2w` missing from the offer set — not un-toggleable but DELETED by any save, 110 MB/night), #1165 (the SDK-mode switch was one-way), #1166 (`chosen_rate` answered "not offered" with `max(rates)` — ten hours of ACC at 416 Hz) | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS — parked 2026-09-02: §2 superseded, §3 executed, §4's endpoint landed in #2042 — the experiment on a strapped idle H10 is owner-scheduled box time, Heron)* |
| [`POLAR-PMD-COMMAND-SURFACE-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POLAR-PMD-COMMAND-SURFACE-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — every documented read-only PMD control-point op, as a real Verity Sense answers it**, recorded verbatim so the next developer does not re-derive the wire format from Kotlin. Companion to `POLAR-ONBOARD-BACKUP` §4a (which established the *offline* bit by reading the SDK); this one is measured on hardware (`0C301E3F`, sw `3.0.16`, fw `0.1.5`, model `INW4J`) via `capture-host/probe_pmd_surface.py`. Every line is marked **MEASURED** / **DOCUMENTED — UNTESTED** / **NOT TRIED — deliberate**. **(1) The clock is settable and lies about it.** The Verity exposes TWO clocks: the one it *answers about* (PS-FTP `GET_LOCAL_TIME`) and the one it *stamps samples with* (PMD `sensor_ns`). `SET_LOCAL_TIME(local civil, tz −240)` was **ACCEPTED**, `GET_LOCAL_TIME` echoed it back exactly — and the sample clock stayed **UTC, 14 415 s adrift**. So reading the clock back verifies nothing about the timestamps in the files you are about to write; only a sample stamp does. This is why `capture.py` puts every device on UTC with `tz_offset=0` — that decision is now measured, not inherited — and why an onboard recording decoded without the offset lands **4 h off and looks plausible**. **(2) SDK mode is a much larger device.** Op `0x04` reports what SDK mode WOULD offer without entering it: **PPG 28/44/55/135/176 Hz** (vs 55 only), **ACC/GYRO 26→416 Hz** with selectable ±2/4/8/16 G and ±250…2000 dps (vs one fixed range each). Offline recording stays capped at 13/26/52 Hz regardless — the ~2 MB flash budget showing through the protocol. Offered ≠ accepted; measuring it is a separate experiment. **(3) The bitmask mixes measurements and MODES.** It sets **eight** bits where Polar publishes five measurement types, because three are capability flags — **`0x09` SDK_MODE · `0x0D` OFFLINE_RECORDING · `0x0E` OFFLINE_HR**. The measured tell: a mode answers `invalid_meas` to a settings read and is absent from the status/trigger lists, while `OFFLINE_HR` — which names a real recordable data type — answers `ok` with an empty menu and appears in both. ⚠️ **§4 was CORRECTED 2026-08-03**: it first called these "undocumented measurement types" and floated skin temperature, when `webmon.py:606` already named them and a test already pinned the behaviour — measured hardware before searching the tree, and turned settled knowledge back into an open question. There is no temperature stream. Do NOT add them to `pmd.MEAS_NAME`: webmon filters capturable streams with `not startswith('0x')`, so naming them there offers three modes to the user as streams. **(4) The link goes deaf, intermittently** — three windows refused every write after 4–9 with GATT `UNLIKELY_ERROR`, a fourth completed all 36 on one link; an earlier draft asserting a hard 4–9 ceiling was disproved before it shipped. So sweeps must be **resumable** (flat plan + index, not nested loops) and ordered by scarcity — one run completed 23/28 commands and missed all four unique one-shot queries because the plentiful kind was asked first. **NOT TRIED on purpose:** trigger writes `0x08`/`0x09` (they persist across power cycles — an armed trigger records on every boot, eats the flash budget and removes the live stream), entering SDK mode, and every undocumented opcode; the probe enforces an **allowlist**, so an unknown opcode is assumed to write. Op `0x0A` answers `invalid_op` — our label for it is wrong or it does not exist on this firmware | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-02)* |
| [`POLAR-VERITY-DEVICE-SURFACE-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POLAR-VERITY-DEVICE-SURFACE-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — the whole Polar Verity Sense surface, reverse-engineered and hardware-validated**, in the shape of `O2RING-PROTOCOL`: GATT map · the complete PMD instruction set · SDK mode · offline recording · the device FILESYSTEM · the on-flash `.REC` format · timebase · security. **No reference implementation exists to check against** — polar-ble-sdk issue #556 ("Accessing offline recording using python") is OPEN AND UNANSWERED and `zHElEARN/polar-python` is streaming-only. **(1) The instruction set is COMPLETE: `0x01`-`0x09`, and `0x00`+`0x0A`-`0x3F` are all `invalid_op`** — swept by sending each opcode ALONE so existence follows from the status code without executing anything; device state byte-identical before and after. **(2) `.REC` needs NO new decoder** — 17-byte header · ASCII UTC stamp · the PMD settings TLVs verbatim from the START · then PMD frames byte-identical to the live link, in 281-byte records = `10 hdr + 269 payload + 2 trailing`. That 2-byte tail is the trap: slicing to the next frame's offset feeds it to the delta decoder and discards all 52 good samples (52 = exactly 944 ms x 55 Hz, which is what showed the BOUNDARY was wrong). 300 frames -> 15,580 samples, 55.08 Hz, zero warnings. **(3) The timebase is UTC** (header stamp matched host UTC to **-0.3 s**) while the Clock Contract stores floating LOCAL — and `polar_psftp` writes that field as **`start_local`**, which it is not. **(4) An offline START must be built from the OFFLINE menu**: with SDK mode on, ACC online offers 416 Hz, offline permits only 13/26/52, and the device answers `invalid_sample_rate` — so any implementation reusing the live negotiation breaks silently the moment SDK mode is enabled. **(5) ⚠️ SECURITY — a device mirror is sensitive twice over:** `/U/0/USERID.BPB` holds the owner's real name and Polar account UUID, and `/SYS/BT/<n>/BTDEV.BPB` is the **bonding table**, one directory per paired host each carrying that peer's address and a **128-bit key** — so anything reaching PS-FTP can read pairing secrets for EVERY bonded host. `polar_mirror.py --redact` blanks both; no device file content belongs in the repo. **(6) There is no temperature sensor** (no `0x1809`/`0x2A6E`; Polar's table lists only HR/PPG/PPI/ACC/GYRO/MAG), and the HR service **emits 1 Hz but always `0000` with `contact_supported: false`** — so `worn` is underivable and the not-worn battery drop can never fire. **(7) An 8th, undocumented service `6217ff4b-…`** (also on H7/H10, purpose publicly unknown) is present and untouched. **Operational:** ONE BLE link and the daemon usually has it (`link_guard`, 5 runs lost) · **`Trusted: no` breaks PS-FTP with `UNLIKELY_ERROR` while reporting Bonded: yes** · use `async with BleakClient` · **mirror once, analyse locally**. §12 records five method errors including *"USB reads what BLE refuses"*, which was wrong — it was the trust bit. **Open:** the on-body recording that would validate the path physiologically (every test was made in the dock, so PPGDex correctly reports no pulse) · a possible ~259 s / 77,434 B auto-stop that would sink night-long backup · whether `in_charger` tracks USB attachment or charge level | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-03)* |
| [`VERITY-OFFLINE-VS-STREAMING-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`](briefs/VERITY-OFFLINE-VS-STREAMING-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — can the Verity record PPG to its own flash all night while the box streams only ACC? No: under two hours, and it stops silently.** Answers `POLAR-ONBOARD-BACKUP` §6 Q2 from `.REC` files this project already pulled — **5.407 B/sample** for 4-channel 22-bit PPG (three files, 55 Hz, stable to 0.002), so **297 B/s → 1.96 h** against the ~2 MB flash ceiling, **1.53 h** with the 459 KB already resident and never erased, and as little as **17 min** if the SDK's 300 KB–2 MB auto-stop fires — which stops the recording *without an error*, in the one configuration that has no live PPG left to detect the stall with. **Polar's advertised "600 h of recording" CONFIRMS the budget rather than contradicting it** — 600 h × 3600 s × 1 Hz × 1 B/sample = **2.16 MB**, i.e. the same ~2 MB ceiling described from the other end, and 600 h ÷ 1.96 h = 306× against PPG's 297 B/s ÷ 1 B/s = 297×, two unrelated sources agreeing to 3 %. It is a **1 Hz HR** figure, and the device says so: `0x0E OFFLINE_HR` is its own measurement type with an empty settings menu. **Closes one open question in `POLAR-VERITY-DEVICE-SURFACE`:** the suspected 77 434 B auto-stop is **falsified** by an 84 235 B recording off the same device the same day. **Refutes the proposal's premise separately:** over 4.19 h last night the Verity's PPG/ACC/GYRO and the H10's ECG had **zero** inter-sample gaps > 3× nominal, concurrently — what broke both sessions at 04:45 was a *daemon restart* named in the journal, not the radio. **Supplies the consumer analysis `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH` §5's GYRO decision was missing:** GYRO is not unused — `ppgdex-dsp.js analyzeMotion` takes `max(accDyn/120, gyroMag/40)` per 0.25 s cell and **GYRO is the max in 99.18 % of them**, so turning it off halves the mean motion index (0.110 → 0.044) and drops 198 cells from the rejected band. But the dominance is a **pedestal, not sensitivity** — `gyroMag` p1→p90 spans 3.47→3.98 dps, a flat zero-rate floor normalising to 0.096 against `accDyn`'s 0.012 — so the trade is **81.4 MB of 254.4 (32 %) to change 0.33 % of gating decisions**, and dropping it is an improvement whose predicted, falsifiable consequence is `meanMotionIndex` landing near 0.044 on the first night. MAG stays additive-only by the source's own words (*does not alter beat SQI*). **176 Hz is not a setting today:** `capture.py` never enters SDK mode (it appears only in the probes), the device's menu is `[55]` in the box journal, and `chosen_rate` honours an unsupported `prefer` by **silently** falling back — so `rates: {ppg: 176}` would yield 55 Hz and no error. Measured: 176 ran on 08-02/03 only. **And the USB pipe was reporting a truncated listing as a complete one** — one 64-byte report, flagged END, cut mid-record, decoding `20260802/` into a file named `"20"` and losing `20260803/` (all 22 `.REC`s) entirely, because a Python slice past the end returns the short remainder. The same reader serves the BLE mirror. Fixed + gated | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-09)* |
| [`PPG-SAMPLE-RATE-AND-PAT-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PPG-SAMPLE-RATE-AND-PAT-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — what PPG sample rate actually buys, measured by decimating ONE night.** SDK mode raised the Verity's PPG ceiling 55 -> 176 Hz and it shipped on the strength of a quantisation argument that was **wrong**: `ppgdex-dsp.js:942` (`refineFeet`) already interpolates each systolic foot to a FRACTIONAL sample index, so beat times were never on the sample grid. **rMSSD is invariant 44 -> 176 Hz** (37.8/37.8/37.7/37.8, identical beat count and mean RR); per-epoch HR error stays 0.2-0.3 bpm. **PAT residIQR is flat from 176 Hz down to 25 Hz (~18.6 ms) then falls off a CLIFF at 22 Hz (40.4 ms)** and detection collapses entirely at 11 Hz — measured with the repo's own `coupledPAT` / `pat-gate.js`, not a hand-rolled pairing. **The extra rate IS used, it is swamped:** solving `total^2 = phys^2 + sampling^2` against the 18.6 ms physiological floor gives a sampling term of 35.8 -> 3.7 -> 1.7 ms at 22/44/176 Hz, so 44 -> 176 Hz moves the total 0.28 ms (**1.5 %**) for **1.81x the battery** (4.74 %/h at 55 Hz vs 8.60 %/h at 176 Hz, measured from `LINK.csv` on consecutive nights; 21.1 h vs 11.6 h runtime). **Verdict: floor 25 Hz, recommended 44-55 Hz, nothing above.** Do not sit at 25 — the cliff moves UP with heart rate (this night rested at ~52 bpm). **Sample rate is not what blocks PAT:** every rate 25-176 Hz clears the 60 ms precision bar and the gate still fails on **matchRate 28.5 % vs >=55 %**, caused by **430 ms of drift** (~109 ppm) walking the lag out of the pairing window — a 2x shortfall against a 1.5 % one. §6 records five method errors that each produced a confident wrong answer with no error message (cross-night comparison confounded by physiology · epochs paired by ARRAY INDEX when the nodes have different counts and start times · a hand-rolled PAT pairing that inflated jitter 18.6 -> 24.3 ms by using one channel, `idx/fs` timing and nearest-R-peak matching, all three already solved in `pat-feasibility-worker.js` · **a flat result reported without a positive control** — it took the owner's disbelief, not any gate, to force the 22/11/5.5 Hz ladder that turned it into a quantified saturation curve). **Corrects** `POLAR-PMD-COMMAND-SURFACE` §2.2a | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-03)* |
| [`CLOCK-MUTATION-AUDIT-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CLOCK-MUTATION-AUDIT-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md) | **The Clock Contract is the least-tested module in the suite — 41 % of mutations go unnoticed.** `CLAUDE.md` calls it *non-negotiable*; an EXHAUSTIVE `tools/mutate.mjs` run (all 81 mutants, 40 min) killed **31**, leaving 50 survivors of which 6 were comment noise → **31/75 = 41 %**. Against the 71-file roster sweep: `pulsedex-dsp`/`dex-ingest`/`hrvdex-dsp` 100 %, `oxydex-dsp` 91 %, `ppgdex-dsp` 83 %, `integrator-dsp` 58 %, `ecgdex-dsp` 50 %, **`clock.js` last**. **The mechanism is uncomfortable:** clock is also the most EXPENSIVE module to test (191 s per `--group=clock` run, sixteen heavy groups, loaded by everything) — expensive-to-test correlates with under-tested, which is backwards for a spine, and no coverage report can show it because clock's line coverage is near-total by construction. **Two survivors are DOCUMENTED invariants:** §2.7's component-range validation (the guard that exists because *`Date.UTC`'s silent roll is a fabricated instant*) has **seven** surviving mutants on one line — every month/day boundary plus three `&&`→`||` rewrites, so nothing checks that month 13 or day 32 is refused; and §3's DMY/MDY *day-component > 12* rule survives `>`→`>=`, so nothing distinguishes 12 from 13 at the exact boundary the rule is written around. Plus §2.1's epoch-plausibility band and the §2.5 midnight-roll loop. **This does NOT say clock.js is buggy** — every survivor is a statement about the SUITE. **A tool defect surfaced too:** 6 survivors mutate a comment, because `codeMask()` desyncs at `clock.js:81`'s regex literal containing quote characters; raw 38 % and adjusted 41 % are both reported so the correction is visible. **§5 items 1-2 EXECUTED 2026-08-02 (§7).** The mask is now regex-aware — and the correction is bigger than six comment survivors: mutant generation moved **81 → 123**, because the desync had also been marking real code as *string* and SUPPRESSING legitimate mutants. The published 38 % rested on a population wrong in both directions, so the re-run is the number that counts, not a patch to the old one. Added `--dry-run` (list mutants, run nothing) so proving *no mutant lands in a comment* need not cost 40 min of suite execution. **The three guards are gated** (19 assertions): §2.1's epoch band and §3's locked-order contradiction, each asserted as a rejection AND its adjacent boundary — a one-sided test kills the `||`→`&&` mutant and leaves every `<`→`<=` alive, which is exactly the state the audit found. All seven survivors re-applied and confirmed killed; the sharpest is §3's *day-component > 12* rule, where the mutant lives entirely in the gap between 12 (ambiguous) and 13 (decisive). **Correction:** this brief first called `clock.js:56` "§2.7's component-range validation" — wrong; §2.7's validator is `_ckMk` and IS gated. Line 56 is §3's file-level lock. **§6 item 4 CLOSED 2026-08-02 (§7.4) — the two zero-kill modules were neither untested nor mis-tagged.** Both had a real, correctly-tagged group aimed at a *sliver*: `cohort-regression.js`'s 20 assertions cover `olsR2`, which is **5 of the file's 65 mutants**; `cpapdex-render.js`'s §RN group drives `renderReviewView`, which contains **zero** — while **242 of its 319 mutants (76 %) sat inside functions the module was ALREADY exporting**. So the real third explanation is *a real test, correctly tagged, covering a sliver, with a tag that makes the whole file look gated* — invisible to a tag sweep (a tag records which module a group is about, not how much it reaches) and invisible to line coverage (`olsR2` is 100 %-covered, and so is the 8 % of the file it represents). Now **11/65** and **61/319** exhaustive; `cpapdex-render` needed **no source change at all**. **Mutation found two of my own assertions wrong:** an absent-metric test written with `undefined` survives deleting `fnum`'s `v == null` half (`isFinite(undefined)` is false) — only an explicit `null` exposes it, and `isFinite(null)` is **true**, so a missing AHI renders **`0.00` graded `ok`**; and a one-to-one recall case that coincidentally still reads 0.5 when the inner `break` is deleted (the exposing case returns a recall of **2.0**). **Honest ceiling:** 108 of the 258 remaining CPAPDex survivors are canvas/DOM code a headless HTML-string harness cannot reach; **150 are pure HTML builders still unasserted** — 19 % is a floor, not a finish line. **§7.5 WAVE 2 + a CORRECTION 2026-08-02 — §7.4's ceiling understated the reachable surface by a third.** It bucketed `renderHistory` (29 survivors) and `renderNight` as DOM-mutating; both are **pure** (each returns an HTML string), so the honest split of those 258 survivors is 69 canvas · **7** DOM-only · **182** reachable, not 150/108. The consequence is concrete: `renderNight` is exported and pure and is the ONLY route to `heroCard` (25 survivors — the largest single function and the node's headline clinical verdict), which §7.4 had written off. Wave 2: **61/319 → 118/319 = 36 %**, still test-only, no source change, no re-bundle. It pins heroCard's four tiers on both sides of both edges (each tier carries a DIFFERENT clinical instruction — a widened band tells the reader to do the opposite thing), the compliance chip's independent second copy of the 4 h rule, oximetryCard's two-polarity grid (odi/t90 lower-better beside nadir/mean higher-better in one row), the honest-absence shield, and cpapEventTimeline's chronology + apnea-class naming. **Mutation found two more of my own assertions wrong:** a 40-row-cap test written with an all-apnea fixture still counts 30 when the `|| hypopnea` half is deleted, and an off-mask-gap test that counts rows cannot see `afterIdx === i` become `!== i` (same row count, wrong session). That is **four wrong-but-passing assertions across two waves, all found the same way** — an assertion written from reading the code encodes the code's shape rather than its contract, and the cheap correction is to re-apply the mutant before believing it. Remaining: 201 survivors — 69 canvas, 7 DOM-only, **125 still reachable**, the biggest being `renderHistory` (29) and `cpapClinicalSummary` (20). **§7.6 CLOSED 2026-08-02 — the exhaustive re-run is 86/123 = 70 %, and the "void" number was recoverable.** All FIVE documented-guard lines now have **zero** survivors (§2.1's epoch band `:37-38`, §3's lock `:56`, §3's *day > 12* rule `:59`, §2.7's `_ckMk` `:113`) — the direct measurement §7.2 could only hand-verify. **And there IS a valid before/after pair, though not the one this brief kept refusing to draw:** §7.3 called 73/123 "void", which was too strong — it measured `HEAD`, i.e. the tree WITHOUT the then-uncommitted guards, so it is the right answer to *how the suite did BEFORE them*. Every confounder was checked rather than assumed: `clock.js` is **byte-identical** (`d1bfb75`) at `6b65e02^`/`6b65e02`/`89cc76b`/`origin/main` (the guards landed as TESTS, not code, so it is the same 123 mutants, not merely the same count); the `clock` tag moved by exactly the two guard groups (17 → 19, and #717's retag sweep added none); both runs used the regex-aware mask. So on an unchanged `clock.js` and an identical population, differing by exactly 19 assertions: **59 % → 70 %, +13 killed** — and 13 > 7 because asserting a rejection AND its adjacent boundary closes mutants either side of a cut. **41 % stays outside this comparison** (81 mutants from the desynchronised mask; no arithmetic recovers a like-for-like leg). The honest ladder: *41 % on a broken population · 59 % → 70 % on a sound one.* **Remaining 37 survivors: 20 are the host-axis fit** (`:260-370`), now the module's weakest region — and UNSPECIFIED: the Contract documents the parser, not the axis, so several are loop bounds and defaults whose correct behaviour is genuinely unstated. Spec first, then gate → follow-up. | Brief *(**DONE** 2026-08-02)* |
| [`CLOCK-MUTATION-COST-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CLOCK-MUTATION-COST-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **The clock.js mutation run costs ~16 h, and the Python fix does not transfer.** ⚠ The ~16 h is SEQUENTIAL — the tool already runs 16 workers and the real wall-clock is ~40 min; narrowing measured and REFUSED (tag-only loses the biggest killer). Measured 2026-08-03: one `--group=clock` run takes **7 m 49 s** (41 groups, 609 assertions) and `tools/mutate.mjs` pays it **per mutant** — ~16 h for clock.js's 123. `capture.py`'s 36-day figure came from mutmut recompiling a 100 MB generated module per mutant and was fixed by persisting the bytecode cache and reusing the scratch; **neither applies here** — `mutate.mjs` patches in place, so the cost IS the test selection. **The obvious in-process fix is UNSOUND and would fail silently:** five DSPs bind `parseTimestamp = DexClock.parseTimestamp` at load time (the delegation pattern `CLAUDE.md` §✅ documents), so a post-load swap is invisible to all of them and every mutant they would have killed reports as *survived* — a false negative claiming "nothing tests this" about thoroughly-tested code. **Proposal:** record which group kills each known-killed mutant, take the union as the minimal sufficient selection, and gate the change on re-running the exhaustive 123 under it and getting the **same 86 killed** — a faster measurement that changes the answer is not a faster measurement. Explicitly out of scope: in-process swapping, and lowering the 70 % baseline. Matters because clock.js is the Clock Contract (*non-negotiable*) and the suite's least-tested module, and a 16-hour measurement is one nobody will run. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-03 2026-08-03)* |
| [`MUTATION-AUDIT-RUNBOOK-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-AUDIT-RUNBOOK-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md) | **How to run a mutation audit — the procedure, after five modules and ~20 measured runs.** The findings live in `audits/MUTATION-AUDIT-FINDINGS-2026-08-02.md`; this is the method, and it exists because most of the time went into failure modes that do not look like failures. **§1 — six ways a run fails while looking fine:** a mid-run `mutmut results` and a poisoned baseline BOTH read as *not checked*; a signal-killed run reports `rc: -15` with `timed_out: false` (check `rc`, never the flag); a source-scanning test reports *failed to collect stats*. Plus how to wait on a run without `pkill -f` killing your own shell (exit 144, three times). **Two added 2026-08-04, and both corrupt the METHOD rather than the code:** a **stale `.pyc`** — on a coarse-mtime volume a mutate→test→restore inside one timestamp bucket reuses the MUTANT's bytecode against restored source, and `git status`, `git diff` and `inspect.getsource` all read clean (it re-reads the FILE by line number, not the code object), so it silently corrupted a negative-control matrix in BOTH directions; and **editing the source while a run is in flight**, which makes the scratch a moving target — compare the run's start time against the file's mtime, and kill rather than wait out a result you cannot attribute. **§2 — the method:** re-measure BEFORE triaging (mutant IDs are per-function and positional, so a stale ID still *resolves*, to a different mutation); predict the equivalents first; confirm each kill BY ID, since a total that moved proves nothing; diff survivor SETS with `LC_ALL=C comm`, and read the survivor/timeout split separately — a *new survivor* is usually a timeout, because mutmut's per-mutant budget derives from the baseline and does not grow with the selection. **§3 —** mutant files reach 113 MB / 1.9 M lines, so `ast.parse` never finishes; diff by line-scanning `def x_<fn>__mutmut_N(` headers. **§4 — every module is heavy for a DIFFERENT reason** (`capture.py` compile · `webmon.py` test time · `clock.js` group selection at 7 m 49 s/mutant); measure where the second goes before optimising. Records the two `mutate.py` fixes so nobody reverts them — the bytecode cache (36 days → 18 s) and hash-keyed scratch reuse with pruning (`/tmp` is tmpfs; 153 orphans reached 2.6 GB). **§5 — never read module source raw from a test:** `capture.py` had TWELVE such scans, which is most of why it sat at 1 % measured; use `tests/_srcscan.module_source()`, gate-backed, and NOT the per-file `SOURCE_SCANNING_TESTS` exclusion, which deletes the real unit tests beside the scan and turns their mutants into fake survivors. **§6 — pick by shape:** pure predicates first; a giant function is not a unit of work (`make_app` holds 386 of 395 survivors — the unit is a route). **§7 — "done" is NOT 100 %:** each module has a ceiling set by its prose-and-equivalent fraction (measured 89-94 %), equivalent mutants are impossible rather than hard, and pinning prose turns the suite into a change-detector. **§8 — the finding this method keeps producing:** a double that accepts an argument and discards it makes the code computing it unobservable while coverage reads 100 %. | Brief *(REFERENCE 2026-08-03)* |
| [`CAPTURE-HOST-SUBPROCESS-SURFACE-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CAPTURE-HOST-SUBPROCESS-SURFACE-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **The mutation campaign's cheap half is done; what remains is one shape.** Four work-units (#805, #823, #828, #852) killed **65 mutants**, all confirmed by ID, all in PURE functions — argv parsing, scheduling boundaries, size arithmetic. The residue is not more of the same: **77 mutants in eight functions**, every one of which reaches the outside world through exactly TWO call sites — `cpap_harvest._sh` (`subprocess.run`) and `storage_targets._run` (`asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`). Measured after the campaign, not read off the stale audit lists (which drifted — `storage_targets` 209→202, `cpap_harvest` 282→280): `cpap_harvest` 262 survivors of which **122 are log prose**, `storage_targets` 192 of which 130 are. **The fixture pattern already exists and was never driven through the clusters** — `test_cpap_wifi_commands.py` records every call and asserts `capture_output`/`text`/`timeout`, which is exactly what a discarding double hides (the §8 finding: #823's `main()` handed `pull()` seven arguments, the test asserted two, and 33 mutants lived behind the five it threw away). `storage_targets._run` has no equivalent. These are not bookkeeping paths: `_wpa_up` is the `sudo -n` bring-up whose 2026-07-28 failure skipped a night of therapy data one HTTP GET away, and `_sh` never raises, so a mutant in its `FileNotFoundError`→127 mapping turns a missing binary into a silent success. **§8 states the deliverable is the eight functions being observable, NOT a percentage** — driving the rate up would mean asserting log text, which pins wording and reds the build on every message edit. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-04)* |
| [`CAPTURE-HOST-SUBPROCESS-SURFACE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CAPTURE-HOST-SUBPROCESS-SURFACE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **Executing the subprocess brief found nothing wrong with capture-host — every serious problem was in the MEASURING INSTRUMENT.** 209 mutants killed across 8 PRs, zero regressions (2770 measured; 76.0 % -> 83.4 %); the brief's 77-mutant scope returned 148. **§1 — the leverage was one design choice:** the recording double REQUIRES the keyword arguments production passes, with no defaults, because the real `subprocess.run`/`create_subprocess_exec` default them (uncaptured output, bytes, no deadline, streams inherited) and every assertion about the RETURN VALUE still passes; four mutants are visible only because the double refuses the call. **§2 — a seventh runbook failure mode:** `s.replace(old,new,1)` on an anchor matching TWO functions silently mutates the wrong one — three mutants read as survived while the control measured code the tests never covered, and once the anchor was made unique two of them were REAL gaps the wrong instrument had hidden. **§3 — a slow test spends OTHER mutants' budget:** one 5.18 s real-clock wait pushed three unrelated `wifi_up` mutants from KILLED to TIMEOUT; a fake clock took the file to 0.04 s and all three came back. **§4 —** widen "don't edit during a run" to *nothing that reads the tree may overlap anything that writes it* (a coverage gate ran while the negative control rewrote the module under it), and note the pre-flight check cannot use a bare `pgrep` because its own cmdline matches. **§5 —** "read both gates" failed twice in the same position (coverage 100 %, ruff red on the next line); proposes a ruff-only pre-commit hook, with a warning to test it against a real release first because the last hook proposed here would have blocked every one. **§6 —** both modules are at their useful ceiling (`cpap_harvest` 280→177, `storage_targets` 202→138, remainder mostly prose); only `harvest` (21) is left. **§7 —** the equivalents, each established by searching for a witness, so nobody re-derives them. | Brief *(DONE — two of three §8 items DONE 2026-08-04: §2/§3 transferred into the runbook (now "**Seven** ways a run fails while looking fine") and §4's in-flight rule widened there to "nothing that READS the tree may overlap anything that WRITES it"; §5's pre-commit hook **declined** — a hook nobody installs is a gate that does not gate — in favour of `capture-host/check.sh`, an aggregate running ruff·shellcheck·pytest that continues past a failure and verdicts from the collected exit codes (5 mutants killed incl. `set -e`; owned by `test_check_script.py`). §6's 21-mutant `harvest` cluster deliberately LEFT — a fresh campaign, not this one's residue)* |
| [`CAPTURE-HOST-MUTATION-FLEET-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CAPTURE-HOST-MUTATION-FLEET-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **(DONE 2026-08-09 — folded into `MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`; §4.1's prize was executed and the rest DECLINED in writing by `RUN-POLAR-MUTATION-STOP-HERE`.)** All 19 measured modules ranked, and the thing that actually predicts a cheap pass: CONCENTRATION, not module history and not how much is left.** `--rank` over the fleet finds **~1,150 reachable** — not the 149 an earlier note implied, which covered only four modules. **§3 — the prize:** `capture.run_polar` holds **502 reachable at 100 % concentration**, 44 % of the entire fleet remainder in ONE function. Earlier sessions measured `capture.py` at 45 % and treated it as intractable because of its SIZE (3,600 lines, 26-minute run, 109 test files); concentration says the opposite — one function, one fixture family. Never attempted; needs its own brief. **§2 — the correction:** after 34→13→14 on already-worked modules it looked like returns had flattened to ~13/pass; never-measured modules then gave 9→11→10→15→40, because `clockcfg` had 27 reachable in one undriven function. The flattening was WITHIN a module. Share ≥60 % = one fixture takes most of it; ≤30 % = scattered, and `link_rssi` at 29 % returned **1 mutant for 3 tests** — the measured proof. **§5 —** the ceiling is NOT 100 %: `flush=`/`XX`-wrapping/case-flips are killable only by asserting exact wording, so the target is zero REACHABLE and a kill-rate goal quoted without its ceiling may be unattainable. **§6 — every serious failure was in the INSTRUMENT, not the code:** a stale `.pyc` running a reverted mutant while git read clean; a non-unique anchor mutating the wrong function (hiding two real gaps); a 5.18 s test spending other mutants' timeout budget; a survivor-set diff undercounting kills by 5; a truncated run counting as a PERFECT score, caught only by the progress file saying `generating` not `FINISHED`. **§7 open:** the `/EcgNightly` corpus is not at that path locally or on vigil, and real frames would legitimately inform `oxyii.parse_live` + `polar_pmd.decode_frame` without breaking the hermetic suite.  ⚠️ Concentration predicts what a pass **costs**, not what it is **worth** — the most valuable `run_polar` family was the SMALLEST (9 mutants, a 4 h 25 m silent freeze). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-09)* |
| [`RUN-POLAR-MUTATION-PASS-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md`](briefs/RUN-POLAR-MUTATION-PASS-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md) | **The fleet brief's §4.1 prize, attempted — and what blocked it was never the size.** `capture.py` was called intractable for its 4,420 lines and 26-minute run. The real blocker is one line of tooling: `mutate.py`'s test heuristic picks files that MENTION the module, and `capture.py` is imported almost everywhere, so it selected 110 of them — a **680 s** baseline that mutmut then pays per mutant. The ten files that actually reach `run_polar` run in **5.83 s**: **117×**, and the difference between a pass that runs and one that cannot start. The trade points the safe way (a mutant killed only outside the scope reads SURVIVED — a false alarm, never a blind spot). **§2 measurement:** 1,286 mutants — 586 killed, 655 survived, 45 timeout; **560 REACHABLE · 128 UNOBSERVABLE · 12 PROSE, ceiling 90.0 %**. Timeouts are NOT gaps and are carried separately. **The mutant-name form cost a 978 s run:** `x__run_polar__*` matches nothing — mutmut writes `x` + the function name verbatim, so `_now`→`x__now__` but `run_polar`→`x_run_polar__`. mutmut's `nothing matches` assertion surfaces as `rc=1` with a truncated traceback and a results dump reading `not checked` for everything — **indistinguishable from a poisoned mid-run read**. `--list` before `--only`. **§3 the first family:** 69 `BUS.register`/`push`/`unregister` argument-drop mutants, all surviving because `test_telemetry.py` exercises the bus directly and `test_capture_runners.py` asserts on FILES — the live contract sat between them, and neither defect this path has shipped (the 2026-08-05 vendor-rate registration that painted ACC amber all night; the missing `bpm` card) reaches a file. **§3.1:** the existing guard is a SOURCE SCAN whose docstring says a behavioural test *"would need the device"* — false, `FlexPolarClient` negotiates all six streams with none, and a scan cannot tell `0` from `1` (mutant 312 survives it). **66 killed, 3 proven EQUIVALENT** (the `.get()` default is unreachable: `writers` is keyed only by `meas_of.values()` ≡ `MEAS_NAME`'s keys). **§4 two corrections measurement forced:** the surviving `hz` mutants are NOT the false-amber defect (`stream_health` reads the REGISTERED nominal) but a wrong live-SSE rate and ring size; and 18 survivors were simply a callback the driver never entered. **§5:** `_set()`'s 66 are the same shape and the obvious next unit; the ~150 `log.*` argument survivors need a kill-or-reclassify decision or they overstate the remaining work by a quarter. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS 2026-08-08)* |
| [`RUN-POLAR-MUTATION-STOP-HERE-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`](briefs/RUN-POLAR-MUTATION-STOP-HERE-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md) | **Four families closed on `capture.run_polar`, and the argued case for STOPPING — with the cost data, because "we ran out of appetite" is not a reason a later reader can check.** Bought: 145 mutants, **130 killed, 15 proven equivalent, 46 tests** — BUS 66/69 (every monitor card's identity + the two-phase negotiated rate), `_set` 43/45 (`status.json`, `monitor.html`, everything `alerts.py` reads), writers 12/22 (PSL column order in the files that ARE the night), bounded awaits 9/9. **§6 — the predictor of WORTH is a NAMED INCIDENT, not a count, and it disagrees with concentration:** the most valuable family was the SMALLEST (9 mutants, 7 tests, guarding the 2026-07-25 freeze — 4 h 25 m lost behind a green card, link up, zero bytes, no stall warning because the watchdog is downstream of what was stuck). Every family worth closing had an incident in the code's own comments; the ones without are the ones declined. **§4 — the rule worth reusing:** a mutant killable only by asserting a TUNED constant (`backoff*2`, `60`→`61`) is not a defect the suite should own — pin the behaviour (backoff grows, it is capped, a stall reconnect beats an error backoff) and let the number move; same trade as `flush=`, `XX`-wrapping and message wording. **§5 — judge before buying:** 13 loop-condition timeouts may be artefacts of `_stop_after` patching `asyncio.sleep`, and NOTHING is written for them until one unpatched run says which. **§7 — the instrument changes outlive the pass**, each having already produced a confident wrong answer: a per-mutant timeout (a hang is its own verdict, not a kill — one mutant burned 79 min of CPU unnoticed), restore in `finally` (a killed run left `capture.py` mutated on disk), a baseline guard (**264/264 "killed" with nothing collected**), a verified line map (text anchors silently skipped 17 of 45 and reported 13/45 as measured), and a progress line. | Brief *(**DONE — 2026-08-15**, all four Done-when items. **§5 MEASURED and its own hypothesis REFUTED:** it guessed the 13 loop-condition timeouts were artefacts of `_stop_after` patching `asyncio.sleep` to a no-op — but those loops DO await a real sleep. Running the real `run_polar` under the REAL sleep with a real deadline, one mutant at a time: the hold loop HUNG and exited only once the link dropped; the pause loop EXITED — and HUNG when the pause was HELD; the unmutated control exited in every arm. **So the operator is not what decides it: such a mutant is real exactly when its SIBLING condition can still be true at shutdown**, because `or` makes `_STOP` unreachable — and for the hold loop that sibling is `client.is_connected`, cleared only by the `finally` AFTER the loop, so nothing inside the process can end it. ⚠️ **One site would have produced either answer** (the first alone says real, the second alone says artefact), which is this family's recurring error, so the second was measured before anything was written and it turned a verdict into a rule. Two tests now gate it at **0.56 s** — §5 expected them to be expensive — using a real sleep deliberately (under `_stop_after` they would pass AGAINST the mutant) and `_run_bounded`, so a regression fails at 6 s instead of hanging the suite; each mutant is killed by its own test and only its own. The exact 13 IDs are unrecoverable (sweep state lived in `/tmp`, lost to the 2026-08-14 reboot) — the mechanism is what they would have been judged by. **§3 `reconnect / bonding` DECLINED on journal evidence** — 3 `re-pair` lines, all on 2026-07-30 (the tail of the 2026-07-29 episode that cost 4.5 h of ECG), 0 escalations in the 16 days since — declined REVERSIBLY with the weakness stated (16 quiet days is weak evidence about a rare failure, and the recovery path shipped because of that incident, so the quiet may be the fix working) and the trigger written down: a second incident reopens it. **§4's rule is in `MUTATION-AUDIT-RUNBOOK` §7** — *a mutant killable only by asserting a tuned constant is not the suite's to own; pin the behaviour and let the number move* — with the boundary it needed: TUNED constants only, not a wire format or a vendor spec. **The declines are recorded per family in `CAPTURE-HOST-MUTATION-FLEET` §7.8**, in that file rather than by reference, so `run_polar` cannot be re-opened as untouched.)* |
| [`MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md) | **(IN-PROGRESS)** The one mutation brief — folds `MUTATION-EQUIVALENCE`, `CAPTURE-HOST-MUTATION-FLEET`, `JS-DSP-MUTATION-FLEET` and `PPGDEX-TESTABLE-SURFACE`, which had begun to contradict each other. **§1 settled:** the target is 90 % of DISTINGUISHABLE mutants, owner-ratified 2026-08-08, and BOTH gates now carry the classification mechanism (JS #1060, Python #1102) with REFUTED/ORPHANED/UNCLASSIFIED loud. **§2 — the finding that reorders the work:** that mechanism is fed by **one file**. `tools/mutate-equivalence.json` holds `clock.js` and nothing else while **~83 battery-measured classifications sit in prose**, so the ratified target is currently unmeasurable anywhere except `clock.js` — and the batteries that produced them were never committed, so the only prober in the repo is `clock.js`-specific. **§3 cost:** concentration (Python, ~1,150 reachable over 19 modules) and tag price (JS, a **300× spread**, 1 s–310 s, splitting the fleet into sweepable vs diff-gate-only). **§4 worth:** a NAMED INCIDENT, not a count — `run_polar`'s most valuable family was its smallest (9 mutants, 7 tests, a 4 h 25 m silent freeze), and those mutants HANG rather than fail so every prior sweep was blind to them. **§5 conversion:** the equivalent-mutant share is a property of what a function DOES — validation/dispatch **77 %**, numeric/spectral 29 %, parsing 26 % — which withdraws PpgDex's 52 % ceiling and means both fleet maps rank the wrong unit. **§6** standing declines, each with evidence (tuned constants, wording, `flush=`, absorbing numeric interiors, ≤30 %-concentration tails). **§2a — found by searching the DISK, not the repo:** two machine-readable result sets sit un-triaged and gitignored-by-design — `.mutation-crawl/` holds FULL sweeps of `hrvdex-dsp.js` (**191/489 = 39.1 %, canary PASSED** — the fleet's first canary-guarded DSP sweep, confirming #1030) and `motiondex-dsp.js` (37.3 %, **canary NONE**), each with its complete survivor list in exactly the shape the prober reads and `probed: 0`; and `/home/michal/tepna-mutation-audit-2026-08-02/` holds 19 capture-host modules' survivor IDs. **§7.7 — the corpus question is ANSWERED and it was a PATH:** `CAPTURE-HOST-MUTATION-FLEET` §7 called `/EcgNightly` absent and blamed an unmounted volume; it is `Ecg nightly` **with a space**, 19 GB, 71 O2Ring `.dat` + 50 H10 ECG + 54 Verity PPG — so the `oxyii.parse_live` and `polar_pmd.decode_frame` passes are unblocked. **§8** dedupes both fleets' instrument rules — every serious failure in this programme was in the INSTRUMENT, and all of one shape: the check ran and reported success about something it never examined. |
| [`MUTATION-PROGRAM-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-PROGRAM-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-11-BRIEF.md) | **(PROPOSED — what executing `MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09` discovered, and the ONE decision it needs from the owner.)** **The fleet is fully swept for the first time: 9996 mutants, 3702 killed, 38.5 %** — all 8 JS DSPs, every one canary-guarded. **§2 is the decision:** 90 % of distinguishable needs **~4943 more kills**, or calling 5493 of 5904 survivors equivalent (93 % of them); even converting EVERY function at the best rate achieved (`parseJSONL`, 56 %) lands at **72.7 %, not 90 %**. The target was set before anything was measured — keep it as a multi-month programme, re-target to ~55–60 %, or go per-file. **§4 refutes the obvious fix:** c8 was added to test 'JS kills less because it is under-covered' and **disproved it** — 77.3 % branch coverage against Python's 100 % cannot explain 38.5 % against 74.6 %. The suite EXECUTES the code and does not check the result, so a coverage floor will not move the number. **§3:** capture-host is at **74.6 %** and two of its modules already exceed 90 %. **§5:** 492 functions hold the 5860 gaps, the top 49 worth **58× more per unit of work** than the last 49 — do the top 30–50 and re-evaluate. **§6:** coverage-guided test selection is worth 10–100× (integrator's 13.8 h sweep → 15–30 min) and c8 is its prerequisite; GPU is the wrong shape of hardware entirely. **§7:** seven cases where a battery measured NOTHING, each a missing SHAPE rather than insufficient breadth — including `parseJSONL`, where populating every optional block killed FEWER (46) than a stats-only fixture (61), because present/absent are different code paths. **§6-bis (2026-08-31): §6's "one optimisation worth building" was BUILT, hit its estimate (78x integrator / 52x hrvdex / 16x ppgdex), and is QUARANTINED as UNSOUND** — 7 of 38 hrvdex tag-kills became survivors under per-line selection, re-confirmed 2026-08-19 against a better collector, so *better collection did not make it sound*. Three proven mechanisms (state from earlier groups, LOAD-executed lines, integrity/audit). A selection narrowing too far does not run slowly, it **fabricates SURVIVED**. The map stays a **diagnostic, not a filter**, behind `--use-coverage-map`. What is genuinely unbuilt is a DIFFERENT design: **UNION-WITH-TAG** (a superset of the tag set cannot lose a tag kill) plus vetted zeros. **§8-bis (2026-08-31): `capture.py` is NOT auditable at current cost — attempted and measured.** CI's blocker was the gate being unable to SEE the module and is FIXED across seven PRs (#1982-#2000, root cause #1998: the shell walk scanned mutmut's generated `mutants/`, 48 scripts where the tree has 24). Local's blocker was never the clean run — shellcheck is absent so that test SKIPS — it is pure **stats-phase cost: 6h54m still in `Running stats`, frozen at 48,409 bytes, byte-identical across FOUR runs**; generation alone is 32.2 min. ⚠️ Corollary: #1954/#1959's red meant **REFUSING — could not measure**, never *survivors exist*; **no survivor list for capture.py has ever existed**. Measurement runs locally/offline, never as a public CI PR. | Brief *(PROPOSED)* |
| [`MUTATION-SUITE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-17-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-SUITE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-17-BRIEF.md) | **(PROPOSED — what BUILDING the suite discovered; spawned by #1443/#1450.)** Six findings, every one of which failed **quietly**. **The coverage map never reached a sweep** — it is an untracked artefact and §👥.1 puts every session in a worktree, so ecgdex ran 290 min and oxydex 193 min unselected; an absent map falls back to the tag filter, failing safe into *slow*, never *wrong*. **It carried no identity** (`{generated: null}`), so a stale map — which selects groups that do not execute the mutant’s line, making it SURVIVE — was indistinguishable from a fresh one. **§1: ten tools cache to the gitignored `.mutation-sweeps/`**, so an empty cache reads as a cold start; `sharedStatePath` is the general fix and nine still need it. **§2: the equivalence ledger has rotted — 379 of 383 keys match nothing**, 117 of ppgdex’s 129 pointing at lines that hold no survivor at all; it is keyed by line number and lines move. The re-anchor invariant: **a key must be checkable at READ time**, because a miss currently reads as “unclassified”. **§3:** build the map only AFTER a `tests/dex-tests.js` change settles — the map’s values are group INDICES and an inserted group shifts every later one. **§4:** the pseudo and statement-deletion lanes run but are not in the public list; their units must never be summed with operator mutants. **§5:** two owner decisions — a non-regression **ratchet** (not a gate: one that reds on equivalents gets switched off, and the real failures go with it), and **model-generated tests as a recorded non-goal** (0/4 judging code correctness on this box; a plausible wrong assertion is precisely the hollow gate this programme exists to find). **§6:** all six are one shape — a check that ran, examined nothing, and reported cleanly — and **three of the six were mine, written while documenting the trap.** | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS)* |
| [`MUTATION-PIPELINE-INTEGRITY-2026-08-24-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-PIPELINE-INTEGRITY-2026-08-24-BRIEF.md) | **PROPOSED — the mutation pipeline reported CONVERGED for three days while discarding its own results.** Six defects found in one day, recorded together because they CHAINED: each hid the next. The innermost — `journalVerdict` testing `!lastN` before `hit` — journalled **1271 seed-pool kills per run as NOPROPOSAL**, so nothing new reached the journal, the distill came out byte-identical to the previous day's apart from one date digit, the candidate count sat at exactly 1516 for three runs, and the tool reported "probe converged". ⚠️ Converged and AMNESIAC are indistinguishable from outside: both produce a stable number, and the discriminator is whether it SHOULD have moved. The outermost produced a fabricated diagnosis — "the source moved since the crawl" about files with ZERO commits since their crawl — costing a planned four re-crawls that were never needed; a tool may name only what it actually checked. Two of the six left a DEAD BRANCH naming the exact case its own guard order made unreachable (`poolHit ? 'pool'`, `both threw`), a searchable signature. §5 records what caught them: not one gate fired — every one was a number that should have moved (1271 kills vs 0 log lines; 165 records vs 0 carrying `after`), and the decisive experiment changed NOTHING at all, because when two explanations both predict improvement only the null run discriminates. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS — pipeline integrity)* |
| [`MUTATION-COVERAGE-SELECTION-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MUTATION-COVERAGE-SELECTION-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md) | **(IN-PROGRESS — §6's coverage-directed test selection, BUILT and MEASURED; read §0 first after any restart.)** **§1: the fleet is 52.1 % across all eight files** (5124/9832), every one CANARY-VERIFIED, against the published 38.5 % — stale by ~14 points. **§1a: one fleet number was hiding a 62-POINT SPREAD** — `motiondex` is at **95.8 %** (effectively at target) while `ecgdex` sits at **33.8 %** holding 1188 survivors alone; per-file targets look better justified by that table than by anything argued when §2a raised the option. **§1b: reachability ranks `pulsedex` (37.5 % unreached) and `hrvdex` (20.2 %) first and second — the same two files §4 named by a different method — and `pulsedex` IS NOT IN `SWEEP_FILES`, so the worst file is outside every headline number.** **§1c: an UNGUARDED sweep produced a plausible WRONG number** — hrvdex with `canary=STALE` reported 331 kills; two later runs, one canary-passed, reported 305 and 304. Nothing about 331 looked wrong; the only signal was the tool declining to say PASSED. **§0: sweep state lives on the DRIVE under `.mutation-sweeps/`, never `/tmp`**; §0a records that a handoff's "every sweep is gone" was WRONG. **§3a: the defect this shipped with** — `suiteArgs` applied to only ONE of two runners, so ecgdex scored 1324 of 1809 INVALID and the CANARY VOIDED the result rather than publishing it. **§3 RETRACTED:** calibration is 0.6-2.5 % of a full sweep, not 90 %. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS)* |
| [`JS-DSP-MUTATION-FLEET-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md`](briefs/JS-DSP-MUTATION-FLEET-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md) | **(DONE 2026-08-09 — folded into `MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)** First mutation-coverage map of the **JS** DSP fleet — the sibling of the capture-host one, and it had never existed for ~31,000 lines of shipped signal processing. All nine sampled: **28 %–68 %** against `clock.js`'s 84 %, and the ranking tracks in-tag test surface almost exactly (`ppgdex` the one exception — 49 groups, 33 %). Tag cost spans **300×** (1 s–310 s), splitting the fleet into *sweepable* and *diff-gate-only*. Both caveats are load-bearing: every rate is a scoped **lower bound**, and every run was **unguarded**. ⚠️ **§2 is corrected by the successor (§5):** a file's rate is a weighted average over functions of different CHARACTER — validation/dispatch converts at 77 %, numeric and parsing at ~27 % — so ranking FILES, and §6's order of work, follow the wrong axis. |
| [`PPGDEX-TESTABLE-SURFACE-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PPGDEX-TESTABLE-SURFACE-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md) | **(DONE 2026-08-09 — folded into `MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)** Why PpgDex runs the fleet's **second-broadest tag (49 groups, more than `ecgdex`)** and still kills only **34 %**. Not neglect: its 767 survivors spread across **84 functions** (largest cluster 8 %, vs HRVDex's 57 %), and **all six top clusters are unexported closures** — 4099 lines, 78 internal functions, and the suite can only call `compute()`. Proposes exposing the pure helpers on `_bare` (HRVDex's existing pattern), with the re-bundle + `computeHash` + fixture-re-verification cost stated — **and §4a then WITHDREW it** on measurement. ⚠️ §4a's own headline (`~27 % killable ⇒ ceiling ≈ 52 %`) is withdrawn in turn by #1052: `loadOwnExport` probes at **77 %**, so the equivalent share is a property of what a function DOES, not of the file. Three successive over-generalisations from agreeing data points, each refuted by measuring one more case. |
| [`H10-ECG-RATE-CORPUS-CHECK-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/H10-ECG-RATE-CORPUS-CHECK-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **The repo states the H10's ECG rate four different ways; the vendor's own decode arbitrates.** Checked against the Polar Sensor Logger corpus (`Ecg nightly/`, 19 GB, 50 ECG files, H10 `02849638`) — PSL decodes the SAME device independently of `polar_pmd.py`, which is what makes it an arbiter. **Measured over 50 files:** modal step median 7 692 672 ns = **129.9938 Hz, +47 ppm** from nominal; per-file mean rate spans **129.887–130.088** (~1 540 ppm), which no single figure describes. So `polar_pmd.py:492` and `PMD-DECODE-SCALE-AND-RATE` §140's **"exactly 130.0000 / 0.00 %"** overstate, and `POLAR-SDK-CAPTURE`'s **129.94** understates. **§3 — it is a REAL clock, not a drawn one:** 1759 distinct inter-sample deltas at a 12.85 % modal share, against the O2Ring's ONE delta at 100 % (`o2ring-timestamp-is-drawn`) — so the H10's ppm figure means something and the drawn-axis finding must NOT be extended to it. **§4 — documentation fix only, change no constant:** +47 ppm is far below `CK_AXIS_MAX_PPM` and below what any consumer resolves; the back-timing comment's Verity-contrast argument is unaffected (Verity MAG +2.6 % vs ECG +0.005 %). **§5 — the method note that matters:** the FIRST file measured gave −646 ppm on 300 002 rows and a clean modal delta, and a brief contradicting the repo was nearly filed on it; across all 50 that file is an outlier. Volume within one file is not a sample size when the quantity varies BETWEEN files. **EXECUTED 2026-08-09** — the comment (now `:535`, not `:492`), §140 **and** §77 carry the measured figure plus the reason the old one was circular (`DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH` §4.1: 98.63 % of that segment's deltas were our own nominal read back); `O2RING-SYNTHESISED-AXIS` §3 gains the ⛔ side-by-side discriminator table; no constant changed. ⚠️ **Two of this brief's own numbers failed re-measurement:** the ppm SIGN was backwards (a step +364 ns LONG is a rate of **−47 ppm**, not +47 — which is why this never really conflicted with `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH`'s −105), and §3's `1759 / 12.85 %` was ONE uncapped file, not the corpus (**660 median / 26.70 %**, worst case 55.38 %, still 44 points clear of the drawn threshold). The §2 measurements otherwise reproduce to the digit. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-09)* |
| [`DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md`](briefs/DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md) | **Every sample rate in the fleet is a label, and all of them are measured here** — H10 ECG 129.9876 (−105 ppm), Verity ACC 51.6825 / GYRO 51.6814 (−6 100 ppm), PPG 55.1293 / 176.4180 (+2 350 ppm), **MAG 20.4980 (+24 898 ppm)**, O2Ring **125.000**. Three separate oscillators inside one Verity, 31 000 ppm apart; only the 32.768 kHz timestamp crystal is quartz (+27 ppm vs NTP). **Extends `H10-ECG-RATE-CORPUS-CHECK-2026-08-04`**, which owns the H10 number and the `PMD-DECODE-SCALE-AND-RATE` correction — this adds the **mechanism**: `130.0000` was measured on the last session before commit `80e05501`, where **98.63 % of deltas are exactly `1e9/130`**, i.e. our own nominal read back. A rate landing exactly on nominal is a fallback signature, not a good clock (H10 ACC@200 does it on 97.2 % of samples). **The divider hypothesis is architecturally right and empirically defeated** — every PPG AFE computes `base/N` (Maxim's datasheet prints "25 sps" as **24.995**), but the numerator is an RC oscillator, so the structure survives only in ratios (`416:52 = 8.000212`, `176:55 = 3.200077`). **The O2Ring is the one crystal-clocked device**: TI AFE4403 off a 32 MHz crystal ÷8 ÷32000 = 125.000, and `156` is an **inserted beat marker**, not a missing-sample sentinel — confirmed at 5-min granularity, but **only 0.936 of H10 beats** and **±300 ppm, 7× looser than crystal precision**. ⚠️ **`O2PPG_FS` does two jobs needing different numbers** — 125.000 costs **+223 s/night** of axis drift, 125.738 costs +24 s; the fix is structural (strip markers to a beat column), never a new constant. Records **15 of its own claims corrected** and two adversarial attacks that failed. Config decided 2026-08-05: **MAG off, GYRO off, PPG 176 kept, O2Ring 200 Hz `RtPpg` on, H10 ACC 25→50**. **§9 EXECUTED 2026-08-06 — the four code items; the two field items cannot be done from a checkout.** **§6.3 DONE:** `effFs` measured the RADIO, not the sensor, and overstated every waveform stream by a fixed factor. Two defects, one cure — `span` ran from the OLDEST frame's ARRIVAL while `total` counted that frame's samples too, so k frames of n at spacing T reported `k·n/((k−1)·T)`, a **k/(k−1) bias that is always positive and never averages out** (the 5 s window holds ~9 ECG frames ⇒ 130 Hz read **146.25 predicted / 146.6 observed**); and BLE hands several frames over per connection event, so arrival times collapse and the denominator measures batching. `push()` now takes the frame's last **device** stamp (`dev_ns`, additive/optional) and the rate is measured between first and last device stamps over exactly the frames that closed inside — an identity. Below two frames the answer is **`None`, never `0.0`** (zero is a *measurement of silence*; silence is already caught by sample age), `stream_health` treats it as cannot-judge so it can never manufacture WEAK, and the monitor already null-guarded. Fallbacks are real, not defensive: no-device-clock streams (O2Ring) keep arrival timing with the bias fixed, and a **backward** device clock falls back the same way — the H10 resets to a 2019 epoch on every strap removal. **Known-answer test written FIRST and observed failing**, per the acceptance item: 12 frames at exact device spacing delivered as `[1]×12`, `[4,4,4]`, `[1,1,10]`, `[12]` must all give 130 Hz to ~1 ppm; 146.25 is pinned as a VALUE so a merely-reduced bias still reds. **§6.2 AUDITED — four of five were already landed and unstamped** (`_ctrl` 0xF0+opcode, 128 ns, `&0x3F`, GYRO/MAG T1 decode-or-raise) **and the fifth CANNOT be done as written.** PPI back-timing was implemented and reddened two tests that pin `back=0` **on purpose**: *"PulseDex reads the Phone column and has a one-way two-pointer matcher, so it depends on this."* And there is no device column to fix instead — `writers.write_ppi` deliberately emits no `sensor_ns` because *every row the box has written has `sensor_ns == 0`*, so back-timing it would write to a missing column off a `last_ns` of 0 and yield **negative** stamps.
