## 4 · Validation, cohort & papers

| Doc | What it is | Role |
|---|---|---|
| [`STALE-BRIEF-GUARD-MEASURES-THE-WRONG-TREE-2026-08-18-BRIEF.md`](briefs/STALE-BRIEF-GUARD-MEASURES-THE-WRONG-TREE-2026-08-18-BRIEF.md) | **DONE 2026-08-18 — `guard-stale-brief.sh:113` ran `git merge-base HEAD origin/main` with no `-C`, so a PreToolUse hook resolves `HEAD` in `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (the SHARED ROOT) rather than the worktree being edited.** It answers *"has this brief moved since the ROOT's HEAD"* when the question is *"since MY branch's base"* — and §👥.1 MANDATES worktrees while §👥.2b-bis measures the root at 92/248/255 commits behind, so the guard reads from precisely the tree the rest of CLAUDE.md says not to trust. ⚠️ **False positive MEASURED** (blocked on `084db04e`, a commit that WAS my HEAD: `--is-ancestor` true, `rev-list --count` 0). ⚠️ **False negative now MEASURED, and TOTAL rather than occasional:** with the root at `origin/main` (measured — `rev-list --count` 0), `base` IS `origin/main`, so `base..origin/main` is empty BY CONSTRUCTION and the hook exits 0 for every guarded path. Its own query returns `''` for `DOCS-INDEX.md` and `FABRICATED-DEFAULTS-FLEET`, **both of which moved within the last 20 commits**. The guard is a **no-op right now, for everyone**. 🔴 **Two correct fixes combined into a silent hole:** the guard can only block when the root is STALE, and `tepna-sync-main.timer` (15 min) plus the 2026-08-18 root drain now keep it current — so **the guard only ever worked while the root was broken**, and fixing the root switched it off with nothing reporting it. Also retires the §👥.2b-bis reassurance: for this guard a pulled checkout is the DISABLED state, not the safe one. **FIXED + GATED:** the hook now resolves the repo from the edited file and anchors queries at that worktree's toplevel; 4 legs added, the false-negative leg seen to fail first (ALLOW → DENY). ⚠️ The FIRST draft of the fix was wrong and the anti-vacuity legs caught it — `git -C <dir>` also makes PATHSPECS relative, so `-- briefs/X.md` became `briefs/briefs/X.md`, matched nothing, and read as "did not move" → ALLOW, turning 3 passing DENYs into ALLOWs. ⚠️ Root cause of the blind spot: every pre-existing test ran `cd "$WORK"` first, so hook-cwd and edit-target were the SAME tree by construction — the suite could not have caught this at any thoroughness. | Brief *(DONE — guard defect found, fixed, and gated two-directionally)* |
| [`INTERDISCIPLINARY-LITERATURE-2026-08-16-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTERDISCIPLINARY-LITERATURE-2026-08-16-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE (living — last-verified 2026-08-18) — the citation base is 86 verified DOIs of which EXACTLY ONE is outside clinical medicine, while every live problem in the suite is metrological, timing, identifiability or verification.** A reading queue for that gap across 14 fields, each tied to the specific Tepna problem it addresses and marked with its verification status. Highest-value entries: **Riley & Greenhall 2004** (`10.1049/cp:20040932`) identifies power-law noise **analytically from the lag-1 autocorrelation, without fitting a slope** — which would RETIRE the `1.96·SE` refusal band in `classifyAllan`/`allan.py classify` rather than tune it, and breaks the EDF circularity (EDF needs the noise type); **Teunissen 1995 LAMBDA** (`10.1007/BF00863419`) — beat alignment mod one RR *is* the GNSS carrier-phase integer ambiguity problem, and GNSS has a third step Tepna lacks: estimate float → search integer → **VALIDATE the fix** (ratio test / success rate), plus cycle-slip detection for a dropped or inserted beat; **generalizability theory** (Cronbach 1972, Brennan 2001) whose **D-study** answers `sensor-trio-nights`'s how-many-nights question **analytically from the corpus on disk**, where the paper currently answers it with 5 M Monte-Carlo trials under a planted model it admits is fully synthetic — and subsumes `nights-icc` as the single-facet case; **PELT** (Killick 2012, `10.1080/01621459.2012.737745`) partitions a series by a global penalised cost so a deep event cannot drag the baseline meant to catch it — structurally immune to the trailing-mean artifact behind the ODI-4 under-count; **covariance intersection** (Julier & Uhlmann 1997, `10.1109/ACC.1997.609105`) is built for exactly the bent uncorrelated-error assumption `sigma-no-reference` limitation (v) concedes, degrading to *conservative* rather than *wrong*; **RJ/DJ jitter decomposition** — deterministic jitter is BOUNDED and random is UNBOUNDED, so one `spreadMs` conflates a hard 7.5 ms BLE interval bound with a tail that has no worst case. Also names a live hole: **five time-frequency citations (Allan 1966, Gray & Allan 1974, Premoli & Tavella 1993, Torcaso 1998, Riley SP 1065) are cited in `sigma-no-reference` PROSE and absent from `CITATION-VERIFICATION`**, so `citation-ledger` cannot see them. ⚠️ Reading queue, NOT adopted methods — nothing read end to end, and `briefs/` is outside the citation gate by design, which is why unverified DOIs may sit here and must be resolved before moving to `papers/` or `docs/`. ⚠️ **Status corrected 2026-08-18 PROPOSED → REFERENCE:** §0 calls this *"a reading queue, not a set of adopted methods"* whose entries are *"surfaced by search"* rather than read end to end — nothing here is executable, so there is no state in which it becomes DONE, and `PROPOSED` read as *not yet started* on a doc already in daily use via `tools/doc-search.mjs`. Its same-day sibling `-DIAGNOSIS` was already REFERENCE. All 4 tracked items closed 2026-08-17; re-stamp `last-verified` when entries are added. | Brief *(REFERENCE living 2026-08-18)* |
| [`INTERDISCIPLINARY-LITERATURE-DIAGNOSIS-2026-08-16-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTERDISCIPLINARY-LITERATURE-DIAGNOSIS-2026-08-16-BRIEF.md) | **REFERENCE — a cross-field diagnosis of the methods actually IN the source, labelled by correspondence rather than by shared vocabulary.** Sibling to the reading queue above and deliberately the other direction: that one asks what to read, this one asks what Tepna has already built and what it is called elsewhere. Six labels (REINVENTED · APPROXIMATION · ALREADY CORRECT · MISSING · UNUSUAL APPLICATION · POSSIBLE NOVELTY) under an evidence rule that **a shared name is never enough** — each diagnosis must name the exact function, the external method, what each requires, and confidence. Reads ALREADY CORRECT on overlapping Allan deviation, the Pan-Tompkins family QRS chain with redundant SQI, Lomb-Scargle direct on irregular beats, and the executable validation substrate; APPROXIMATION on `hostAxis` (a one-way BLE arrival cannot separate event time, offset and transport delay without a delay model) and on confidence fusion under unknown dependence; MISSING on GUM-style uncertainty propagation — *a binary trusted flag cannot distinguish a 1 ms result from a 50 ms one* — and on an explicit identifiability protocol before new cross-sensor claims. §8 states what the diagnosis **cannot** establish, including that code policy is not deployment evidence for PPS. ⚠️ **ADOPTED, not authored here** — found untracked in the shared checkout, author unknown, committed at the owner's direction with two marked adopter's notes: §2.1's EDF recommendation was already closed AGAINST by measurement (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS` §6, `HOSTAXIS-STABILITY-FOLLOWUPS` §3), and §6's `dead-ends.html` contradiction was VERIFIED on adoption — the abstract calls the ~96 ms scatter an artifact of a 450 ms acceptance window while the body still leads with it as *what actually limits it*. | Brief *(REFERENCE)* |

| [`BUZZ-FIDUCIAL-HARDWARE-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md`](briefs/BUZZ-FIDUCIAL-HARDWARE-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md) | **(REFERENCE — nine commanded buzzes, three devices, live box capture.)** **CONFIRMS `KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE` §4b's co-location correction on hardware for the first time**: co-located **85–155×**, separated **2–5×**, no overlap — a tap does NOT transmit between body segments, the clapperboard principle does. **CORRECTS `O2RING-BUZZ-FIDUCIAL` §2b**: only ACCELEROMETERS detect the buzz. The ring's 125 Hz pleth (which §2b named as the detector) does not discriminate, the H10 ECG is flat, and 🔴 the ring's OWN ACC is the WORST detector of its own buzz when clamped. ⚠️ **The buzz must never be used to TIME anything** — the command stamp is not the event (~1 Hz poll jitter) and per-sample host stamps are back-timed across the packet. Three offset methods compared on one session: PMDARRIVAL min-filter **+994.7 ms** (n-bias measured at ±1.8 ms), hostAxis median +2428.8 ms (inflated by 191 vs 29 packets/min asymmetry), buzz artifact +440/+388 ms — **not averageable, they estimate different quantities**. Also records four incidental defects incl. a `clock_skew_sec` of **−26.7 years** passing unguarded | Brief *(REFERENCE)* |

Ships the estimator, the `emerging` tier, and the three preprints the validation corpus unlocks (`papers/cpap-flow-reference.html`, `acc-respiratory-rate.html`, `effort-typing-null.html` — all DRAFT until the analysis tool is ported). Records two constraints on what may be claimed: a tilt-angle channel is provably redundant, and **posture robustness is untested** (corpus roll IQR 13.1–17.9°) **§4a PREMISE CHECK 2026-08-03 — the blocking item was STALE and named the WRONG blocker.** §4 held all three papers **"DRAFT, not submittable"** behind one item: *"port the analysis harness — currently the numbers regenerate only from a Python harness outside the repo."* Verified against the tree: `resp-acc-analysis.js` (654 lines) **landed in this brief's OWN PR #347**, exports `RespAccAnalysis`, is wired into **both** test lanes with a known-answer group, and the page drives it (`folderInput` → `change` → `run()`) — and **this brief's own §6** records a regeneration *"driven headlessly against the tool's engine"*, i.e. the harness was not merely ported but USED. The brief contradicted itself. **The real blocker is the FIGURES:** `papers/figures/` holds 27 figures and **none** belong to these three papers, which carry **zero `<img>` tags** between them — nothing was ever emitted or referenced. Plus `PAPERS-AUDIT.md` rows. **Not established and not ticked:** that the harness produces CORRECT figures end-to-end — that needs a browser + the CPAP corpus and remains §6's open item (the folder-ingest/FileReader/render path is still unexercised). The papers are still not submittable, but for a much smaller reason than a port. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS 2026-07-21 — estimator landed; papers blocked on the analysis-tool port)* **§10 EXECUTED 2026-08-06 — the figure layer is BUILT, and a whole capture route was invisible.** **(a) FIGURES.** The page rendered tables only — **zero `<canvas>` anywhere** and no export path — so no run of it, headless or manual, could emit a PNG; §4a's re-scoping is right that the blocker was never *"run the tool"* but a capability nobody had built. Three canvases now render from the numbers the tables already compute: **Bland–Altman** against the CPAP-flow reference (bias and ±1.96 SD direct-labelled), the **MAE-vs-coverage abstention curve**, and **per-night MAE** as sorted dots with a labelled median — each downloadable, plus a stacked `acc-resp-figures.png` using the same export shape `nights-icc-analysis.js` already has, so `papers/figures/` keeps ONE convention. The arithmetic is **gated because it is published**: `RespAccAnalysis.blandAltman` is pure and lives in the module BOTH runners load (the app layer is loaded by neither), so plot and table cannot drift; known answer hand-checkable rather than recorded — diffs {+2,0,−2,+4,−4} ⇒ bias 0, **sample** SD √10, limits ±1.96·√10 — **mutation-verified** (population SD reds 3 assertions by value, 2.828 vs 3.162), and it **refuses** rather than fabricating (one pair has no SD; ±0 limits would read as perfect agreement). **Two layout defects were caught by RENDERING the figures and looking at them**, which no colour validator can do: the right annotations were **clipped** against a fixed gutter (`−1.96 SD −3.32` → `−1.96 SD -3.3`) and the coverage axis divided its range into fifths, reading **45 % · 56 % · 68 % · 79 % · 91 % · 102 %**. Gutter now measured from the widest label; axis takes explicit deciles. **(b) THE INVISIBLE NIGHTS.** §4a recorded and left this: `groupFiles` matched `_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_ACC.txt` (phone) while the capture host writes the SAME BYTES as `_YYYYMMDDHHMMSS_ACC.txt` — one 14-digit run, no separator — and `tools/trio-batch.mjs` has carried both as RE_POLAR/RE_POLAR_CH all along. Box nights passed the filename filter then fell out of the loop on a bare `continue`: **not counted, not logged, not skipped-with-a-reason**. Three papers rest on which nights this apparatus can see, so an entire capture route was excluded from the corpus and the exclusion appeared in **no count and no reported n** — the suite's own "a check that reports a clean result about something it never examined". Parser moved into the pure module and gated both ways (both layouts parse, both resolve to the SAME instant, refusal still refuses on no-stamp / 13-digit / non-ACC / null); mutation-verified against the pre-fix regex — 2 red, refusal assertions stay green. Caller now reports `nAcc` / `unstamped` / `noFlow` with examples. **Still open:** §1's corpus run — unchanged, it needs the files a checkout does not carry — but a run can now emit the figures AND will see the box nights it used to drop. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS 2026-07-21 — triaged 2026-09-01: CODE COMPLETE; the only remaining item is DATA-blocked — the paper figures need paired ACC+BRP nights and only 6 dates intersect locally, an owner staging action, not a work-unit)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-APNEA-TYPING-REVIEW-2026-07-22-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-APNEA-TYPING-REVIEW-2026-07-22-BRIEF.md) | `typeApneaByEffort()` types apneas from chest-ACC effort **amplitude**, which measurement shows does not carry that information: effort during central apnea sits at **0.99×** its own night baseline (not absent, *normal*), only **16.5%** fall below half baseline where a single RIP belt reaches **84%**, and best achievable discrimination is **AUC 0.691**. An absolute `EFFORT_FLOOR_G` gate therefore types 83.5–95.4% of central apneas as OBSTRUCTIVE — wrong for the dominant class. The CPAP-pressure explanation was tested against `MaskPress.2s` and **fails** (ρ = −0.174, p = 0.0008). Offers four options (recommended: abstain) and does **not** presume the owner's call. **EXECUTED 2026-07-31 — option 1, abstain:** `obstructive`/`central` are now permanently **`null`** (never `0` — a zero reads as "measured none"), every desat is `untyped` with a machine-readable `typingWithdrawn`/`withdrawnReason`, no `apnea_obstructive`/`apnea_central` impulse is emitted, and both tuning constants are **deleted** + gate-scanned so the rule cannot be re-enabled by tuning. The surviving measurement is `effortCovered` (how many desats the chest ACC witnessed). Option 3 (central-only) rejected on the same corpus: the shipped central branch fired at `frac === 0` against a floor mis-scaled 0.2×–3×, so it too asserts a type the feature does not support. §7.2 corrects this brief's own premise — **there was never a render surface** (typing shipped export-only; the typed events were built and dropped), and §7.3 records that there was likewise no evidence badge to re-tier. Gated by the rewritten `apnea-typing` group, now pinning the **negative** (effort present throughout ⇒ no type) | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`MOTIONDEX-RESPIRATORY-RATE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-22-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MOTIONDEX-RESPIRATORY-RATE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-22-BRIEF.md) | What executing the resp-rate brief surfaced. Blocking item: re-run the 26-night corpus end-to-end through `resp-acc-analysis.html` so the papers' numbers trace to a repo tool. Records that **sample-rate precision is this codebase's recurring silent failure** (it bit three times in one work-unit — count÷duration, an assumed rate, and a millisecond-quantised stamp giving a 1.2% error → tens of minutes of clock-offset error) and proposes a shared `nativeHz` helper; plus integer-decimation-is-not-resampling, a double-filtering footgun, that the confidence gate is **not** an apnea filter, that posture is untestable on this corpus, and that the browser render-coverage gate was never run. **§2·§3·§4 GATED 2026-08-01 (§2a).** The premise was worse than stated: all three fixes existed, but `resp-acc-analysis.js` was loaded by **neither test lane**, so `nativeHz`/`toGrid`/the channel constructors had **zero** coverage and every fix rested on the comment above it — the same shape as the defect. Now wired into both runners with the three modes pinned as known answers: `nativeHz` reads the `relNs` sensor counter (**25.3400 Hz**) and the ms-stamp fallback is pinned as *measurably wrong* (**25.6410 Hz, +1.19 %**) so deleting that branch cannot pass quietly; a 20 % dropout moves the rate 0.00 Hz (count÷duration would read ~20 % low); the last peak keeps its **absolute time** through `toGrid` (297.00 s → 297.00 s, where a 5.07 Hz grid does not); one `flowChannel` pass attenuates 0.8 Hz by **−36.69 dB** vs −64.66 dB for two, passband −0.07 dB. Two of those reproduce figures this brief published independently (§2's 1.2 % error, §8's −36.6 dB), which is why they are known answers rather than guessed tolerances. Mutation-verified by reintroducing each original bug — **and one assertion was itself broken, caught only by mutating**: the §3 check first snapped the output peak to the nearest crest and *passed under the decimation mutation*, since decimation slides the peak onto a different crest. **Not built, with reasons recorded:** the shared `nativeHz` spine helper (inlined into every bundle → re-stamps all 8 `manifestHash` values for one caller) and the proposed "no DSP computes a rate as `n / durSec`" lint — scanned, and it has **no subjects**: the only count-over-duration expressions are genuine *event* rates (`oxydex-dsp.js:3079`, `oxydex-fusion.js:326`), inseparable from sample rates by source scan without the grandfather list `CPAP-REAL-CORPUS-FOLLOWUPS-II` §4 removed | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS — parked 2026-09-05: nothing open to code; §1's preprint-headline leg is the OWNER's publish-vs-retract call, §6/§7 need a second subject. Prior: triaged 2026-09-01: every section resolved and NOTHING is open to code — §2·§3·§4 gated, §5 a recorded caveat, §6 closed as probably-never, §7 needs a second subject, §8 REFUTED by measurement; the shared `nativeHz` spine helper stays deliberately unbuilt, its lint measured to have no subjects)* |
| [`PPGDEX-OPTICAL-DETECTOR-AND-SIGMA-REDERIVE-2026-07-11-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PPGDEX-OPTICAL-DETECTOR-AND-SIGMA-REDERIVE-2026-07-11-BRIEF.md) | §1 — the optical beat detector counted a prominent DIASTOLIC wave as a second beat (TERMA has no amplitude discrimination; at 48 bpm the intruder lands ~625 ms out, twice the fixed 0.30 s refractory), so optical HR read 2× true on 4 of 17 trio nights. Fixed by sizing the refractory from a windowed-autocorrelation cadence. §2 — re-derived the published Verity σ: the gate was MISDIAGNOSING harmonic doubling as "poor PPG contact" (cost 41% of the corpus, nights 10→15); σ confirmed 1.94→1.85, and the dead "6.2" prose traced + fixed. **DONE 2026-07-22 close-out:** the 4 doubled nights re-run on real raw Verity+H10 confirm OLD ratios 1.90–2.20 (`harmonic-double` band) → NEW ~1.00; `pickChannel` gained an inert-on-corpus `harmonicOutlierRefIdx` guard (gate-backed, 0 fixtures moved); the three residual §2 doc items closed as recorded conclusions (no other-paper edits — the SENSOR-TRIO CI curve is simulated, VERITY-SIGMA §7's non-resting need is orthogonal, SIGMA-REWRITE 1.94 confirmed) | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-22)* |
| [`TRIO-ARTIFACT-GATE-AND-N15-POWER-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TRIO-ARTIFACT-GATE-AND-N15-POWER-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | The three-cornered hat is a VARIANCE estimator with no robustness to artifact: 3 bad epochs of 86 (3.5%) inflated σ_H10 from 2.5 → 9.6 bpm. Two σ estimates were wrong in OPPOSITE directions — Verity's CI optimistic (the quality gate was censoring the hard nights), H10's pessimistic (one 15-min artifact burst). Validated fix: a cross-corner consensus gate (AF-safe by construction) **⚠️ §3’s consensus-gate solution is DISPROVEN** — `TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT` prototyped it on the real corpus (*"that gate … is either unreliable or biases the noisiest corner"*); build the fused-weight hat instead. §1/§2 findings and the N=15 power material STAND and still feed `SENSOR-TRIO-NIGHTS-PAPER`, which is why this is a scoped note and **not** a whole-brief `Superseded-by`. **Done-when RECONCILED + §6 degeneracy MEASURED 2026-08-01.** Two Done-when boxes still asked for exactly the cross-corner consensus gate the ⚠️ banner declares dead — the banner landed 2026-07-18 and the list was never reconciled with it, so a reader working it top-to-bottom would have built the forbidden thing; both are RETIRED in place with the substance **re-homed, not dropped** (the paper still owes both corrections, now under the fused hat's Done-when). **§6** answers the residual-degeneracy box with `tools/tch-degeneracy-stats.mjs`, and two of its three claims do not survive: *"several nights"* is really **8 of 39 (21 %)** — every one the same non-negativity-boundary failure, where the boundary member's σ is ~0 *by construction, not measurement*; **which corner is NOT attributable** (OxyDex 5 · PpgDex 2 · ECGDex 1, `P(X≥5 | n=8, ⅓) = 0.088` — tempting because OxyDex is the 1 Hz-quantised corner, which is precisely why it is recorded as unestablished); and the *"correlated-error regime"* attribution is **inverted by the one proxy available** — degenerate nights carry **less** co-motion ρ (median 0.26 vs 0.41, two-sided permutation p = 0.090), suggesting they fail because there is too little co-motion for the ρ-rescue to grip, not because they carry more correlated error. Offered as hypothesis, not result: settling it needs ~25 degenerate nights (~125 trio nights vs the 40 available). | Brief *(**DONE — 2026-08-14**; all five Done-when boxes closed — 2 DONE/REFUTED, 2 RETIRED-in-place as *do not build*, 1 MEASURED — and the `ms;hr;c` ownership that was the last thing holding it open was discharged 2026-08-09. ⚠️ **DONE ≠ superseded**: §1/§2's findings and the N=15 power tables stay live reading for `SENSOR-TRIO-NIGHTS-PAPER`. §6.2/§6.3 remain open questions **parked on corpus size** (~125 trio nights vs 40) — parked, not delegated)* |
| [`TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT-2026-07-14-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT-2026-07-14-BRIEF.md) | Executes `TRIO-ARTIFACT-GATE` with a better estimator. Prototyping on the real corpus showed the proposed hard consensus gate (and every single-cue fix) is unreliable or biases the noisiest corner. The validated answer: a **fused-weight hat** — per-second per-sensor confidence `c = density_trust × quality_trust` (DSP tier) drives a **weighted-variance** TCH. Ground truth: recovers 1.51/1.56/2.59 (planted 1.50/1.56/2.60), exact, unbiased, AF-safe, O(n), no corpus-tuned constant | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS)* |
| [`TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-14-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-14-BRIEF.md) | Where the fused-hat findings generalize across the fleet: `RMSSD`/`SDNN`/`CV`/`MAGE` are all variance-family estimators with the SAME 0-breakdown fragility, and `beatConfidence` is signal-agnostic. Transfer map — ECGDex-own-HRV (drop-in), PulseDex/HRVDex robust HRV, OxyDex ODI / CPAPDex AHI artifact-event suppression, GlucoDex robust variability, revive the dead `bSQI` (detector B) **Do 4 MEASURED and REFUTED 2026-08-03** (`tools/cgm-variability-check.mjs` ships with it). Finding 5's "compression lows inflate CV/SD/MAGE" was a principle-transfer hypothesis nobody had measured. A breakdown-point-0 estimator is only wrong when there IS a tail: on the real 28-day Lingo corpus **`SD/MADn` = 1.07**, so a robust scale moves CV by **0.85 pp** (14.2 → 13.3 %) against a 36 % consensus threshold. **The committed synthetic reads 2.48** — flat 89–98 baseline with planted meal peaks makes MADn tiny — so the hypothesis would have "confirmed" on it at 8.3 pp of illusory improvement. The nocturnal artifact is REAL but lands on LEVEL, not variance: overnight is 3.70× enriched in sub-70 readings (40.1 % vs 1.1 % morning) while being the FLATTEST daypart (CV 10 vs 15), so the exposure is mean/GMI/TBR/LBGI, not SD/CV/MAGE. Not claimed: that these are compression lows — unfalsifiable from the trace alone. Do 1/2/3/5 stand unmeasured, and the same one-command ratio check applies to each. **Do 5 EXECUTED 2026-08-20**: bSQI was unreachable (its producer `detectPeaksB` was module-internal while the exported `computeSQI` requires its peaks), so finding 6's "silently ≈ 0 corpus-wide" had never been checkable. Made observable (#1553) and measured over 16 real H10 segments / 30,306 beats: median **0.9951**, the second-healthiest term — the claim is REFUTED as written. It survives sharpened on 1 segment in 16, where one artifact 9.8× the median R amplitude lifted detector B's global-max floor above every real R peak: bSQI **0.0019** while `cleanBeatPct` read 99.8. Fixed by CAPPING the reference at 3× the typical R peak (#1554) — 15 of 16 segments byte-identical, 0 fixtures moved. Do 1 owes no build: (a) already satisfied by the rich export's per-beat `timeseries.rr.conf`, (b) still unjustified at 0.19 % of epochs. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-20)* |
| [`TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-20-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TCH-FUSED-ROBUST-HAT-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-20-BRIEF.md) | What closing the fused-hat transfer map surfaced. **`kSQI` is the term actually running low on real data** — median **0.457** of a possible 1, never above 0.52 on any of 16 segments, while the other three terms sit at ceiling; that implies a windowed kurtosis of ~6.2 against the source's own *"clean QRS window: kurt ~5–15"*. Two readings needing opposite responses: the `/8` mapping is mis-scaled for this hardware (the composite loses ~0.16 of its 0.30 share on EVERY beat, making `sqiThr` 0.3 a stricter gate than it reads as), or kurtosis is doing fine-grained discrimination in a narrow band and the level is irrelevant. The discriminator is whether `kSQI` VARIES within a record — a near-constant term carries no information whatever its level — and that decides it without changing a line of compute. Also carries Do 1(b), the parent's one live question (0.19 % of epochs, still no reason shown), and the method note: three candidate fixes were ranked WRONGLY by the aggregate — the runner-up moved the median 0.009 while degrading six segments by 0.11 each.  **§1 ANSWERED same day:** `kSQI` is NOT near-constant — per-beat CV **0.12–0.50** within records, p5→p95 spanning 0.047→0.596 — so it is doing real discrimination, reading (b). But `clamped@1 = 0` on every segment: no real beat saturates the mapping, so the term contributes ~0.20 of its nominal 0.30. A re-scale is **DECLINED** because `buildNN` gates on an ABSOLUTE `sqiThr` 0.3 and 0.30 × 1.0 IS that threshold — correcting the level would let a good-kurtosis beat pass on kurtosis alone with detector B disagreeing, an implausible RR and a dead-lead amplitude (0.135 excluded today → 0.54 included after). The low level is PROTECTIVE. No compute change. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-20)* |
| [`R5-HR-TRIPLET-REFERENCE-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/R5-HR-TRIPLET-REFERENCE-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | R5 executed: the HR triplet's independence test is NOT RUNNABLE — a hat cannot be validated by one of its OWN corners (σ_ECG²(TCH) ≡ cov of the other two corners' errors, verified to 6.7e-14, so the test has exactly zero power). What IS measurable is bias: OxyDex under-reads by −0.36 bpm, invisible to every published σ. The fix is one cable — connect the ResMed oximeter | Brief *(PROPOSED — the two do-now write-ups landed 2026-08-04: `sigma-no-reference` Limitations (ix) now states the σ is variance-only and never validated against truth, carrying the −0.269 bpm pooled bias (n=3,136 epochs/40 nights, 11.0 σ, `tools/oxy-hr-bias.mjs`); `sensor-trio-nights` §2.2 now carries the identity derivation. What remains is hardware-gated, not unstarted)* |
| [`R5-HR-TRIPLET-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`](briefs/R5-HR-TRIPLET-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md) | **The bias was an estimator; the σ is almost untouched — and one of those needs saying out loud.** Spawned by R5's bias item. The parent showed *"OxyDex under-reads by −0.36 bpm"* is a cross-node confound — `ECGDex 60000/mean(RR)` vs `OxyDex median(1 Hz rate)` differ by −0.299 on 1670 real blocks, and the ring's firmware tracks chest ECG to −0.027 (0.6σ) over 252 windows. This asks the question that follows: is the **σ** an artifact too? **No** — removing the estimator term moves the O2Ring leg under 2 % (2.60→2.554), inside stated uncertainty, and 3.5 % is an upper bound because the two statistics share one RR series. Owed: pick ONE epoch statistic fleet-wide (a third, `mean(rate)`, reads +0.203 the other way), NAME it in the epoch block so a consumer can refuse a mismatched pair, and either isolate the mechanism in real RR or stop claiming one — synthetic series give +0.03, +0.54 (opposite sign), −0.03 and none reproduces −0.299. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS 2026-08-04 — triaged 2026-09-01: measurement COMPLETE, three of four owed items closed; the fourth is `[~]` by design — OxyDex has no intervals so the fleet `rate-of-mean` switch is ROUTED, NOT TAKEN, an owner call because it moves a published value)* |
| [`ZEPHYR-INSTRUMENT-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`](briefs/ZEPHYR-INSTRUMENT-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md) | The vigil Zephyr nRF52840 dongle as the OPEN BLE timing instrument (paired daytime task). Measured 2026-08-22: it enumerates/HCIs/RX cleanly (38 devices, -91 floor) but is NOT a capture upgrade -- the Realtek hears deeper (-102 dBm, external antenna) and its intermittent deafness is auto-recovered by tepna-btreset; three "Realtek deaf" readings that night were a double-scan Busy confound, caught by a clean single scan (a near-miss fabricated disproof). So the Realtek keeps production capture; the Zephyr value is being the only reflashable controller. Task 1 (physical/owner): flash controller-side ACL timestamping + a fixed static address. Task 2 (~1h software): a btmon inter-arrival jitter probe (the spreadMs / connection-interval floor the hostAxis code reasons about), run on the free hci2, non-disruptive. Neither blocks the mutation programme. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS — parked 2026-09-02: Task 1 SDC flash done; controller timestamping is a reflash (owner) and the Task 2 jitter probe needs `btmon` on the box's free adapter, a daytime paired session)* |
| [`PROFILED-HOTSPOTS-CI-AND-DSP-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PROFILED-HOTSPOTS-CI-AND-DSP-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | CPU-profiled ground truth, not op-counts. The CI `test` gate is ECGDex-bound and SINGLE-threaded — `lombScargle` alone is 35% of it. `DEX_GROUP` filters the REPORT, not the RUN, so CI sharding is not a workflow-only change. `PpgDex.compute()` spends 28% in `mean`, because `pearson()` recomputes `mean(tmpl)` once per beat. Plus §0: a synthetic microbenchmark lied by 18× — profile the real pipeline first | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-21 — §2/§1a executed via CI-SHARDING; §3 retracted (vm-realm profile artifact, EFFICIENCY-AUDIT §M1); §4 dismissed; §1b decided: LEAVE `lombScargle` as-is)* |
| [`TRIO-POWER-N15-FINDINGS-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TRIO-POWER-N15-FINDINGS-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | Attempted SENSOR-TRIO-NIGHTS §181 (the N=10→15 power re-run). **The σ re-fit is NOT landed** — the planted σ and the paper's Tables 1–3 are ONE ATOMIC UNIT (those tables *are* the Monte-Carlo at that σ), so changing one without the other silently publishes a simulation of a truth nobody chose. **Blocker 1:** a 50k/100k re-run does not complete — the WebGPU lane accelerates only the N-sweep; the ρ/duration sweeps are CPU-bound (ETA advanced 1 min in 25 min at 100k). The paper is also self-inconsistent: Table 1 says 50,000 trials, the abstract says 720, the tool defaults to 500. **Blocker 2 (FIXED here):** the planted σ is TRIPLICATED (page/CPU/GPU) and was ungated — a stale cache made the tool REPORT σ=2.72/1.86/1.94 while SIMULATING at 2.60/1.58/1.85, and briefly faked a GPU≠CPU divergence (they are bit-identical). Measured-but-unlanded: the 15-night hat is **2.60/1.58/1.85** and moves 2 cells (H10 ±0.25: 1→2 — a *quieter* corner needs MORE windows, because the TCH couples them; Verity ±0.15: 3→2) | Brief *(PROPOSED — ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-04: NOT data-blocked, and never was.** `uploads/trio/` already COMMITS 25 post-host-axis nights against this brief's target of 15, and the fold that day turned out to REPRODUCE that exact set rather than extend it. The 15 further nights on disk are CONFOUNDED (code version and date are the same variable — the tool says *regenerate, do not subset*). Original note: A clean 25-night trio fold landed 2026-08-04 from the 19 GB PSL corpus at `Ecg nightly/`, via `tools/trio-batch.mjs` → `tools/tch-multinight.mjs`: 23 estimated / 25 nights, one producing-code version, median σ ECGDex 0.56 · PpgDex 2.71 · OxyDex 1.11 (classic). ⚠️ NOT comparable to the paper's published 2.41/1.28/1.42 — different estimator (classic/ρ-on vs the fused-weight robust hat) and the ordering differs, which is the question the re-fit exists to answer. Trap recorded: a pre-populated output dir gave a 41-night MIXED-code corpus and the tool caught it itself)* |
| [`WORKER-REALM-GATES-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/WORKER-REALM-GATES-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | Follows PPGDEX-WORKER-CLOSURE. The `cadenceSamples` bug was not bad luck — **nothing in this repo executed a worker**. **The trap:** `detectChannelsAsync` falls back to serial on `w.onerror`, so a throwing worker still returns CORRECT NUMBERS — any check comparing worker output to serial **passes on a completely dead pool**. Demonstrated: against the pre-fix DSP the new rig reds on `NO worker threw` while `worker output ≡ serial` **still passes**. **§1** the PPG blob now EXECUTES in the headless gates (`new Function` reproduces the empty worker realm in both lanes) — necessary because the STATIC check said `CLOSED` on code that still threw `REFR_CADENCE_FRAC is not defined` (a module `const` does not travel with `Function.toString()`). **§2** a REAL-Worker rig in the browser lane (spawned · none errored · ≡ serial). **§3 ⚠️** `ecgdex-app.js`'s `WORKER_SRC` carries a **FOURTH, ungated `parseTimestamp`** (`_ckPF`) that runs ONLY above the 5 MB worker threshold — a drift there would corrupt timestamps **exclusively on large overnight recordings** while every fixture stayed green; measured to agree with `DexClock` on all 12 vendor formats, now pinned. Audit also: 10 surfaces driven in a real browser → **0 uncaught errors**; H2 (OverDex/Data-Unifier missing `PPGMorph`) **resolved as a non-issue**. Gates only — no re-bundle | Brief *(DONE — 2026-07-12)* |
| [`PPGDEX-WORKER-CLOSURE-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PPGDEX-WORKER-CLOSURE-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | `Uncaught ReferenceError: cadenceSamples is not defined` (reported from the live demo). The PPG worker is a `blob:` built from an INLINED source string whose deps array is **hand-maintained**, and the worker realm starts **empty** — the optical-detector fix gave `detectBeats` an adaptive refractory sourced from a new `cadenceSamples()` + `REFR_CADENCE_FRAC`, and **neither was shipped**. **Why no gate saw it:** `w.onerror` falls back to serial, so the NUMBERS stayed right — and the equiv/headless gates run `analyze()`/`compute()`, which are serial BY DESIGN and **never touch the worker**. Symptoms: a console error, and all 3 LED channels detected on the MAIN THREAD (the exact thing the pool exists to prevent). A module `const` **does not travel with `Function.toString()`** — a static call-graph check missed `REFR_CADENCE_FRAC`; only RUNNING the worker realm found it. Verified worker ≡ serial on 3 real captures × 3 channels (9/9, byte-identical). New gate re-derives the closure from the DSP's own source text; reds against `origin/main` naming both symbols | Brief *(DONE — 2026-07-12)* |
| [`PPGDEX-PI-AND-PARSE-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PPGDEX-PI-AND-PARSE-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | Executes `EFFICIENCY-AUDIT-FINDINGS-2026-07-12` §C1+§P1. **A shipped app was showing a wrong number.** `ppgdex-morph.js` stepped its sample loop by `winSamp = winSec × fs`, and `fs` is the median sensor-ns delta — so on a real Polar file it is NEVER integral (176.26 → 10575.6). A fractional `Float32Array` index returns `undefined` → `NaN` → **`pi` silently `null`**: the surfaced per-window perfusion-index trend was plotting **1/2, 1/8, 2/7** windows on real captures (now 2/2, 8/8, 7/7). Rounding ONLY the sample bounds leaves beat→window assignment untouched (proved: `analyze()` hashes identically with `pi` stripped) and is **2.24×** faster. Plus §P1: `parsePPG` ran `parseTimestamp` on all ~190k rows for a `lastTs` only a degenerate fallback reads → **2.08×**, byte-identical. The audit's 3rd finding was **REJECTED** — it would have broken PAT feasibility (`pat-feasibility-worker.js` reads `tMs` on those rows). Pinned by a regression group using a NON-INTEGER `fs` (an integer `fs` passes even against the bug — which is why the suite never caught it) | Brief *(DONE — 2026-07-12)* |
| [`PPGDEX-PI-AND-PARSE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/PPGDEX-PI-AND-PARSE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) **§1 CLOSED 2026-08-01 — NOT ACTIONABLE: there is no consumer that can opt out.** The safe `opts.stamps` shape was implemented, measured and then REVERTED UNSHIPPED. Re-measured on a real 79.7 MB Verity companion (1.17 M rows): **1.78×**, not the proposed 2.08×, and below this section own ≥2× bar. The consumer census had gone stale — 2 named, **9 actual** (gaining resp-acc-analysis-app, motiondex-dsp, trio-batch, polar-sense-ppg) — which is precisely the hazard the section was written about. And **every one reads `tMs`**: decisively `tools/trio-batch.mjs:1299` sets `relNs: NaN` on every row it keeps, so `tMs` is its SOLE time base and opting it out drops every row. Nothing shipped because a `ppgdex-dsp.js` change costs PpgDex + both orchestrators re-bundled and fixture re-verification **for a knob no caller can pass** — and a dormant optimization is a standing invitation to opt a caller out later without redoing the audit, where the failure mode is silence | Residue from executing PPGDEX-PI-AND-PARSE. **§1** the companion-parse 2.08× (`parseSensorXYZ`), deferred not dismissed — needs a back-compat-safe `opts.stamps` shape because `pat-feasibility-worker.js:117` reads `r.tMs`. **§2** drop `ppgdex-morph.js` from biome's formatter-exempt list once it is genuinely reflowed. **§3** sweep the fleet for other float-stepped sample loops used as array indices — the failure mode is SILENT (`undefined`→`NaN`→`null`, no throw); candidate house-invariant lint. **§4** the synthetic fixtures all used an integer `fs`, so they could only prove the code works on inputs that never occur — audit the other nodes' generators for the same tidiness | Brief *(DONE — 2026-08-14; §3/§4 EXECUTED 2026-07-14, §1 CLOSED 2026-08-01 as NOT ACTIONABLE, and **§2's trigger fired 2026-07-15 — `02d335d5` reflowed the whole tree and retired the formatter-override list, so there is no list left to drop `ppgdex-morph.js` from.** The condition was met the day after the brief was deferred for lack of it; only the stamp was outstanding — see §2a)* |
| [`GATE-INTEGRITY-AND-DEVLOOP-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/GATE-INTEGRITY-AND-DEVLOOP-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | Executes `EFFICIENCY-AUDIT-FINDINGS-2026-07-12` §G1+§D1+§D2. **§G1** a ⊘ SKIP is neither pass nor fail, so the gate could shrink in SILENCE — the raw corpus is gitignored, so on a fresh clone **all 8 real-recording equivalence legs (the whole GATE-C surface) have never run on the merge path** (CI 2093/11-skipped, GATE B 10 of 23; local 2113/2, 23/23) and nothing pinned the skip count. Every skip must now be DECLARED in `tests/expected-skips.json` — an undeclared skip FAILS, fail-closed — plus a loud COVERAGE report and `DEX_UPLOADS=<path>` (which also makes CI's coverage reproducible locally). **§D1** `--jobs=N` forks the CI partition locally: **100.7 s → 24.9 s**, identical verdict (`npm run test:par`; `npm run check` uses it). **§D2** the two committed ledgers were a conflict ENGINE (45% of commits, 10 merge-resolutions in 48 h) because scalar `count`/`generated` changed on BOTH sides of any disjoint add — and a conflicted PR gets **NO CI AT ALL** (GitHub builds `pull_request` against the merge commit; it hit PR #43 AND #46 in one session). Now line-oriented text + `merge=union`, single-sourced in `tests/list-format.js`; proved in a scratch repo to auto-merge where the JSON conflicted, with the `list == fs` staleness assertion as the backstop | Brief *(DONE — 2026-07-12)* |
| [`TRIO-BATCH-O2RING-DAT-2026-07-13-BRIEF.md`](briefs/TRIO-BATCH-O2RING-DAT-2026-07-13-BRIEF.md) | **Six analyzable nights were invisible because the oximeter's CSV export stopped.** `trio-batch` discovered the oxy corner with `.csv` only — and *anchors the whole night on it*, so a night with a complete ECG+PPG pair was dropped for want of an anchor. The O2Ring writes a **native `.dat` beside the vendor CSV**, and when the export stops (app not opened, phone not synced) the `.dat` is all that survives: **20 CSV-less `.dat`s** sat in the capture folder, 6 of them (07-07 … 07-12) with full concurrent Polar streams. **OxyDex has read this format all along** (`isO2RingBin` / `decodeO2RingBinToCSV`, live on the browser drop path) — only the *headless* corner was blind, because `compute()` takes `{samples\|rows\|text}` and never bytes. The fix **exposes the existing decoder rather than copying it** (a second implementation of the 3-byte layout / `0xFF 0xFF` trailer / `motion×2` scale would drift), prefers the vendor CSV when both files exist, and ranks the anchor by **duration, not bytes** — bytes stopped being comparable the moment a `.dat` (~10× denser) could compete with a CSV. Equivalence is *proved*, not asserted: 2026-07-06 has both files, and same-code CSV-path ≡ `.dat`-path with **zero diffs** across every metric, event and timeseries value. Failure was **silent by construction** — one `⊘` among hundreds of `·` lines, exit 0; the silent-drop ergonomics are deliberately left to their own brief | Brief *(DONE — 2026-07-13)* |
| [`CI-SHARDING-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CI-SHARDING-2026-07-12-BRIEF.md) | Executes PROFILED-HOTSPOTS §2→§1a. The 4-minute `test` gate was ONE single-threaded step at 104% CPU on a multi-core runner; now 4 parallel shards — **4m05s → ~1m10s** (101.3 s → 25.3 s makespan, 4.00x). `group()` now skips *execution*, not just the report (so `--group` finally means what it claimed). The shards **partition** the suite — index-based, never name-based, because a pattern shard silently drops any group no pattern claims and every shard still goes green. `verify-shard-union.mjs` proves the partition on every push (0.08 s) and `--deep` proves the shard-union is assertion-for-assertion identical to the unsharded run. `genSynthetic` memoisation deliberately NOT taken: those groups regenerate on purpose to assert byte-reproducibility, so a cache would speed the gate up by weakening it | Brief *(DONE — 2026-07-12)* |

## Maintaining this index

When you add a root-level `*.md`, add one row here in the matching group with its real H1 and a
role tag. When a brief is fully executed and superseded, move it to `docs-archive/` and drop its row
(or relabel it *Historical*). This file is the one place a newcomer should land before opening any
single brief.

Every relative link in this file is gate-checked (`docs-ledger` check 4 — CLAUDE.md §📌): a `briefs/` link resolves against the real brief set (4a), and every other `docs/·audits/·wiring/·root` link resolves
against a whole-tree path inventory (4b) the Node lane recomputes from disk on each run. If you move or
rename a linked file, just fix the link — there is no committed inventory to regenerate (the gate reads the
tree straight from the filesystem; CPAP-REAL-CORPUS-FOLLOWUPS-II §4).
